# civ64 — design (phases 1 and 2)

This is the design record of the x64 port of `CIV.EXE` that `claude/docs/x64-port-prompt.md`
specifies (the "prompt" below; section numbers like 5.2 refer to it). It records every design
decision, the platform facts that are not in the binary (each marked **platform fact
(inferred)** with its source), the inventories of phase 0 and the items that later phases
decide (marked *open (phase N)*).

Evidence labels used here and in all port documents: **listing** (read from
`claude/src/asm`/`claude/src/data`), **recovered C** (`claude/src/c`), **docs** (`claude/docs`),
**model (self-consistency)**, **original-process observation quoted in claude/docs**, **original
under OTVDM on Windows 11**, **platform fact (inferred)**, **choice** (a free design decision).

## 0. Inventories (phase 0)

Generated by `gen/inventory.py` from the recovered C, the listings and `claude/docs/db`
(addresses, names and mnemonics only; no original bytes). Machine-readable copy:
`build/cache/inventory.json`.

| Inventory | File | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| Liveness | [inventory/liveness.md](inventory/liveness.md) | 1065 functions: 854 live, 178 unreferenced + 33 transitively dead (findings D1, recomputed and equal) |
| APIs and call sites | [inventory/apis.md](inventory/apis.md) | 156 imports, 141 with live call sites. Dead-only: CreateDialogIndirect, mciSendCommand, SetPaletteEntries, GetSystemPaletteEntries, UnrealizeObject (as the prompt lists) **and also** CreateFont, FindResource, LoadResource, FreeResource, GlobalReAlloc (their only callers are D1 functions). C0W-only (replaced): InitTask, InitApp, WaitEvent, LoadLibrary, `__fpMath`. The disposition of every import: [API-MAP.md](API-MAP.md) |
| Run-time calls | [inventory/runtime-calls.md](inventory/runtime-calls.md) | 48 distinct segment-1000 callees of game code; 43 from live code (lseek, ltoa, printf, puts, tell only from dead code) |
| Pseudo-intrinsics | [inventory/pseudo-intrinsics.md](inventory/pseudo-intrinsics.md) | uses per run-time routine; all replaced by `rt`/`w16` (5.4) |
| Pointer/handle data | [inventory/pointer-handle.md](inventory/pointer-handle.md) | 143 globals, 64 struct members |
| Out-of-range sites | [inventory/out-of-range.md](inventory/out-of-range.md) | findings U, C, G (index outside an object) and A addresses: the allowlist seed |
| Stack-resident locals | [inventory/stack-locals.md](inventory/stack-locals.md) | candidates by rule 5.2 item 2 (a), (b), (d); register variables |
| Uninitialised values | [inventory/uninit.md](inventory/uninit.md) | 33 rows of findings 8.2 |
| Evaluation order | [inventory/eval-order.md](inventory/eval-order.md) | 1,219 full expressions (544 with several calls, 447 call + order-sensitive read, 147 `a[i] = f()`, ...) |
| Divisions and shifts | [inventory/div-shift.md](inventory/div-shift.md) | 444 16-bit and 29 32-bit `idiv`, 2 `div`; 311+25+34 shifts by CL; F_LXLSH and LXMUL@ calls |
| Floating point | [inventory/float.md](inventory/float.md) | x87 only in 1000, 1068 (ShowPediaEntry) and 1190 (stdio fallback) |
| String literals | inventory/strings.md | 1,001 literals: 419 carry an address comment, 426 match one strings.json entry, 88 ambiguous, 68 unmatched (resolved from the listing operand at translation) |

Front-end facts established while generating them (**model (self-consistency)** of the
recovered sources): all 89 C files and the headers parse with the port's own front end
(`gen/cfront`); `civ_structs.h` has 78 structures, and all 496 documented member offsets and
61 documented sizes agree with the computed byte-packed 16-bit layouts; the 806 globals of
`civ_globals.h` agree with `names.json` (address; type size within the names.json extent).
The game segments have 87 `cs:` operands (**listing** count; corrected after review finding
m1, which found the earlier "53, all jumps" wrong): 63 `jmp word ptr cs:[bx+table]` switch
dispatches and 24 `mov ax, word ptr cs:[bx]` value scans of sparse switches (for example
1048:01EA, which searches the 68 command ids at 1048:03D8; 1030:9C38, 1058:06C9,
11F0:0D0C/1C18/39F7, 1220:12A7). Both read constant tables in their own code segment.
**Rule:** the translator turns every such dispatch or scan into a C `switch`, so no core code
reads code-segment bytes (code selectors have no image, 3.1). Segment 1000's CS-relative reads
belong to `rt`.

Front-end type rules that the listing decided (phase 1): a pointer to a `near` function
without its own distance modifier is a 16-bit near code pointer (`_scantod`'s
`int near (*Get)(...)` arguments take 2 bytes, 1000:03BC); Borland's segment-based pointer
modifiers `_ss`/`_cs`/`_ds`/`_es` give 16-bit pointers (`__vprinter`'s `char _ss *args`, 1000:19D8).
The shim header `win16.h` defines them as empty for its syntax check, so the port's
preprocessor keeps them (as it keeps FAR/NEAR/PASCAL). With these rules every one of the 1,896
parameter locations that names.json records agrees with the prototype layout (`gen/frames.py`;
1,890 before the jump stubs `write` and `_readEntry` became checkable, 3.4).

## 1. Language and build

* **Standard.** `core`, `rt` and `platform/w16` are written in the common subset of C99 and
  C11/C17 with `<stdint.h>`, `<stddef.h>`, `<string.h>`, `<limits.h>` (`<math.h>` in `rt` only).
  Strict C89 is **not** the target, because the fixed-width types of `<stdint.h>` are required.
  No VLAs, `_Static_assert`, `_Alignas`, `_Generic`, anonymous structs/unions or extensions;
  static layout checks use `typedef char name[(cond) ? 1 : -1];` (`core/civ_headers.c`).
  `static inline` functions (C99) replace every macro that would evaluate an argument twice.
* **Checks** (`build.cmd`): MSVC `/std:c11` and `/std:c17` with `/W4 /WX /permissive-
  /fp:precise`, and the C99 parse with the clang front end shipped with Visual Studio (`clang-tidy
  ... -- -std=c99 -pedantic -Wall -Wextra`), preceded by the `--list-checks` guard. Every `.c`
  file of `platform/w16`, `rt`, `core` and `core/gen` goes through all three; generated headers
  are covered through the generated `.c` files and `core/civ_headers.c`, which includes every
  generated header and expands every accessor form once (`civ_headers_probe`: G_RD/G_WR, G_IX,
  G_RDX/G_WRX, G_RDX2/G_WRX2, GF_RD/GF_WR, GF_RD2/GF_WR2, GF_RDX/GF_WRX, F_RD/F_WR, F_AX,
  F_RDX/F_WRX, F_RDO/F_WRO and the F32/F64/F80 tags; the earlier claim lacked the two-index and
  floating forms, review finding m5). The test programs (`tests/test_*.c`) are compiled with
  `/std:c17` and the same warning flags and linked with the headless back end.
* **Variants.** Release, and Checked (`build.cmd checked`: `CIV_CHECKED=1`, the image-bounds
  build of 5.2 item 3 with the out-of-object recorder and the API argument checks).
* **Disabled warnings** (command line only, each justified here): `/wd4702` (unreachable code,
  kept by rules 6.7/6.8). Any further `/wd` needs an entry here.
* `_CRT_SECURE_NO_WARNINGS` on the command line. Release links `/MT`. Never `/fp:fast`.
* Only `platform/win32` and `win64` include `<windows.h>`.

## 2. Layers and files

```
core/            translated game functions (one .c per recovered C file; phase 3)
  acc.h          typed accessors on original offsets (hand-written, 3.2)
  civ_headers.c  header/accessor compile coverage and static layout checks
  gen/           generated: civ_layout.h, civ_frames.h/.c, civ_dispatch.h/.c, civ_adapters.h,
                 civ_core.h, civ_api.h/.c
rt/              Borland run-time subset, state in the DGROUP image; rt_api.h, rt_unimpl.c generated
platform/w16/    Win16 emulation: vm (memory/selectors/stack/frames), ne (loader), host.h (the
                 back-end interface), w16_api.h and w16_unimpl.c (generated), USER/GDI/KERNEL/
                 COMMDLG/MMSYSTEM (phases 2 and 4)
platform/win32/  host back end (presentation, input, wall clock, sound, files; phase 5)
tests/headless/  headless back end and harness
tests/           unit and differential tests (no original bytes)
gen/             Python generators: cfront (front end), inventory, layout (+ accessor tags), frames,
                 dispatch, interfaces, listing; translator (phase 3)
```

`core`, `rt` and `w16` are byte-identical in both builds; only the back end differs (5.0). The
generated `*_unimpl.c` files hold fault stubs for every interface function that is not
implemented yet, so the build links at every stage; a stub disappears when the implementation
is defined (the generators look for the definition).

Generator order (each reads the caches of the previous ones): `inventory.py` → `layout.py` →
`frames.py` → `dispatch.py` → `interfaces.py`. Every generator exits non-zero on an
unexplained cross-check finding.

## 3. Memory model (5.2)

### 3.1 Images, selectors and limits

* **Selectors.** Code segment *n* is `0x1000 + 8(n-1)` (`0x1000..0x1220`); data segments
  `0x1228..0x1418`; DGROUP `0x1420` (also SS). Dynamic global blocks take selectors
  `0x1428..0x7FF0` in steps of 8 (3,450 values). The platform scratch selector `0x7FF8` (3.7) is
  never handed out, and a selector is taken only from the FREE or FREED state (review finding
  m2). A block of *k* 64 KB tiles takes `sel, sel+8, ..., sel+8(k-1)` (`__AHINCR` = 8,
  `__AHSHIFT` = 3).
* **Selector reuse** (review finding B2; **deviation from the prompt, confirmed as implemented
  by the user, 2026-10-11 about 08:00 UTC**). Prompt 5.2 item 5 says freed selectors are quarantined and never reused. This
  program cannot run under that rule. On the required 256-colour path every sprite draw
  allocates and frees a global block (DrawTileImpl 11A8:0F56 calls NewPtr at 11A8:1287, whose
  GlobalAlloc is at 1100:05E6, and DisposPtr, i.e. GlobalFree, at 11A8:13A1), so the 3,450 dynamic selectors would be
  used up after about 3,450 sprite draws. Windows 3.1 reused selectors, so the original never
  reaches that state. Policy (**choice**, deterministic, `vm_block_alloc`):
  * a new block takes never-used selectors, ascending, while *k* of them remain;
  * after that it takes the run of *k* consecutive FREED selectors whose most recent release is
    the oldest (ties: the lowest selector), and fails if there is none;
  * a FREED selector stays invalid until it is reused: every access faults (`VM_SEG_FREED`), so
    a stale far pointer faults for as long as possible (in steady state about 3,400 later
    allocations);
  * host memory is released at GlobalFree.
  Fidelity entry: "selector values and reuse order differ from Windows 3.1; a stale far pointer
  faults until its selector is reused". `test_vm` covers 10,000 allocate/free cycles, the reuse
  order, stale-pointer and double-free faults, the 1,500,000-byte block, exhaustion of the
  never-used selectors (the last one is 0x7FF0) and the release of host memory.
* **Granularity.** **Platform fact (inferred):** Windows 3.1 rounds global-heap blocks to
  32-byte units and sets the selector limit to the rounded size − 1 (general Win16 knowledge of
  the KERNEL global arena). No Windows 3.1 observation exists in this project; an OTVDM
  observation would show OTVDM's behaviour, not Windows 3.1's (review finding M1). The rule has
  these consequences (decision (a) of M1):
  * **C1** (5.3): segment 12D8 has 0x4610 bytes, so its limit is 0x461F. The writes to
    12D8:4610..4615 (InitGameColorTables, store at 10E8:2E72) stay inside it, as they must
    (`test_ne` checks the limit and that 12D8:4620 faults; `civ_headers.c` checks it statically).
  * **C2** (`g_treasury[g_aliveCivsMask]`, word read at 12D8:4444 + 2·mask, 1030:298E). Bit 0
    (barbarians) is never cleared, so every odd mask can occur. The prompt's "only 0xE5 and
    0xE7 occur in play" restricts only the masks 0xE5..0xE8 that address the C1 bytes (the
    earlier text misread it; M1). The initial mask is `0xFF >> (6 − numAi)`, 0xFF with six AI
    opponents (findings C2). Odd masks up to 0xED read inside the limit (0xED: 12D8:461E..461F).
    Odd masks 0xEF..0xFF read beyond it (0xEF: 12D8:4622) and go to the fault policy (3.3): the
    port stops with a "general protection fault" dialog naming the active statement and the
    data address (the fault run p49-c2-fault: 1030:28A7, 12D8:4642). These masks are
    reachable in late games with most civilizations alive, when the rule runs (the human's
    spaceship has started and the earlier triggers failed, 1030:296B..2987). Fidelity entry "C2
    masks 0xEF..0xFF fault"; the prompt-7 "C1/C2 bytes" test (phase 3); the masks are listed in
    [inventory/out-of-range.md](inventory/out-of-range.md). Option (b), a sourced Windows 3.1
    rule that admits 12D8:4643, stays open should such a source appear.
  * **Huge blocks** (review note N3, *open*): Windows 3.1 enhanced mode may give the non-last
    tiles of a huge block limits that run to the end of the block, so that a word access at
    offset 0xFFFF of a non-last tile would not fault. The port gives every non-last tile the
    limit 0xFFFF, so such an access faults (3.3). A KERNEL source is needed to decide.
* **Images.** Each data segment is a host byte array of `limit + 1` bytes, initialised from the
  file image; slack bytes are zero and are part of snapshots. Code segments have no image
  (reading data through a code selector faults).
* **DGROUP.** `0x0000..0xB4BD` file image (BSS 0x5146..0xB4BD zeroed by the replaced C0W
  start-up, 1000:0020..002D), then the 9,216-byte stack `0xB4BE..0xD8BD`, then the 3,072-byte
  local heap `0xD8BE..0xE4BD`: **stack, then heap** (the order `data-structures.md` infers). The
  image is 0xE4BE bytes rounded to 0xE4C0 (limit 0xE4BF). Initial emulated SP = 0xD8BE. The stack
  area starts zero-filled (**choice**; the KERNEL's own fill is unknown).
* **Instance header** 1420:0000..000F (review finding m9). **Platform fact (inferred):**
  KERNEL's InitTask writes the task's stack bounds into this reserved header
  (`claude/docs/data-structures.md` 2.2), whose Win16 layout is: a zero word, the saved SS:SP,
  pLocalHeap, pAtomTable, pStackTop, pStackMin, pStackBottom (general Win16 knowledge of the
  instance data). rt's start-up writes zeros, then pStackTop = 0xB4BE (lowest stack address),
  pStackMin = pStackBottom = 0xD8BE (the initial SP). pLocalHeap stays 0 until phase 4 decides
  LocalInit. No game or run-time code reads the header (`dgroup_refs.json` lists only
  1138:049F, which is the constant `push 2`, not a reference); the header is in the comparison
  mask (9).
* **PSP and environment** (m9; **choice**). The start-up allocates the 256-byte PSP and then the
  environment block as the first two dynamic blocks (selectors 0x1428 and 0x1430), before any
  game allocation; Windows allocated both in KERNEL before the task ran, with unknown selectors
  (masked). The PSP holds the environment selector at 0x2C and the command tail at 0x80/0x81.
  `_psp` (1420:0016) receives the PSP selector and `_pszCmdline` (1420:001C) the offset 0x81
  (InitTask's ES:BX).
* **GlobalAlloc limits** (m9). **Platform fact (inferred):** in 386 enhanced mode a single
  Windows 3.1 global block can be at most 16 MB − 64 KB (Windows 3.1 SDK description of
  GlobalAlloc; general knowledge, not verified in this project). The port's GlobalAlloc fails
  above 0xFF0000 bytes and when no run of the needed tile count is free. So OpenResFile's
  1,500,000-byte request (23 tiles) succeeds and the in-memory `.rsc` path runs (prompt 5.5).
  CheckMapGrowLimit's probe (1208:0E96, at most 4,096,000 bytes, 63 tiles) succeeds at its first
  request while such a run is free; WdwMapOpen discards the probe's result anyway (u096).
  GlobalSize returns the 32-byte-rounded size (whole tiles for all but the last tile).
* **Lock counts (Q16, decided in phase 2; choice, the Windows 3.1 behaviour is not known
  here).** `platform/w16/kernel_mem.c`: lock counts are 16-bit and saturate at 0xFFFF;
  GlobalUnlock of an unlocked block returns 0 and changes nothing; GlobalFree frees a block
  whatever its lock count (and logs it), so the leaks L1/L2/L14 never keep a block alive or make
  a later GlobalFree fail. Invalid handles: GlobalLock returns NULL (also for 0), GlobalUnlock 0,
  GlobalFree the handle, GlobalSize 0, GlobalHandle 0. Padding: blocks are zero-filled whatever
  the flags. `test_heap` covers these rules.
* **ES after GlobalFree (Q14; choice).** The w16 API glue leaves ES unchanged, so after
  `__GlobalUnlockFreeES` ES still holds the freed selector. This decides the DGROUP word that
  the recovered run-time defect K1 (`__SubHeapFree` 1000:4922) decrements: DGROUP:(selector+4),
  where the selector value is the port's (3.1). `rt_heap.c` reproduces K1 on the emulated stack
  and logs it; the program's heap use is inferred to need a single sub-heap segment, so K1
  should not run (findings K1).
* **Data relocations.** The loader applies the 63 internal far-pointer relocations of DGROUP
  (52 into DGROUP, 7 into 1000, 4 into 10C0); their values are virtual far pointers (data) or
  original code addresses (code, resolved through dispatch). `test_ne` checks all 63.
* **GlobalReAlloc** keeps the selector while the tile count does not grow; when it grows, a new
  run is allocated by the policy above, the bytes are copied, the old run is released and the
  handle changes (**choice**; GlobalReAlloc is reached only from dead code, so no game path
  depends on it).

### 3.2 Virtual far pointers and accessors

* A data pointer in `core` is a `uint32_t` virtual far pointer `(sel << 16) | off` (`vptr_t`).
  Host pointers exist only inside `platform/w16/vm.c` and the back ends.
* All image state goes through `mem_rd8/16/32` and `mem_wr8/16/32` (little-endian) on virtual
  far pointers: images, the emulated stack, global blocks, resources and platform scratch.
* **Generated layout** (`core/gen/civ_layout.h`, `gen/layout.py`): `VA_<g>` (virtual address of
  each global), `SZ_<g>` (bytes), `EXT_<g>` (names.json extent when larger), `SZ_<S>` and
  `OF_<S>_<m>` (structure sizes and member offsets), and the **accessor tags**: `GT_<g>` /
  `FT_<S>_<m>` give the type of a global or member (`ACC_T_I8 ACC_T_U8 ACC_T_I16 ACC_T_U16
  ACC_T_I32 ACC_T_U32` integers, `ACC_T_FP` far pointer, `ACC_T_NP` near pointer, `ACC_T_F32
  ACC_T_F64 ACC_T_F80` floating, `ACC_T_S` structure, `ACC_T_A` array). The tags are prefixed so
  that no core macro can capture them (review finding m5). Arrays have `GE_`/`FE_` (element
  tag), `GS_`, `GS2_`, `GS3_` / `FS_` (stride per index) and `GN_`, `GN2_`, `GN3_` / `FN_`
  (element count per index, review finding M3; `GS3_`/`GN3_` for the two three-index globals);
  an incomplete array (`[]`) takes its count from the names.json extent and the stride. The
  generator never emits data bytes.
* **Accessors** (`core/acc.h`): `G_RD(g)`, `G_WR(g, v)` (scalar global); `G_RDX(g, i)`,
  `G_WRX(g, i, v)`, `G_RDX2(g, i, j)`, `G_WRX2(g, i, j, v)` (array elements); `GF_RD(g, i, S, m)`,
  `GF_WR(g, i, S, m, v)`, `GF_RD2`/`GF_WR2` (member of a structure element of a one- or
  two-index global array), `GF_RDX`/`GF_WRX` (element of an array member of such an element);
  `F_RD(S, m, p)`, `F_WR(S, m, p, v)`, `F_RDX`/`F_WRX`/`F_AX` (member, array-member element and
  its address through a far pointer), `F_RDO`/`F_WRO` (member through a pointer that the
  translator relates to a global object); `G_IX` and `va_ix`/`va_ix2` (addresses). Each argument
  appears once in the expansion and is evaluated once. Signed loads go through `vm_s8/16/32`
  (defined conversions, no implementation-defined casts); stores convert to the unsigned width
  (defined modulo arithmetic). Floating-point tags read and write IEEE bit patterns through
  `rt_float.h` (section 4); there is deliberately no `float` store form (no game code stores a
  binary32 value).
* **Evaluation order of stores** (m5). In every store form the address arguments and the value
  are arguments of one function call, whose evaluation order C leaves unspecified. The translator
  therefore linearizes (rule 6.4) every store whose index and value expressions contain side
  effects or calls: they go into temporaries first, in listing order.
* **Index arithmetic.** `va_ix(base, i, stride, disp)` = `sel:((off + i*stride + disp) mod
  0x10000)` computed in `uint32_t`, exactly the listing's 16-bit effective address. Indexes are
  `uint32_t` parameters, so an `int16_t` index converts modulo 2^32 (defined; m5) and a negative
  index wraps the same way (`g_cities[-1]` is 1420:54E0, checked statically in `civ_headers.c`
  and in `test_acc`). The selector never changes: an access leaving the selector's limit faults
  (3.3), never another segment.
* A multi-byte access must lie within the selector limit as a whole; an access with
  `off + size - 1 > limit` faults (386 protected-mode behaviour).
* **Pointer arithmetic.** far `+ - ++ --` change only the offset (mod 0x10000, `fp_add`); `==`/`!=`
  compare 32 bits; relational operators compare the unsigned offsets; huge pointers carry into
  the selector by 8 per 64 KB and compare/subtract as 5.2 item 4 states (`hp_add`, `hp_sub`,
  `hp_cmp`). `FP_SEG`/`FP_OFF`/`MK_FP` are `VM_SEL`/`VM_OFF`/`VM_PTR`.
* **Checked build** (`CIV_CHECKED`; review findings M3, m4, m5). Every indexed access checks
  each index against its own dimension: the first, second and third index of a global array
  (`VM_OOB_INDEX1`, `VM_OOB_INDEX2`, `VM_OOB_INDEX3`) and the index of an array member
  (`VM_OOB_MEMBER`). It also checks the whole access against the object's extent
  (`VM_OOB_EXTENT`). The two three-index globals, `g_spaceshipGrid` and `g_palacePieceYOffsets`,
  use `acc_ix3` (18 accesses in 6 units; added 2026-10-09 after round 8, before which they were
  `va_ix(acc_ix2(...), k)`, with the third index and the extent unchecked: verify/round8-R8D.md
  G-1); the address and the evaluation order of the indexes are unchanged. An address-only form
  (`G_IX`) admits one past the end (i ≤ n), the legal C loop bound. Pointer accesses check
  member-array indexes (`F_AX`, recorded under the member offset because the object is unknown)
  and, in the `F_RDO`/`F_WRO` forms, the address against the extent of the object the translator
  relates the pointer to. The generated code indexes a member array of a global array element
  with `acc_ix1m` (`g[i].m[k]`: i, k and the extent) and every other member array with `acc_pm`
  (`p->m[k]`, `x.m[k]`, and a row of a two-dimensional member array, keyed 0): 40 and 52
  accesses in 22 units, added 2026-10-09 after round 9, before which they were `va_ix` forms
  with the member index unchecked (verify/round9-R9A.md G-2); the addresses and the order of
  evaluation are unchanged. Both admit index n for width 0, which the translator passes for an element that is a
  structure or an array (a row), so index n of `p->m[n].f` or of a row index is not recorded
  (round10-R10A.md N3); an address of a scalar element gets the element's width, so a one-past
  address `&p->m[n]` would be recorded, not admitted (whether generated code forms one was not
  checked; round11-R11A.md N1); `acc_pm` records are keyed by the member offset, so rows (key 0)
  and members at the same offset share one record (N1). Not checked: indexing through a pointer
  that is not a member array (`va_ix(RD_ACC_T_FP(...), k)`), including the recovered C's casts
  `((int far *)&g_terrainImprovementRules[i])[k]` (1078, two sites),
  `(&g_terrainTypes[i].food)[yieldType]` (1030, two sites) and the `reserved00` cast at
  10D0:0067; a member array without a count; the last index of a member array of three or more
  dimensions (none exists); `g.m[k]` of a global that is not an array (none exists); and the
  raw `mem_rd` reads of annotated body-push values (round10-R10A.md N4). A violation is recorded once per (statement site, object, kind), with
  the first address, the index and a count (`acc_chk`, `acc_chk_index`, `vm_oob_hits`); the
  access itself proceeds unchanged. The site is the last `/* SSSS:OOOO */` statement marker
  executed (`VM_SITE(addr)`, emitted by the translator before each marked statement).
  `fr_enter` saves it and `fr_leave` restores it, so a hit after a call is charged to the
  caller's statement, not to the callee's last one (m4). Examples now recorded that the
  whole-object check missed: U3 `g_units[civ][-1]` (1188:013F, INDEX2) and
  `g_units[civ][128]` (11F0:08DA). Array locals (frame objects, stack-resident under 5.2 item
  2(a)) and the array members of structure locals check their index the same way (`acc_fx1`,
  `VM_OOB_INDEX1` / `VM_OOB_MEMBER`; added 2026-10-09 after round 7, before which indexes into
  frame objects were not checked: 439 sites in 80 objects). Their address changes with BP, so the
  record's object field is the key `FR_KEY(displacement)` = 0xFFFF0000 | the 16-bit bp
  displacement (of the array, or of the structure local plus the member offset). The element
  stride covers each access, so the index test is the extent test. Copies through a pointer into
  a frame object (strcpy, memcpy; "Copies that overrun a frame object" in 3.4) and pointer
  arithmetic on one (`fp_add(FR_PTR(bp, -518), t_6)`, SubstituteTextTokens 11D0:02B4) are not
  object-checked; the test covers the accesses made through the indexed lvalue. An access of
  width 0 (the address of a structure element, `&pts[2]`) admits index n, as the global forms
  do (round8-R8A.md N4). Only the nodes a form evaluates through `lval`/`rv` emit their annotated
  value pushes. The frame branches of `lval_index`/`lval_member` (the array node, and in
  `lval_member` the `local[i]` node), the member-array forms `p->m[k]`, `g[i].m[k]` and `x.m[k]`
  (the member node; round10-R10A.md N2) and the global-array branches (the identifier `g`, `g[i]`
  of `g[i][j]`, `g[i].f` and `g[i].m[k]`, `g[i]` and `g[i][j]` of `g[i][j][k]`, `g` of `g.f`,
  `table` of `table.b[i]`; round12-R12A.md N4, phase4-review-1.md F13-N4) build their addresses
  without that, so an annotated body push attached to such a node would not be written; none
  exists, and such a push makes generation fail ("annotated body push ... not emitted";
  round8-R8A.md N2). `test_acc` covers U3, a first-index overflow, one past the
  end (not recorded), member and pointer-member indexes, `F_RDO`, the site restore, a
  three-index global (`g_spaceshipGrid`'s last byte; the third index alone, and with the extent)
  and frame
  objects (ChooseCityProduction's `items[0x1030]` at BP D58A is 1420:F504; one record for two
  BPs; index n recorded with a width, n + 1 with width 0; a member array at an offset keeps its
  own key; `local[i].member`). The harness
  writes the records into every snapshot and its index (`oob records N`). The allowlist is keyed
  like the records, by statement marker: `gen/oob_allowlist.py` (run by `build.cmd`) lists the
  markers of the statements that carry an `/* OOB: findings Xn */` comment in core, and every
  marker of a function whose OOB comment stands after its header line (a cited function or a
  site before its first marker); the comments come from the findings U, C and G sites and the
  verifier-located sites (`gen/findings_sites.py`, `gen/annot.py`), so they are the seed of
  [inventory/out-of-range.md](inventory/out-of-range.md) restricted to translated code.
  `civ_harness --oob-allowlist build\cache\oob-allowlist.txt` prints every record whose site is
  not listed (`HARNESS_OOB_UNLISTED`) and fails the run, as does a record that did not fit;
  `run-startup.cmd` passes it (g-24: 0 records, allowlist of 44 entries). `test.cmd` checks both
  outcomes with the stub core's `oob` mode (one record at 1008:F0E0): with an empty allowlist the
  run fails and names the site, with `tests/harness/oob-stub.txt` it passes. Folded displacements (findings A) carry
  no comments: a hit there is unlisted until triaged. No marker is emitted before a re-evaluated loop
  condition or a `for` step, so a hit there is charged to the last marker executed before it: the
  marker before the loop on the first evaluation of a `while`/`for` condition, then whichever
  marker the body (or the step) executed last, which can vary with the path through the body;
  a `do`-`while` condition always follows its body. An allowlisted site in a loop condition is
  therefore listed at every such marker (H29, TruncateStringToWidth 1098:0087: markers 0073 and
  006F; verify/seg019_1090_r.md round 8 R8-N1, round 9). The sites listed so far are covered;
  unchecked reads (C16 RLLEncode: the loop-condition reads at 069C and 0781, evaluated first
  under markers 069C/076C and then under the body markers 068B/075B; verify/seg019_1090_r.md
  round 10 R10-N1) record nothing whatever their marker. Each unlisted hit is triaged as 5.2 item 3
  says (RECOVERY-ERRATA or FIDELITY + allowlist + comment). All conditionals use
  `#if CIV_CHECKED` (m5).

### 3.3 Faults (5.3)

One policy for: an access beyond a selector limit, selector 0, a freed or never-allocated
selector, a code selector used as data, a divide error and a call through a non-code pointer
(`vm_fault`, `VmFaultKind`). Default: stop with a modal "general protection fault" / "divide
error" message naming the original address (the active statement marker) and exit code 3; never
touch host memory. The headless back end records the fault and returns control to the test
(`hh_fault_jmp`). Sites with a proven original effect reproduce it instead (fidelity register).
C1 is not a fault (3.1). Calls of interface functions that are not implemented yet fault with
`VM_FAULT_INTERNAL` ("not implemented yet" / "not ported yet").

`vm_fault` never returns (review finding m6): it calls `host_fault`, which ends the run (Win32:
dialog and exit code 3) or, in the headless back end, longjmps back into the test. Code after a
`vm_fault` call is defensive only: `mem_host` faults instead of handing out its 64 KB dummy
buffer for more than 0x10000 bytes, and `disp_call_*` return 0 instead of calling through a null
table entry.

### 3.4 Emulated registers, calls and stack frames (5.2 items 2 and 7)

* **CPU state** (host-side, not in an image): `cpu.sp`, `cpu.bp`, `cpu.si`, `cpu.di`,
  `cpu.ds` (always 0x1420 in game code) and `cpu.ax`, `cpu.bx`, `cpu.cx`, `cpu.dx`, which are kept
  only where a caller reads a register the callee left (rule 6.5, rule 6.6 class (c)): every API
  glue function and every rt function leaves its result in AX (and DX for 32-bit results), and the
  translator writes AX/DX at the return paths of the functions whose callers read them.
* **Call model.** Every ported function (core and rt) is an ordinary C function with one hidden
  first parameter `uint32_t ra`, the original return address of the call site, followed by the
  original parameters in port types (`gen/porttypes.py`: `int`→`int16_t`, `unsigned`→`uint16_t`,
  `long`→`int32_t`, `unsigned long`→`uint32_t`, `char`→`int8_t`, far/huge pointer→`vptr_t`, near
  pointer→`uint16_t`, `float`/`double`→`double`; a structure passed by value is not a C
  parameter, see "Structures passed by value" below; `long double` only in rt prototypes,
  section 4). The prototypes of all 699 live game functions are generated (`core/gen/civ_core.h`).
* **The caller pushes the argument words.** At every call site the translator emits, in listing
  order, the evaluation and push of each argument exactly as the listing pushes it
  (`stk_push16`/`stk_push32`; a structure passed by value through `rt_F_SPUSH`, which writes
  F_SPUSH's transient return-address and DI/DS words below the copied block as 1000:14F8..1518
  does; the extra words of the adapter calls; the variable arguments of `sprintf`). Nested calls
  inside argument lists therefore run with the already-pushed words in place, exactly where the
  original had them, without any special bracketing. When the listing pushes the segment word of
  a far-pointer argument before a nested call inside that argument (CompleteResearch
  1170:2352/2899 and ShowNewspaper 1138:049E: a `?:` of two string literals whose condition
  calls a function; SubstituteTextTokens 11D0:029E: a pointer into a local buffer whose offset
  is computed by a call), the translator pushes that word first, with the
  value of the listing's operand (`push ds`/`ss` is the DGROUP selector; `gen/translate.py`
  `early_seg_push`; the generated code changed on 2026-10-09 after round 7, before which the word
  was pushed after the nested call; verified in round 8, verify/round8-R8A.md). The function
  compares the segment push only with the nested calls' `call` addresses: a word pushed after the
  first of two nested calls, or between a nested call's own argument pushes and its call, would
  not be placed exactly, without an error, the pushed constant is not compared with the C argument's selector, and the `mov r, seg X; push r` form takes the textually preceding instruction (round8-R8A.md N3); R8A's bounded search found no other instance of the two known shapes. The C arguments of the call carry the same
  values (one evaluation, held in temporaries) so that the callee can read them without memory
  traffic.
* **The callee's frame.** The first statement of a ported function is
  `uint16_t bp = fr_enter(&FR_<name>, ra);`, the last before each return
  `fr_leave(&FR_<name>, bp);`. The generated descriptor (`core/gen/civ_frames.c`, from the listing's
  prolog and return instructions by `gen/frames.py`) says what the prolog writes: the return
  address (4 bytes `retf`, 2 bytes `ret`; offset word below segment word), the saved BP (`BP+1`
  after `inc bp` for Windows far frames, `BP` for plain frames), the saved DS when the prolog has
  `push ds`, the `sub sp,N` reservation (reserved, not written), and the frame saves of SI/DI/DS
  in prolog order (only pushes that every return path pops again; see "Frame saves and body
  pushes"). `fr_leave` restores SI/DI/DS/BP **from their stack slots** (as `pop` does) and pops
  the return address plus the bytes the return instruction pops (`popbytes`: `retf N` of pascal
  callees, 0 for cdecl). A cdecl caller then drops its arguments with `stk_drop` exactly where the
  listing adjusts SP (`add sp,n`, `pop cx`, a deferred adjustment, or the epilogue's `mov sp,bp`),
  so later frames lie where the original's did.
* **Frame saves and body pushes** (review finding B1). `gen/frames.py` accepts a push after the
  prolog as a frame save (and a `push ds` right after `mov bp,sp` of a plain frame as the DS
  save) only if every return path pops it, in reverse order, directly before the frame teardown
  (`lea sp,[bp-N]`, `mov sp,bp`, or the `pop cx` discards of the locals). The return paths are
  found by walking the function's control flow from its entry, jump tables included (`Flow`),
  so a return outside the recorded address range (`__vprinter`) is found too. Every other push
  is a **body push**: the translator, or rt, emits it with `stk_push16` at its listing position
  together with the matching `stk_pop16`/`stk_drop`. An epilogue that contradicts the accepted
  saves is an unexplained generator finding. Result: of the 37 descriptors that listed a DS save
  before the review, only `__StructCopy`, `movmem`, `_qsortExchange` and
  `_Win87emExceptionCallback` keep it. The 31 game functions (the `push ds` there is the
  segment word of a far-pointer argument to DebugTraceFunction or strcpy) and the run-time
  functions getcurdir, tzset, chdir, unlink, `_chmod` and `__DOScreate` now have body pushes;
  37 live functions have body pushes in all. `test_vm` checks NewPtr, ApplyGlobalWarming,
  `_chmod`, chdir, `__DOScreate`, movmem and `__vprinter` (whose return, popping 12 bytes, lies
  outside its recorded range).
* **Body pushes and pops in core** (review finding M2). Every push and pop inside a function body
  that is not an argument push (temporaries, spills, the `push dx; push ax; pop eax` register
  copies, `push es`/`pop es`) is emitted at its listing position with the listed value
  (`stk_push16`/`stk_push32`/`stk_pop16`/`stk_drop`). The words below SP are then where the
  original left them (prompt 5.2 item 7), which rule 6.6 class (a) depends on. Examples:
  1030:0139 (ProcessCity), 1130:011A (ChooseFileToOpen), 1208:0EFC (CheckMapGrowLimit) and
  1030:97BD..97CA (DrawArrowButton). The translator's push matcher (7) covers them, and the
  phase-3 last-writer analysis counts them as writers. rt follows the same rule (`rt_int.h`).
* **pop cx teardown** (review note N1). An epilogue that discards the locals with `pop cx`
  (Button 1100:0AA2, MapDistance 11C8:02EC; 106 live descriptors, flag `popcx`) leaves the last
  discarded word in CX. `fr_leave` sets `cpu.cx` to that word as the emulated stack holds it
  (`test_vm`). That equals the original's CX only when the port writes that slot (a
  stack-resident local); the slot of a local kept as a C variable holds a stale word
  (verifier notes PlayWaveFileAsync 1150:00C3, RaiseRandomLandmass 1028:09EA,
  DrawLandmassRandomWalk 1028:0A8D). No translated caller reads CX after such a return; a caller
  that does (rule 6.6 class (c)) makes that local stack-resident under 5.2 item 2(e).
* **Copies that overrun a frame object** (5.2 item 2(c) and (e); **choice**: a run-time copy
  into a local buffer that the original does not bound counts as an index leaving the local
  object). Every slot such a copy reaches that the function reads afterwards without writing it
  first is stack-resident, so the port reads the bytes the copy left there, as the original does:
  * MenuZ (1220:0156), strcpy into the 512-byte `textBuf` bp-0x278 at 1220:035A..036D (findings
    C10): `hOldFont` bp-0x58, `lpfnOldWndProc` bp-0x56, `lpfnSubclass` bp-0x52, `pictureSrcPort`
    bp-0x4A, `timerId` bp-0x46, `hTextBox` bp-0x36 (written after the copy only when the title is
    not empty) and `caption` bp-6, reached by copies of 545 to 630 bytes with the NUL and read by
    SelectObject, SetWindowLong, FreeProcInstance, KillTimer, GlobalFree, the picture copy and
    the caption (from 631 bytes on, the copy also overwrites the saved DS, a control word:
    `fr_leave` faults, FIDELITY.md F33); and the parameters `dlg`, `x`, `y`, `colorIndex` and
    `autoSize` (bp+6..bp+0x14), reached by copies of 639 to 653 bytes and read afterwards
    (GetDItem 1220:0387 receives `dlg`) (verify/seg069_1220_p1.md round 2 N1, round 3 T1/T2,
    round 4 N1/N2);
  * DrawScreenTextWithShadow (10D8:02DF), strcpy into the 256-byte `textBuf` bp-0x106 at
    10D8:036D (findings C12): `hOldFont` bp-6, reselected at 10D8:0460 (verify/seg028_10D8.md N1);
  * IntroMovieCallback (11E0:0221), GetIndString's copy of up to 256 bytes into `text` bp-0x4E at
    11E0:029C (findings C12): the parameters `movie` and `frame` (from 85 and 89 bytes, the length
    byte included), read at 11E0:08E5..090A and 08A5 after the copy (verify/seg061_11E0.md N1).
    The locals textX..hOldFont are rewritten after the copy before any read, and windowLeft/
    windowTop are not read on the paths after it: they stay C variables;
  * DrawTextAtPen (10D8:002D) and DrawTextAtPortXY (10D8:00E0), strcpy into the 256-byte
    `pstrBuf` bp-0x104 at 10D8:00A2 / 016E (findings C12): `hOldFont` bp-4, from 257 bytes,
    reselected at 10D8:00D0 / 01A0 (verify/seg028_10D8_r.md T1, DrawTextAtPortXY T1);
    GetScreenTextWidth (10D8:05B8), strcpy at 10D8:05D2 into bp-0x102: no local lies beyond the
    buffer, only the control words (verify/seg028_10D8_r.md GetScreenTextWidth N1);
  * TruncateStringToWidth (1098:0000), strcpy into `pstr` bp-0x104 at 1098:001E (findings C12):
    the parameter `str` (bp+6..bp+9) from 267 bytes, read afterwards (verify/seg019_1090_r.md T2);
  * RunListBoxDialog (1098:1909), strcpy into `itemBuf` bp-0x7E at 1098:1B0F and LB_GETTEXT into
    `selText` bp-0xDC at 1098:1C62 (findings C12): `result` bp-8 (from 119 bytes of the strcpy),
    `lpfnOldWndProc` bp-0x36, `lpfnSubclass` bp-0x32, `keepRunning` bp-0x2E and `index` bp-0xA
    (LB_GETTEXT from 167, 171, 175 and 211 bytes), and the parameters `itemCount` bp+0xA,
    `pSelection` bp+0x12 and `onSelect` bp+0x16 (from 137, 145 and 149 bytes of the strcpy)
    (verify/seg020_1098_p1_r.md T2; LB_GETTEXT counts as a copy, as GetIndString above).
  Parameters beyond the control words are treated like locals: the original reads the copied
  bytes from them until it returns (and the return itself then stops the port, below).
  A copy that also reaches the frame's control words (saved DS, saved BP or the return address;
  IntroMovieCallback from 76 characters on, MenuZ from 631 bytes, DrawScreenTextWithShadow from
  261 bytes, DrawTextAtPen, DrawTextAtPortXY and TruncateStringToWidth from 259 bytes,
  GetScreenTextWidth from 257 bytes, RunListBoxDialog from 125 bytes, counting the NUL) makes the
  original return through them, which the port cannot reproduce: `fr_leave`
  stops with the 5.3 "general protection fault" at the function's return when a control word
  differs from the one `fr_enter` pushed (fidelity entry F33). With the shipped resources none of
  these copies is long enough (C10, C12: latent).
* **Jump stubs** (review finding m3). `write` (1000:402D, `jmp __write`), `_readEntry`
  (1000:3493, `jmp read`) and `__CallRealCvtVector` have `FR_JUMP` descriptors: no frame of their
  own. rt implements a stub as a direct call of its target, whose frame then holds the stub
  caller's return address and arguments (parameters at `[bp+6]` for the far targets).
  `fr_enter` faults if it is given an `FR_JUMP` descriptor (`test_vm`).
* **Frame data** (`gen/frames.py`, 1,065 functions; 854 live descriptors emitted). Kinds, all
  functions / live: FR_WIN_FAR (`mov ax,ds; nop; inc bp; push bp; mov bp,sp; push ds; mov
  ds,ax`) 859 / 699; FR_FAR_BP (`inc bp; push bp; mov bp,sp`) 115 / 91; FR_BP (`push bp; mov
  bp,sp`) 35 / 27; FR_NONE (run-time helpers and leaf code) 53 / 34; FR_JUMP 3 / 3.
  Cross-checks: pascal callees pop exactly their prototype's argument bytes; cdecl game
  functions pop nothing; far frames return far; the declared distance matches the return
  instruction; each of the 1,896 names.json parameter locations equals the prototype layout;
  every epilogue agrees with the accepted frame saves. One explained finding remains (N_LUMOD's
  dead near entry stub, 1000:143A). Register-argument helpers (`__LongMul`, `F_LXLSH`,
  `F_SPUSH`, `__ftol`, ...) have `argbytes` 0.
* **Structures passed by value** (review finding M4). Such a structure is not a C parameter. The
  caller writes the copy into the argument slots with the listing's own form: `rt_F_SPUSH`, which
  also writes F_SPUSH's transient return-address, DI and DS words below the copy as
  1000:14F8..1518 does, or `push dword` for a 4-byte structure (UpdateMapMoveCursor's POINT,
  WdwMapProc 1208:01D7). The callee reads, writes and takes the address of its own copy at
  `[bp+N]` (`FR_PTR(bp, +N)`), as DrawArrowButton does (`lea ax,[bp+rc]` at 1030:96CB, 96D9,
  96F2, 9711). The generated prototype names such a parameter in a comment (`/* rc: 8-byte
  structure by value, stack-resident */`), and `porttypes.push_expr` refuses structures. Scope: 8
  parameters of 7 live functions (DrawArrowButton, DrawExitButton, DrawButtonBevel,
  FindNearestPaletteColor, EraseRect, UpdateMapMoveCursor, RunTextInputDialog with two). A Win16
  API that takes a 4-byte structure by value (PtInRect's POINT) receives its bytes as
  `uint32_t`.
* **ES.** `cpu.es` is loaded where rt's listing loads ES. Core code does not track ES: its `les`
  loads become far-pointer accesses. A word that rt pushes from ES (`push es`) is therefore the
  last ES that rt loaded, not necessarily the original's, which core code may have changed since.
  Fidelity entry "ES words pushed by rt"; the emulated stack is masked in comparisons (9).
* **Return addresses.** `ra` = `(code selector << 16) | offset of the instruction after the call`
  in the virtual code-selector scheme (the translator takes it from the listing's call
  instruction). A plain near call pushes only the offset word; `push cs; call near` pushes both.
  The original's run-time code selectors were Windows' values and are unknown; this is a
  fidelity entry ("return-address segment words use the virtual code selectors").
* **Locals.** Ordinary C variables of the exact width, except the stack-resident ones of 5.2
  item 2 (a)..(e), which live at `0x1420:(BP-N)` with their original offsets (names.json
  `locals[].loc`) and are read and written with `FR_PTR(bp, -N)` and the accessors. Parameters
  that the body assigns or whose address is taken are stack-resident too (the original writes
  the argument slot at `BP+N`).
* **Register variables** are views of `cpu.si`/`cpu.di` of the declared width (`REG_SI_I16`
  ...), so callee prologues save, and epilogues restore, the real values that the original kept
  in SI/DI (5.2 item 7; verifier findings of 2026-10-08). The translator binds:
  * the locals of names.json `register_vars`, and locals whose declaration comment in the
    recovered C starts with the register (`int i;  /* si */`, `/* di: ... */`; names.json lacks
    about 500 of them). A conflict between the two sources stops the translation;
  * register parameters: a parameter that the listing's first write of SI/DI loads (`mov
    si,[bp+x]`) is read and written through the register from that point on. The load is
    emitted at the start of the body when it lies in the straight entry block, otherwise at the
    statement whose marker governs it (DrawLeaderAnimationFrame 1160:2F0C, after GetPort and an
    early return). When the recovered C copies the parameter into a register local
    (`hShapeReg = hShape;  /* si */`), the local is the view and that statement does the load;
  * far pointers that the original holds in DS:SI or ES:DI (a declaration comment that is
    exactly `ds:si` / `es:di`): the C variable stays a `vptr_t` and every assignment mirrors its
    offset into the register (`vm_shadow_si`), as the `lds si` / `inc si` of GenerateWorldMap's
    EARTH loop (1028:00AD..00B9) leave it.
  Compiler scratch use of SI/DI that no C variable holds (`mov si,di; shl si,1; add si,dx` in
  LabelBodiesAndFinalizeMap 1028:0EA7..0EAE) is found by the translator: a write of an unbound
  SI/DI that reaches a call before the register is written again stops the translation unless a
  `regs` annotation (gen/annot.py) sets the register at the right statement. 9 live functions
  have such writes, 8 of them followed by a call (LabelBodiesAndFinalizeMap is annotated; the
  others are annotated with their slices).
* **AX for byte arguments** (rule 6.5, 6.6 class (c); verifier findings R1/C1). Borland pushes a
  `char` argument with `mov al,...; push ax`, so the argument word's high byte is whatever AH
  held. The translator evaluates that byte from the listing (`gen/lsteval.py`, from the
  latest anchor whose run to the push has no merge: the last call, the end of a Windows far
  prolog with AX = DS = 0x1420, or the statement marker; frame stores in the run are followed).
  After a call, AX is the port's `cpu.ax`, which every API glue and rt function and every
  translated function returning 16 or 32 bits leaves as the original does; a void or `char`
  game function whose AX a caller pushes this way gets a `ret_ax` / `ret_ah` annotation with the
  listing's AX on each return path (FillScreenBufferRect 1028:1D15, GetScreenBufferPixel
  11E8:0DCF, SetScreenBufferPixel 11E8:0E19). When no single anchor reaches the push without a
  merge, every acyclic listing path from the prolog end or a call's last drop to the push is
  evaluated; the high byte is taken only if all paths give the same text (a loop on a path makes
  it unknown). A push whose high byte no anchor or path set determines takes an `ah` annotation
  (GenerateWorldMap 1028:044A) or stops the translation. Where AX differs per path, a `regs`
  line mirrors the listing's AX into `cpu.ax` on the path that changes it and the `ah`
  annotation reads `cpu.ax` (DitherPictureTo16Colors 10C8:00E2/0110, RenderTileFromKey
  1118:0389/03A3); `cpu.es` is mirrored the same way where a held push saves ES
  (CompositeTileToBuffer 11A8:1453).
* **Listing evaluation** (`gen/lsteval.py`). A value is computed symbolically from the listing
  over a straight run. Two evaluation points exist: the anchor (clusters: the lines are emitted
  where the run starts) and the end of the run (byte-argument high bytes, multi-path values).
  At the end of a run, a value that holds a read of state the run later changes is stale and
  becomes unknown: a frame read when the run stores to overlapping frame bytes, an
  emulated-memory read when the run stores to an emulated slot (a resident C variable or spill),
  every read after a store the evaluator cannot place, `cpu.si`/`cpu.di` after SI/DI writes,
  and `cpu.sp` after a push or pop.
* **Transient pushes** (a push popped again with no other stack event between, e.g. `mov ax,seg
  data; push ax; ...; pop es`). A push whose value the listing operand gives is written at the
  start of its statement or right after the call of the statement that precedes it, at the same
  SP. If the push lies between two argument pushes of a call in progress (DrawSmTiles 1210:0E59
  after OffsetRect's 0E54 push 0), it is written right after the argument push that precedes it,
  at that SP. A group of pushes whose values the listing's own instructions determine (a
  cluster) is evaluated from its anchor: the statement marker, the preceding call of the
  statement, the argument push before it (ErrorDiffuseDitherImage 10C8:0246, the LXMUL@ operand
  shuffle after memset's 0241 push), or the first instruction of a loop body that has no marker
  of its own (the loop's back-edge target; the body must be expression statements only;
  ErrorDiffuseDitherImage 10C8:02D0). Argument pushes of calls whose arguments merge from
  several paths are never anchors (the C emits only one path's pushes).
* **Held pushes** (`hold`). Pushes held across control flow are written before the statement
  with marker `at` and popped before the statement with marker `pop_at`; `pop_at` "end" pops
  them where the function falls off its end, when only the epilogue follows the pops in the
  listing (CompositeTileToBuffer 11A8:1520/1521 -> 15C3/15C4).
* **Call matching annotations.** `shared` names a listing call that several C calls on exclusive
  paths share (Borland's merged tails); `calls` assigns the C calls of a target under a marker to
  listing calls explicitly, with `@first-push` to name one argument path of a call whose paths
  merge (AdvanceMovie 10B0:05D5 SCOPY@, 10B0:0AD9/10DF ForgetGrPort).
* **Helpers without C calls.** A structure assignment of more than 4 bytes is SCOPY@ (1/2/4-byte
  structures are copied with moves); a 32-bit `*`/`<<` is LXMUL@/LXLSH@ when the listing calls
  it (386 code may compute it inline); an index into a huge pointer with a power-of-two element
  size above 1 is scaled by LXLSH@ when the listing calls it (ErrorDiffuseDitherImage
  10C8:073A, `(long)(x + dx) << 3`), after the index and before the base is read; a cast from
  floating to integer is FTOL@ (OpenResFileStdio 1190:07B0).
* **Statement markers** are comments that start with an address of an instruction of the
  function itself. A comment naming a data address (`/* 12F0:0000 */`) or another function's
  code is a cross-reference, not a marker.
* **Pointer differences.** near: a 16-bit int; far: the difference of the offsets as a long
  (the listing's `sub ax; sbb dx,0` with DX zeroed), divided by the element size with a signed
  32-bit division; huge: `hp_sub` (element size 1 only).
* **Checked build:** the API glue verifies that the words the caller pushed equal the C
  arguments (`API_CHECK16/32`); the translator adds the same check to the prologue of every
  core function in the checked build (phase 3), which catches push-order errors.
* **Calls from `w16` into `core`** (window, dialog and timer procedures, EnumFunc): `w16` pushes
  the arguments in the callee's convention (pascal: first argument first), then calls
  `disp_call_<CLASS>` with `ra = 0xFFF0:kind` (**choice**, fidelity register: the original
  return address lies in USER/GDI code). Frames of USER/GDI/KERNEL code are not modelled
  (fidelity register): a callback's frame starts directly below the words `w16` pushed, which
  lie directly below the API call's argument and return-address words when the callback runs
  inside an API call (SendMessage, UpdateWindow, CreateWindow, DispatchMessage, the modal loops).
  The callback's saved BP and DS words and every segment word pushed for it are the port's
  values (virtual selectors, the port's BP chain); the original's came from USER/KERNEL. They are
  covered by the same fidelity entry as the return-address segment words (review note N2).
* **API calls** (`core/gen/civ_api.c`, generated): the caller pushes the pascal argument words;
  `API_<Name>(ra, args)` pushes the far return address, calls `w16_<Name>(args)`, sets SP to its
  value before the argument pushes (the Win16 callee pops them) and leaves the result in AX
  (and DX).
* **Overflow.** SP below 0xB4BE is reported in the checked build (stack overflow into BSS:
  `g_qsortWidth`/`g_qsortCompare` 1420:B4AA/B4AC and `g_lpScreenBuffer` 1420:B394 lie just below);
  `cpu.sp_min` gives the maximum depth for the acceptance records.
* **UNINIT last writers** (rule 6.6; 5.2 item 2(b) and (e)). One entry per read of an unwritten
  local or register, with the last writer on every path, the class and the port's mechanism:
