### 3.5 Code pointers and dispatch (5.2 item 4)

`gen/dispatch.py` finds every code pointer the binary forms: OFFSET relocations in code (47),
FAR_ADDR relocations in DGROUP data (11: the exit hooks `_exitbuf`/`_exitfopen`/`_exitopen` →
NullExitHook, the `#pragma startup` far entries, the k-d tree comparator table 1420:2420..242F),
and FAR_ADDR relocations in code that are not `call far` operands (none). Cross-check with the
recovered C: every function designator used as a value in live game code is such a target, and
every target referenced from live game code is used as a value in the C. 50 live targets
(RadioMenuDlgSubclassProc 11E0:0B73 is taken only by a dead function) fall into 9 signature
classes:

| Class | Signature (port types) | Targets |
|---|---|---|
| WNDPROC | far pascal `int32_t(u16 hwnd, u16 msg, u16 wParam, i32 lParam)` | 13 window and subclass procedures |
| DLGPROC | far pascal `int16_t(u16, u16, u16, i32)` | DlgProc, CivDialogProc and 4 `BOOL` subclass procedures (List/Modal dialog, MenuZ, TextInput) |
| TIMERPROC | far pascal `int16_t(u16, u16, u16, u32)` | TimerProc 1008:0D68 |
| FONTENUMPROC | far pascal `int16_t(vptr LOGFONT, vptr TEXTMETRIC, i16, i32)` | EnumFunc 1008:0E12 |
| QSORTCMP | far cdecl `int16_t(vptr, vptr)` | the 3 k-d tree comparators, CompareCStrPtrsNoCase |
| MOVIECALLBACK | far cdecl `void(vptr, i32, vptr)` | About/Intro/BirthMovieCallback (AdvanceMovie's `lcall es:[bx+0xA2]`, 10B0:0597/1459) |
| LISTSELECT | far cdecl `void(vptr, i16, vptr)` | OnCivilopediaListSelect (RunListBoxDialog's `lcall [bp+onSelect]`, 1098:1C89) |
| UNITCALLBACK | far cdecl `void(i16, i16)` | DeleteUnit, WakeUnitClearOrders and 11 `*Callback` functions of 11C0 (ForEachUnitInStack's `lcall [bp+callback]`, 11C0:1F1B) |
| VOIDFN | far cdecl `void(void)` | segment-1000 hooks and start-up entries (rt) |

* **Table** (`core/gen/civ_dispatch.c`): sorted by address; each entry binds the ported C
  function once it is defined (the generator looks for the definition in `core/*.c`, `rt/*.c`),
  otherwise a null pointer. `disp_call_<CLASS>(code, ra, args)` resolves the value; an unknown
  value (data pointer, garbage) faults "call through a value that is not a code pointer", a
  class mismatch faults, an unported target faults "not ported yet". The caller has already
  pushed the arguments (3.4). The game's indirect call sites: 4 in the listing, the same 4 in the
  C (checked by the generator).
* **Window procedures** called by `w16` (DispatchMessage, SendMessage, CreateWindow, the dialog
  manager) may have class WNDPROC or DLGPROC: CivDialogProc (1098:1838, declared `BOOL`) is the
  class procedure that InitApplication registers for CIVDIALOG (reference 1098:086C), and the 4
  `BOOL` subclass procedures are installed with `SetWindowLong(GWL_WNDPROC)`; only DlgProc
  (1098:16E2) is a dialog procedure proper. USER reads DX:AX from a window procedure. For a DLGPROC-class target the LONG result is
  `(cpu.dx << 16) | AX`, the emulated DX at the return (**choice** reproducing the register
  model; every receiver that uses the high word of such a result is audited as a rule 6.6 class
  (c) site in phase 3).
* **MakeProcInstance** returns its argument (the original code value) and FreeProcInstance does
  nothing: no game code inspects a thunk value (**choice**, fidelity entry "instance thunk values").
  SetWindowLong(GWL_WNDPROC) stores and returns code values; DefDlgProc calls the dialog's
  DLGPROC through dispatch.
* **Segment-1000 near code pointers** (the near `#pragma startup` entries 1000:41B0 and
  1000:17D8, `__vprinter`'s putter, `_scantod`'s Get/UnGet) never leave the run-time library;
  `rt` resolves them with its own table.
* **Adapters** (`core/gen/civ_adapters.h`): calls whose argument list differs from the callee's
  declaration. The caller pushes every word the original pushed; the adapter calls the callee
  with its real signature and ignores the extra C arguments. Found by the generator in live code:
  `GetDialogItemText` (1098:2735) called with 4 arguments by RunTextEntryDialog in 10F0 (the
  prompt's known case), and `RegisterRandomControlClasses` (1058:0B4D, declared `(void)`) called
  with `hInstance` by InitApplication (1098:06F8, push at 1098:08A4; recovered C in
  `seg019_1090.c`), a case the prompt does not list. The prompt's
  `StubReturnZero_1098_18F7` is called only by the dead wrappers 1068:0000/0026 (D1), so it
  needs no adapter; `DelayTimerTicksLongFn` is a stale cast with the callee's real `long`
  signature and needs none. All are listed in `TRACE.md` (phase 3c).

### 3.6 Handles and system ids (5.2 item 4)

16-bit values from deterministic per-kind pools (`platform/w16/handles.c`): never 0, even and
below 0x8000, so signed 16-bit compares see them positive. **Reuse** (review finding B2, the
same rule as the selectors in 3.1): never-used values first, ascending; after that the value
released longest ago (first in, first out), so a stale handle stays invalid for as long as
possible. An exhausted pool makes the creating API fail (it returns 0). The earlier rule "never
reused within a run" would exhaust the HDC and GDI pools after about 2,000 sprite draws
(DrawTileImpl creates and deletes a memory DC and a bitmap per draw); Windows 3.1 reused handles
too (offsets in USER's and GDI's local heaps). The USER, HDC and GDI ranges are disjoint, so the
w16 layer can diagnose a handle passed to the wrong family; values of different kinds (a global
memory handle and an HWND, say) can coincide, as in Windows 3.1. Each scheme is a fidelity entry
"original numeric value unknown; port scheme X"; U1/G10/Q89 make `g_hAccel` and `g_hInstance`
observable through `g_cities[-1]`. `test_vm` covers the pools (order, FIFO reuse, double free,
foreign values, 10,000 HDC cycles).

| Kind | Scheme |
|---|---|
| Global memory (GlobalAlloc) | handle = first selector + 2, so the selector policy of 3.1 is also the handle policy; GlobalLock returns `sel:0`; GlobalHandle returns the handle in AX and the selector in DX |
| HINSTANCE / HMODULE | the DGROUP handle 0x1422 |
| Resources (HACCEL, HCURSOR, HICON from LoadAccelerators/LoadCursor/LoadIcon) | global memory handles of blocks holding the parsed resource (KERNEL loads resources into global memory) |
| HWND, HMENU | USER pool 0x2000..0x2FFE (2,048 values) in creation order (desktop window first) |
| HDC | pool 0x3000..0x3FFE (2,048 values; window DCs of CS_OWNDC windows are created with the window, common DCs per GetDC) |
| GDI objects (pens, brushes, fonts, bitmaps, palettes, regions) | one pool 0x4000..0x7FFE (8,192 values); stock objects at fixed ids 0x0E00 + 2·index (created first, never released) |
| Timers | SetTimer with a window returns its nIDEvent; window-less timers come from the pool 0x0002..0x00FE (128 even values; the earlier "1, 2, ..." included odd ids, review finding m8) |
| DOS file handles (`_lopen`, `open`) | lowest free of 20 slots from 5 up (0..4 pre-opened); Borland fds equal them. Prompt 5.5 fixes this scheme, so HFILE values and fds are the stated exception to "even" |

**Names** (m8). The core function `WinMain` (1008:0000) keeps its name. The Win32 host's entry
point is `wWinMain` (the linker then selects `wWinMainCRTStartup`), and no translation unit
includes both `<windows.h>` and `civ_core.h` (1), so the two never meet.

### 3.7 Platform scratch selector

Structures that `w16` passes into `core` (WM_CREATE's CREATESTRUCT, WM_GETMINMAXINFO's
MINMAXINFO, WM_DRAWITEM's DRAWITEMSTRUCT, EnumFunc's LOGFONT and TEXTMETRIC) live in the scratch
selector 0x7FF8 (64 KB, **choice**; LB_GETITEMRECT writes the game's own RECT, bp-0x2C at
1098:1B6A, corrected in P4-7), allocated as a stack (nested
callbacks get disjoint areas). They keep their Win16 layouts (section 5).

## 4. Run-time layer (5.4)

* **State** in the DGROUP image at the original addresses: `g_randSeed` 1420:4EE0 (checked
  statically), `errno` 1420:0010, `_ctype` 1420:4BAE, `_streams` 1420:4CBC, `_openfd` 1420:4E4E,
  `_nfile` 1420:4E4C, `_fmode` 1420:4E76, `_atexitcnt` 1420:4BAC, `g_qsortWidth`/`g_qsortCompare`
  1420:B4AA/B4AC (checked statically).
* **Interface** (`rt/rt_api.h`, generated): `rt_<name>(ra, args)` for every live segment-1000
  function: the 43 run-time functions that live game code calls, the 8 segment-1000 dispatch
  targets and the run-time functions they reach, 155 in all (the earlier "51 entries" counted
  only the first two groups; review finding m11). Same call model as core (3.4). Register inputs
  that a helper reads besides its C prototype become trailing C parameters (`REGISTER_EXTRA` in
  `gen/interfaces.py`: `__StructCopy`'s CX byte count). `rt` is hand-written from the listing
  (Borland-exact observable behaviour), with fault stubs (`rt/rt_unimpl.c`) for the functions
  not written yet (117 of 155 at the end of phase 1; none since phase 2, when all 155 were
  written). The common contract and the contract of each source file are in `rt/README.md`;
  each entry's own contract is the comment above its definition.
* **Frames.** Every rt function moves SP by its original frame (`fr_enter`/`fr_leave` with the
  generated descriptor), writes its prolog words and performs its body pushes and pops at their
  listing positions (3.4). The RTL functions it calls internally do the
  same, so the stack below SP holds the original's return addresses and saved registers. The
  contents of RTL local areas are written where rt's algorithm writes them (qsort's locals,
  `__vprinter`'s buffer); a class (a) read whose last writer would be an RTL local that rt does
  not reproduce is reclassified (b) with a fidelity entry (phase 3 last-writer analysis).
* **Helpers** (rule 6.11): `__StructCopy` (SCOPY@), `__LongMul` (LXMUL@), `F_LXLSH` and
  `F_SPUSH` are rt functions with exact frames whose bodies are explicit byte copies and
  `uint32_t` arithmetic converted with `w32` (the frames keep the stack effects of the calls the
  original makes).
* **Start-up** (`rt/rt_start.c`, phase 2) replaces C0W (1000:0000) and follows its listing
  instruction by instruction:
  * InitTask's results come from `platform/w16/kernel_task.c` (hInstance 0x1422, hPrevInstance
    0, the command line, nCmdShow 1; 3.1).
  * DGROUP 0x5146..0xB4BD is zeroed (the listing's `rep stosb`).
  * WaitEvent(0) gives AX 0 and InitApp gives AX 1. These are replaced imports, with the
    original's pushes, return addresses and `retf` pops.
  * `g_startupBiosTicks` comes from INT 1Ah, `g_dosVersion` from INT 21h 30h, and
    `g_protectedModeFlag`/`g_winFlagsHighBit2` from GetWinFlags. All are recorded values (3.1);
    the time of day is the injected one.
  * LoadLibrary("WIN87EM") loads nothing and returns the synthetic module handle 0xFFF8.
    `g_fpMathEntry` (1420:5110, read by no code) receives 0xFFF8:0001. `__fpMath` services
    0/2/3 have no effect; any other faults.
  * The `#pragma startup` table 1420:511C..5140 runs in RunPragmaStartupTable's order: lowest
    priority first, rescanning, entries marked 0xFF. Near entries go through rt's table and far
    entries through dispatch.
  * Then `WinMain` runs, and its result goes to `exit`.

  Two paths fault as unreachable: the BIOS midnight flag (the virtual INT 1Ah never reports one)
  and the INT 21h 4Ch after exit(255). `test_start` runs the start-up to WinMain's entry, with
  the default environment and with an injected TZ: `vm_stop_entry` (`platform/w16/vm.h`) makes
  `fr_enter` of WinMain fault before its frame, with the five argument words on the stack.
* **Floating point** (review finding m7; prompt 5.4).
  * **Encoding** (`rt/rt_float.h`). A `double` in an image, an argument slot or a local is its
    IEEE binary64 bit pattern, low dword first, exactly as `fstp qword ptr` writes it; a `float`
    is its binary32 pattern; an x87 temporary (`fstp tbyte ptr`) is the 80-bit extended format.
    Host arithmetic is IEEE binary64 (`/fp:precise`, never `/fp:fast`). The conversions are
    exact: every binary32 and binary64 value round-trips bit for bit, and every double widens
    exactly to an 80-bit value; a NaN reads as a quiet NaN with its sign (payload not kept).
    Signs are set with `copysign` (MSVC's `NAN` has its sign bit set). `test_float` checks the
    host format, hand vectors (±0, subnormals, the extremes, infinities, NaNs, 1.5f, 256.0f,
    double pi as an 80-bit temporary), 800,000 binary64 patterns through every path against the
    host's own bits and an independent integer 80-bit encoder, 1,000,000 binary32 patterns, the
    memory layout and the accessor forms.
  * **Arguments and results.** A `double` argument is written into the 8 bytes that the
    listing's `sub sp,8` reserves, at the listing's address (`mem_wr_f64`; for example
    1190:1ADF..1AF4 before `pow`). A result returned in ST0 is the C return value of the rt
    function (there is no emulated x87 register stack); the caller stores it where the listing
    stores it (`fstp tbyte ptr [bp+powTemp]` → `mem_wr_f80`).
  * **Constants** are decoded from their image bytes with the binary32/binary64 integer decoders
    (`G_RD(g_flt256)`, `G_RD(g_pediaGapLineFactor)`); `test_float` checks 1.5f and 256.0f.
  * **long double** (prompt 5.4). `gen/porttypes.py` admits `long double` only in rt prototypes;
    anywhere else it raises a generator error. The five long-double temporaries of game code are
    exact in double (**listing**; every value is an integer, or a multiple of 0.5, far below
    2^53):
    * 1068:1004 (ShowPediaEntry): `textY + lineHeight * 1.5f` (int16 operands, the 1.5f at
      1420:10F0), then `__ftol`: a product of at most 17 significant bits and a sum below 2^17,
      both multiples of 0.5;
    * 1190:074A (OpenResFileStdio): for k = 2..0, `pow(256.0, k) * fgetc(fp) + dataOffset`, then
      `__ftol`; `pow` gives 65536, 256, 1, `fgetc` gives −1..255 (`fild word`), `dataOffset` is a
      zero-extended 32-bit value (`fild qword`): |sum| < 2^33;
    * 1190:1B00 (ReadBEWordFromStream, k = 1..0, accumulator zero-extended 16-bit), 1190:1B89
      (ReadBEShortFromStream, accumulator signed 16-bit) and 1190:1C12 (ReadBELongFromStream,
      k = 3..0, accumulator zero-extended 32-bit): the same pattern, |sum| < 2^33.
    In each case the result equals the integer computation, including the −256^k contribution
    of a −1 from `fgetc` at end of file (prompt 5.4). The proofs assume that `pow(256, k)` for
    k = 0..3 takes the listing's integer-exponent path (square and multiply of exact powers of
    two). rt's `pow` (phase 2) confirms it. Classified as the listing does, x = 2^8 and an
    integer y take the square-and-multiply path 101B..103C, whose products of powers of two are
    exact under any precision or rounding control. `test_math` checks 1, 256, 65536 and
    16777216, and negative exponents.
  * **x87 state words** (**choice**; no Windows 3.1 observation). `__ftol` saves and changes the
    control word in a stack local (fnstcw/fldcw). Its value there is 0x037F: the FNINIT value,
    with all exceptions masked, 64-bit precision and rounding to nearest. WIN87EM's per-task
    initial value is not known here. `pow`'s status word after FRNDINT of an integer y is 0x3000
    (TOP 6 after its two loads, no exception flags). Both words live in the emulated stack,
    which comparisons mask (section 9).
  * **Run-time float functions.** Live in segment 1000: `pow`, `__ftol`, `_matherr`, `matherr`,
    `__ExpForPow`, `__ExpCore`, `__LogCore`, `__Pow10Core` (with the 80-bit constants
    `g_ld1e32`..`g_ld1e4096`, partly beyond double range), `__xcvt` and `__StorePackedBCD`. Game
    code reaches only `pow` with x = 256 and k = 0..3 and `__ftol` (the five sites above). `__xcvt`
    serves only `__vprinter`'s e/f/g conversions, and no live format string has one (strings.json
    survey: the two `%`-then-e/G matches, 1080:085B "% exceeds even" and 11E8:01F1 "%     GDI: ",
    are `strcat` operands, not formats). rt implements the reached paths exactly; every other
    path faults (`VM_FAULT_INTERNAL`, "floating-point path not proven exact") until an exact
    80-bit model exists.
  * **`__ftol`** (1000:110D): truncation toward zero to a 64-bit integer, low 32 bits returned;
    NaN, infinities and |x| ≥ 2^63 give the integer indefinite (prompt 5.4); ported through
    `int64_t` after a range check, never an out-of-range cast (phase 2).
  * **Translation of the five sites** (`gen/translate.py`, phase 3a). Floating locals are
    stack-resident (x87 memory operands): a `long double` local is its 80-bit frame slot,
    written with `WR_ACC_T_F80` and read with `RD_ACC_T_F80` (exact: every value stored is a
    double). An integer converted to floating is stored where the listing stores it for FILD:
    the next FILD of a compiler temporary in listing order takes the conversion, the moves into
    the temporary right before the FILD must fill it, and the port writes the bytes that FILD
    reads as the same number (16-bit for `fild word`, 32-bit for `fild dword`, 64-bit for `fild
    qword`; the value range must fit). A `double` argument is the listing's `sub sp,8`
    (`stk_reserve(8)`) and the `fstp qword ptr` into the reserved bytes (`mem_wr_f64` at the new
    SP). A `long double` parameter of a run-time routine is ST0 (FTOL@): nothing is pushed.
    Floating arithmetic (`+ - *` in host double) is admitted only under the statement markers
    whose proof is above (`FP_EXACT` in the translator); any other floating operation stops the
    translation. A floating literal is the image constant the listing loads for it (`fconst`
    annotation: OpenResFileStdio's `256.0` is `fld dword ptr [g_flt256]` at 1190:075E; Borland
    keeps a double constant that a float represents exactly as a float).
* **Closed in phase 2:**
  * the per-function contracts (`rt/README.md`);
  * the x87 state words (above);
  * the byte list of RTL state that cannot be kept identical (section 9, "Comparison mask").
* **Coverage** (Checked build). Every `fr_enter`, and every `VM_COVER` of an entry without a
  frame (register helpers, entry aliases, vector jumps, the task entry), records the function.
  `vm_shutdown` reports the record to the host (`host_coverage`). `test.cmd` merges the records
  into `build/test/<variant>/coverage.txt` (`tests/coverage.py`). At the end of phase 2, the
  tests and harness runs enter 145 of the 155 entries. The other ten are the unproven float
  entries, which fault at entry, as `test_math` checks.
* **Error exits** (abort, `raise`, `_FpeSignalDispatch`, `_Win87emExceptionCallback`) end in
  `_ErrorMessage`, whose USER calls are GetDesktopWindow and MessageBox (the emulated box since
  P4-6). `test_start` follows every path to the MessageBox call, where its MessageBox hook stops
  it with a fault, and checks the message offset and caption there.

## 5. Win16 emulation (5.5)

* **Model A** (virtual 640x480 8-bit screen of physical palette indices), as the prompt requires.
* **Interface.** `w16_<Name>` implementations (prototypes generated in `platform/w16/w16_api.h`,
  Win16 types: 16-bit handles and integers, 32-bit LONG/DWORD/COLORREF, `vptr_t` pointers, code
  values for procedures; a POINT passed by value arrives as the `uint32_t` of its 4 bytes). All
  156 imports are mapped in [API-MAP.md](API-MAP.md): 141 implemented behind `API_` glue (139 for
  game code; GetDOSEnvironment and GetModuleFileName for rt's start-up), 10 dead, 5 replaced.
* **Host interface** (`platform/w16/host.h`): allocation, fault report, log, files relative to
  the run's data directory, directory listing, wall clock (microseconds) and sleep, present
  (virtual screen + palette + dirty rectangle), input events, sound, message box. `w16` reaches
  the host only through it.
* **Win32 back end** (`platform/win32/host_win32.c`, phase 5; `tests/headless/host_headless.c` is
  the headless counterpart). One thread: the host window's messages are pumped in host_present,
  host_poll_event and host_sleep_until_us (choice; prompt 5.5 allows a UI thread).
  * **Start-up** (`win64/civ64_main.c`; the checks in `win64/civ64_start.c`, phase 6, so that the
    Win32-headless test runs each without a window or message box): `civ64.exe [--data <dir>]
    [--scale auto|1|2|3] [--timing classic|host] [--dump-frames <dir>]` (prompt 4 item 10).
    `--data` defaults to the program's directory; `--scale auto` (default) is the largest integer
    k whose 640k x 480k client fits the work area of the monitor that holds the cursor (up to 8);
    `--dump-frames` names an existing directory that receives every present as
    `frame-NNNNN.bmp`, the harness's format. Port-level failures show a message box naming the
    cause and exit with code 2: a bad option, a data directory that is refused (ending in
    `\original\CIVWIN`), missing or unreadable, a missing, unreadable or different civ.exe
    (pinned size and SHA-256), a missing dump directory, an unreadable `vgasys.fon` (the headless
    back end falls back to the first added face instead), the window or its DIB section; faults
    exit with code 3, host memory exhaustion with 4. `RUNNING.md` is the user's description.
    The boot offset is drawn once per process from QueryPerformanceCounter,
    GetTickCount64 and time() (section 6 item 1). The core's `WinMain` is reached through
    `win64/civ64_game.c`, which does not include windows.h (the names clash); the program's own
    entry is `wWinMain` (`/ENTRY:wWinMainCRTStartup`). Since extension SI a second civ64 in the logon session ends
    right after its command line is read, before civ64.cfg (12.2 "Single instance").
  * **Files** only under the data directory: relative paths, no drive, no `..`, no leading
    separator, no wildcards or control characters, at most 127 characters (`hw_valid_relpath`).
    No registry, WIN.INI or clipboard access. The common dialogs' file choice is an in-memory
    dialog template listing the data directory (the system dialog keeps a most-recently-used list
    in the registry). Fonts are read (vgasys.fon from the Windows directory for the system face,
    civfonts.fon from the data directory), never installed.
  * **Presentation.** The 8-bit screen goes through the physical palette into a 640x480 32-bit
    top-down DIB section. Only the dirty rectangle is converted; a host WM_PAINT re-presents the
    retained bits. The manifest declares per-monitor DPI awareness v2. The window is created for
    a 640k x 480k client at the monitor's DPI (AdjustWindowRectExForDpi). Since 2026-10-10 (the
    user's request) it has a sizing border and a maximize box: `layout()` fits the screen into
    the client with its proportions, centred, the rest black; at a whole multiple k the dirty
    rectangle is stretched by k with COLORONCOLOR (nearest neighbour), at any other size the
    whole screen with HALFTONE; mouse positions map back through the same fit; the game's
    cursor is made again at the fitted size; WM_DPICHANGED takes the size Windows suggests;
    the client is at least 320x240. The window class carries the user's icon (`win64/civ64.rc`,
    resource 1, large and small).
  * **Input** goes into a 1,024-event ring that host_poll_event empties; `platform/w16/host_sync.c`
    posts the events into the emulated queue at the game's polls (section 6 item 7). Keys: vk, scan
    code (+0x100 for an extended key) and the character the host's TranslateMessage gives, as its
    Windows-1252 byte (WideCharToMultiByte code page 1252, WC_NO_BEST_FIT_CHARS). Characters with no
    1252 byte are dropped and counted (FIDELITY F81); dead keys are consumed by the host.
    Mouse: client coordinates divided by k and clamped to the screen, with the button state;
    SetCapture holds the mouse while a button is down. **Focus and capture loss** (review P5MT-2):
    when the host window loses the keyboard focus every key still down is released, in key order;
    when Alt is down a Tab press comes first, as Windows 3.1 gave the program's window the Tab of
    Alt+Tab as WM_SYSKEYDOWN (platform fact (inferred)), so releasing Alt does not open the game's
    menu bar (choice). When the capture is taken away while a button is down, the button is
    released where the mouse was last seen. A full ring drops input and logs it once (P5MT-9). Alt and F10 alone (SC_KEYMENU) open no host
    menu. Until UX1 Alt+F4 reached the game as keys (the emulated window manager closes, F78) and
    closing the host window (its close box or system menu) posted WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_CLOSE to the game's
    active window, so the game's own close handling decided (choice; FIDELITY F82). *(UX1, the user's
    direction of 2026-10-15, supersedes this for the host window: Alt+F4, the close button, the system
    menu's last item "Exit Sid Meier's Civilization 64bit" (since HM "Close") and WM_CLOSE are the host's exit, decided by
    `hw_exit_decide`: at once before the game window opens, the warning box in the game; 12.2 "Exit".)*
  * **Cursor:** the game's cursor resources (module cursors) become host cursors through
    CreateIconFromResourceEx at 32k pixels; predefined cursors (IDC_ARROW, IDC_WAIT) map to the
    host's; ShowCursor's hidden state hides the host cursor in the client area. *(Since S2 the game's
    cursor is scaled by the visible layout's cursor factors (12.2 "Cursor"); since UX1 a profile's own
    pointer, the DOS torch, is drawn at `present_pointer_factors`: UX1's Windows game cursor's factors, since
    UX1b between its size before UX1 and the Windows game cursor's.)*
  * **Sound:** PlaySoundW(SND_FILENAME | SND_NODEFAULT, async or sync) on files of the data
    directory only; a missing file is silent. **Message box:** MessageBoxW on the host window. *(Since UX4
    (2026-10-16, F109) `hw_message_box(owner, text, caption, type)` (`host_win32.h`) in place of the six
    `MessageBoxW` calls: an in-memory dialog built and themed with UX1's machinery (F109's light or dark mode,
    following a change at once) with MessageBoxW's semantics: caption, word-wrapped text, the system's icon and
    sound, the buttons of `MB_OK` .. `MB_CANCELTRYCONTINUE` with Windows' own labels, `MB_DEFBUTTONn`, the answers,
    Escape and the close box, centred in the work area of the owner's monitor, modal over the owner; any other flag,
    or a dialog that cannot be made, is the system's MessageBoxW unchanged; 12.2.)*
    **Faults:** the report names the fault kind, the address and the statement, then the process
    ends with code 3.
  * **Waits:** host_sleep_until_us uses a high-resolution waitable timer with
    MsgWaitForMultipleObjects, so the window stays responsive while the game waits.
  * **Tests** (`tests/win32/test_host_win32.c`, built as `w32_test_host_win32.exe`, run by
    `test.cmd`): a hidden window, quiet mode (no dialog ever: messages to stderr, file choices
    cancelled). Directories and the refused original directory, the files sandbox, time, the
    1252 conversion, present/convert/clip, keys and characters, mouse scale and clamp, SC_KEYMENU,
    WM_CLOSE, the cursor, the system font, and the host_sync bridge (posting, clock following,
    poll versus wait, the keypad under NumLock). Since the gate fixes and phase 6 also: focus and
    capture loss, the remaining services in quiet mode (no sound ever), host_fault and host_exit in
    a child process, civ64's option parsing and every data-directory refusal, `--dump-frames`
    (98 checks).
* **Message queue** (`platform/w16/queue.c`, phase 2; prompt 5.5). These are choices where
  Windows 3.1's behaviour is not observed here.
  * **Retrieval order:** sent messages (synchronous, through the window manager), posted
    messages (none: the game imports no PostMessage), the quit flag, input, WM_PAINT (window
    manager hook), WM_TIMER. WM_QUIT is retrieved after posted messages and before input, and
    only without a window filter.
  * **Filters:** first = last = 0 admits everything, otherwise first ≤ message ≤ last. Input is
    searched in queue order, and non-matching messages stay queued.
  * **Input queue:** holds 120 messages. Consecutive mouse moves for the same window coalesce
    (raw moves with raw moves, below).
    When the queue is full a new event is refused, and the harness counts and logs it.
  * **WM_TIMER** is synthesized when retrieved, and only when the input queue is empty. Its time
    is GetTickCount's value at retrieval, and its lParam the TIMERPROC or 0.
  * **The MSG** (18 bytes) is written only on TRUE. A FALSE result charges the PeekMessage cost
    (section 6). PM_NOYIELD is ignored (there is one task).
  * **An undispatched WM_PAINT** (P47b, 2026-10-15; record `p47b-record.md`, review
    `verify/p47r2-review.md` finding 1; FIDELITY F80, F86; `queue.c` header "An undispatched
    WM_PAINT"). WM_PAINT is synthesized from the update regions at every call and stays retrievable
    until the window is validated, as in Windows 3.1, so a loop that passes another window's
    WM_PAINT on without dispatching it (RunListBoxDialog 1098:1BEE: PeekMessage, IsDialogMessage
    only) gets it again at every call. A PeekMessage with PM_REMOVE that returns the WM_PAINT of the
    window whose WM_PAINT the previous such retrieval returned, while no update region has been
    validated or dropped since (`w16_wnd_validations`, a count in `user_wnd.c` that grows in
    `upd_clear` (BeginPaint, ValidateRect(NULL), DispatchMessage's and UpdateWindow's validation, a
    minimized window's dropped region), a partial ValidateRect, a freed window and a window-manager
    reset), and while the virtual clock, read when the call starts (before its own host
    synchronization), still shows the time at which that retrieval returned (nothing between the
    two calls charged time or synchronized with the host), returns it again but counts as an idle
    poll: it charges W16_COST_PEEK_FALSE_US (1 ms) and is a wait point with the host input of a
    FALSE PeekMessage (`w16_host_sync(W16_SYNC_WAIT)`; a ranged PeekMessage holding WM_PAINT waits
    without input, as its FALSE end), before the message is written. Input taken there is returned
    by the next PeekMessage, ahead of the WM_PAINT. PM_NOREMOVE retrievals neither count nor
    charge. Loops that poll something else between their calls (MenuZ's loop 1220:0AC0, whose key
    pump's FALSE PeekMessage charges time; the city screen's PumpCityScreenMessage 1030:9AED) are
    not idle by the rule and behave exactly as before, so no base output changed. Choice;
    **inferred** about Windows 3.1: such a loop spins there too while real time passes, input
    (retrieved before WM_PAINT) still reaches the dialog, and the window is painted once a loop
    dispatches its WM_PAINT (the game's main loop, after the dialog closes). Before, the virtual
    clock stood still in such a loop (a TRUE PeekMessage costs nothing) and civ64 admitted no host
    input (F86), so the game hung. Tests: `test_queue` (`test_paint_again`), `test_user`
    (`test_p47b` (1)), `test_host_win32` (the host path), `p4tests.py` `listbar`
    (`p49-findcity-resize`).
  * **Exposure ignores occlusion** (standing choice, recorded by P47b in `user_wnd.c`'s header;
    FIDELITY F80): hiding, moving or destroying a window invalidates (with erase) every visible
    window whose rectangle meets the uncovered area, also where windows above it still cover it
    (**inferred**: Windows 3.1 limits an update region to the visible region). The covered parts
    are painted under the windows above, which costs time but no pixels. P47b kept it: clipping
    would not remove the list-dialog hang, since the windows a game-screen change moves or sizes
    need painting anyway, and it would change the paints (WM_NCPAINT, WM_ERASEBKGND, WM_PAINT
    traffic and frames) of many base runs.
  * **Key states:** retrieving a key or mouse-button message with PM_REMOVE updates the emulated
    key-state array (0x80 down, 0x01 toggled on each press). GetKeyboardState copies it out, and
    SetKeyboardState replaces it; the host's state is never touched.
  * **DispatchMessage of WM_TIMER with a TIMERPROC** pushes (hwnd, WM_TIMER, id, dwTime) in
    pascal order and calls the procedure through dispatch with return address 0xFFF0:0003. A
    procedure that leaves SP unbalanced faults (`VM_FAULT_STACK`). A message without a window
    returns 0. Window messages go to the window manager's hook.
* **Mouse input** (phase 4, P4-1; `queue.c`, `user_wnd.c`). Mouse input posted without a window
  (the back end's, and the harness's `hwnd 0` mouse lines) is raw input at a virtual-screen
  position; input posted for a window keeps the target and parameters it was given (tests).
  * **Posting:** wParam becomes the MK_ state at that moment (buttons from the posted mouse
    messages, MK_SHIFT/MK_CONTROL from the posted VK_SHIFT/VK_CONTROL key messages), lParam
    and pt the screen position. The MK_ state is part of the queue's snapshot (header byte 1).
    A raw move posted after a trailing move that came from raw input (raw, or already routed as a
    client or non-client move) replaces it and is routed afresh (F14, review 1 D3).
  * **Routing** happens when the queue first examines the message, in queue order, unless the
    filter excludes both its client and non-client numbers. The capture window takes it as
    client input (no WM_NCHITTEST, WM_MOUSEACTIVATE or WM_SETCURSOR). Otherwise the window under
    the point is the topmost visible top-level window holding it and then, within client areas,
    the topmost visible enabled child (a disabled child passes the point to its parent);
    WM_NCHITTEST gives the hit-test code, and a point in a disabled top-level window goes to that
    window itself with HTERROR, without asking. The filter pre-check admits every number the
    message could become (client, non-client, double click). HTCLIENT
    gives the client message with client coordinates; other codes the non-client message
    (number - 0x160, wParam the code, lParam the screen point). Input over the desktop is
    dropped (choice: the desktop window belongs to the system). While the hook runs the entry
    is marked: a nested PeekMessage skips it and input posted meanwhile stays; a hook that
    removes the entry faults (`VM_FAULT_INTERNAL`). HTTRANSPARENT is not modelled (F75).
  * **Double clicks:** a button down within 500 ms (GetDoubleClickTime) of the previous down of
    that button and inside the SM_CXDOUBLECLK x SM_CYDOUBLECLK (4 x 4) rectangle centred on it
    (offsets -2..1, a half-open RECT), in the same window with the same hit-test code, becomes the
    double-click message: in a client area only for a CS_DBLCLKS class, in non-client areas
    always; HTERROR takes no part. The click after a double click starts a new pair.
  * **Removal** of a routed message with HTERROR sends WM_SETCURSOR(window, MAKELONG(HTERROR,
    client message number)) and drops it. Removal of a routed button down outside the active
    top-level window, without the capture, sends WM_MOUSEACTIVATE(top-level window,
    MAKELONG(hit, client message number)); MA_ACTIVATE and MA_ACTIVATEANDEAT raise the top-level
    window with the windows it owns through chains of owners, in their order, expose what that
    uncovers of each and of their children (WM_NCPAINT, the synchronous erase, WM_PAINT later, as
    when a window is hidden; F14, review 1 D1, D2) and activate it; the *ANDEAT results drop the
    message. Then, without the capture, WM_SETCURSOR(window, MAKELONG(hit, client message
    number)). An eaten or dropped message does not change
    the key-state array; the queue searches again.
  * **DefWindowProc:** WM_NCHITTEST from the frame geometry (system-menu, minimize and maximize
    boxes SM_CXSIZE wide in the caption rows; menu bar; scroll bars and the box where they meet,
    HTGROWBOX; sizing-frame edges with corner zones of SM_CXSIZE x SM_CYSIZE; HTBORDER for other
    frames; HTNOWHERE elsewhere); WM_MOUSEACTIVATE and WM_SETCURSOR ask a child's parent first;
    WM_MOUSEACTIVATE returns MA_ACTIVATE; WM_SETCURSOR sets the class cursor for HTCLIENT and
    beeps for HTERROR with a button down; WM_NCLBUTTONDOWN on HTMENU opens the menu (P4-3,
    below), on HTVSCROLL / HTHSCROLL tracks the scroll bar (P4-4) and on the caption, the boxes and
    the borders runs the system commands (P4-5). The frame's arrow and sizing cursors and
    WM_PARENTNOTIFY for clicks in child windows are not modelled (FIDELITY F75).
  * Tests: `test_queue` (posting state, MK_SHIFT/MK_CONTROL, the hooks' order, filters with
    double-click numbers, drops, the eaten restart, coalescing after routing, nested routing, the
    serialized flags, non-client key state) and `test_user` (every hit-test code, routing,
    capture, disabled windows and a disabled top-level window with a child, click activation and
    raise with children and owner chains, MA_ACTIVATEANDEAT, MA_NOACTIVATEANDEAT, double clicks
    and the rectangle's edge).
* **Non-client drawing** (phase 4, P4-2; `user_wnd.c` nc_paint). The frame, the caption with its
  window text, system-menu box and minimize/maximize boxes, the menu bar's items (system font,
  mnemonic underline, grayed text) and the standard scroll bars with their range and position are
  drawn into the virtual screen in Windows 3.1 default-scheme colours; the pixel layout is a choice
  recorded in FIDELITY F26 (user confirmation pending, phase 7). One geometry function
  (`nc_parts`) serves drawing and WM_NCHITTEST. SetScrollPos/SetScrollRange with the redraw flag
  repaint the bar. Tests: `test_user` (caption text and boxes, the bar, the mnemonic, grayed
  items after DrawMenuBar, arrows and the thumb before and after SetScrollPos).
  **Frames painted over (RV4 P47-R2, 2026-10-15; record `p47r2-record.md`).** At the EndPaint of a
  window without WS_CLIPCHILDREN (its paint DC covers its descendants) every visible descendant, at
  any depth (also below a child with WS_CLIPCHILDREN), whose non-client area meets the painted
  region is marked (`Wnd.nc_pending`); its next BeginPaint sends it WM_NCPAINT (wParam 1) first,
  then takes its update region and sends WM_ERASEBKGND (a window that this WM_NCPAINT destroys gets
  0 and a zeroed PAINTSTRUCT). Any whole-frame drawing (`nc_paint`: the synchronous one of a show,
  move or exposure) clears the mark. The mark alone is no WM_PAINT reason, so a parent paint that
  covers only a child's frame, not its client area, leaves the frame covered until the child paints
  for another reason (not reachable in the game). Consequence (review finding 7, recorded by P47b):
  a child's update region is limited to its client area, so a parent invalidation or exposure that
  meets only a child's border or bars marks the child but gives it no WM_PAINT, and the parent's
  paint covers that frame until the child paints again (its next WM_PAINT, a show, a move, a bar
  change); **inferred**: Windows 3.1 keeps update regions that include the frame, so such a child
  would get WM_PAINT and its WM_NCPAINT. A standard bar shown or hidden by SetScrollRange repaints the window itself only (its
  frame at once, its client area and children invalidated with the synchronous erase, WM_SIZE when
  the client size changes; `set_rect`'s REPAINT_FRAME) and exposes nothing in its parent or below;
  before, it exposed the window's rectangle as MoveWindow does, which sent WM_NCPAINT, WM_ERASEBKGND
  (with WM_CTLCOLOR) to the dialog and WM_NCPAINT, WM_ERASEBKGND and a pending WM_PAINT to the
  overlapping top-level windows. Choices implementing the user's decision of 2026-10-11; **inferred**
  about Windows 3.1: it sets its "frame needs painting" state when the non-client area is
  invalidated and redraws only a changed frame; marking at the parent's EndPaint is the port's own
  rule (FIDELITY F80). Rules in `user_wnd.c`'s header ("Frames painted over") and the SetScrollRange
  comment. Tests: `test_user` (`test_p47r2`; since P47b `test_p47b` (2) the bar shown and hidden
  with nothing reaching the popup or the lower window, (3) a bordered grandchild below a
  WS_CLIPCHILDREN child under a non-clipping parent, (4) a window destroyed by its own WM_NCPAINT
  inside BeginPaint).
* **List boxes, statics and DIBs** (phase 4, P4-7; `user_listbox.c`, routing and statics in
  `user_ctl.c`, scroll-bar hiding in `user_wnd.c`, CreateDIBitmap in `gdi_obj.c`). A survey of the
  game's control use (every SendMessage / SendDlgItemMessage site and the opened DIALOG templates)
  found plain string list boxes only (DIALOGs 131 and 140, the Civilopedia's "Listbox" at
  1098:1AB5), no owner-drawn list and no WM_MEASUREITEM / WM_COMPAREITEM; the EDIT messages the game
  sends (EM_SETSEL, WM_SETFONT, WM_SETTEXT, WM_GETTEXT) were already handled; no dialog text has
  a '&', and the game's letter keys go through its own MenuZ code, so dialog mnemonics stay
  unmodelled (choice). The LISTBOX control keeps its strings (LBS_SORT inserts in order), draws
  rows of the font's height with the selection highlighted, scrolls with its vertical bar (or by
  columns with LBS_MULTICOLUMN and the horizontal bar), hides a bar when everything fits, and
  answers keys, letters, clicks and double clicks with LBN_SELCHANGE / LBN_DBLCLK (a click in a
  partly visible last column selects its own item, F17; paging and scrolling count the wholly
  visible columns); its state is not used after a send that destroyed it (F17); standard scroll
  bars can now be hidden and shown (SetScrollRange). STATIC accepts SS_CENTER and SS_RIGHT
  (DIALOG 666). CreateDIBitmap builds an 8-bit device bitmap from an 8-bit BI_RGB DIB with
  DIB_RGB_COLORS (reached only on a device without RC_PALETTE). Rules in the files' headers
  (inferred from the SDK; pixels, limits and the marked points are choices, FIDELITY F80). Tests:
  `test_user` (`test_listbox`), Q17 in `test_io`. Since RV4 P47-R2 (2026-10-15) the list dialogs'
  lists (Find City, the Civilopedia) show their border, and their bar when the items need more than
  a page, once the opening paints are done (the frame rule under "Non-client drawing"), verified also
  after each Civilopedia hide/show; tests `test_user` (`test_p47r2`), `p4tests.py` `findcity` and
  `listbar` (runs `p49-findcity`, `p49-findcity-bar`, `p49-pedia`, `p49-pedia-units`). Since P47b
  (2026-10-15; review finding 1) RunListBoxDialog's loop no longer stalls when another window is
  exposed while a list dialog is open (for example the game screen following a host resize, F110):
  the undispatched-WM_PAINT rule under "Message queue" (FIDELITY F80); `p49-findcity-bar` must stop
  at its virtual-time limit, and run `p49-findcity-resize` (the host view grows while Find City is
  open) closes the dialog and ends with a 1024x768 frame.
* **MessageBox** (phase 4, P4-6; `user_msgbox.c`, the class in `user_wnd.c`). A modal popup of
  the built-in class "#MsgBox" (registered at the first box, so the program's class atoms do not
  change) with BUTTON controls: the buttons, ids and default button of the MB_ codes, an icon
  square for the MB_ICON codes with MessageBeep, the text split at line breaks and wrapped to 400
  pixels, centred on the screen. Its loop disables the owner's top-level window (the desktop
  window or none as hwndParent: no owner; with MB_SYSTEMMODAL or MB_TASKMODAL every enabled
  visible top-level window of the program, at most 32, F16), releases a capture first, takes Return,
  Escape, Tab, the arrows and the mnemonics for the box, translates and dispatches everything else
  (Space and clicks reach the buttons, other windows keep painting and their timers run), and
  ends on a button's BN_CLICKED, WM_CLOSE (Alt+F4) or WM_QUIT (IDCANCEL, posted again); the owner
  is enabled again and the earlier focus restored, else the window active before the box
  (not an icon) is activated again (F16). Rules in the file's header (inferred from the
  SDK; layout, icons and the marked points are choices, FIDELITY F79). A test seam
  (`w16_msgbox_set_hook`) lets `test_start` stop the error-exit paths at the call. Tests:
  `test_user` (every button set's keys, a click, Space, Alt+F4, owner disabling, focus, centring,
  icons, no owner, a long word, WM_QUIT; the question and hand boxes written as BMPs; review 3:
  a system-modal box owned by the desktop disables the program's top-level windows).
* **Disabling the focus window** (`user_wnd.c` `w16_EnableWindow`; since extension D5K2, 2026-10-16, also `user_dlg.c`
  `w16_dlg_focus_successor`, prototype in `w16_int.h`). Windows 3.1's rule: a disabled window cannot hold the
  focus, so EnableWindow(FALSE) on the focus window leaves no focus, and keys then reach the active window as system
  keys. Since D5K2 (the user's direction of 2026-10-16 11:12, "Fix Windows too"; FIDELITY F184, a recorded
  deviation) the focus goes instead to `w16_dlg_focus_successor`: the nearest dialog of the dialog manager
  (`DlgInfo.used`, CreateDialog) that holds the window through WS_CHILD parents, if it is visible and enabled; its
  default push button (the defid DM_GETDEFID would report, read from `DlgInfo` without sending the message) when it
  exists, is not the disabled window and is visible and enabled; else its first usable WS_TABSTOP control
  (IsDialogMessage's Tab order, `next_tab`); else the dialog itself (its DefDlgProc restores a control as before).
  The focus change is the usual one (`set_focus_raw`: WM_KILLFOCUS with the successor, WM_SETFOCUS, then WM_ENABLE).
  Top-level windows, children of windows that are no dialog, and hidden or disabled dialogs keep Windows 3.1's rule;
  hiding (the focus goes to the top-level window, i.e. the dialog), destroying and minimizing are unchanged. In this
  program only MenuZ's 1220:07FC (Go Back on the first setup page) disables a focused control: after a Go Back to the
  difficulty page OK has the focus and the keys work (D5-6). Tests: `test_user` `test_d5k2_focus` (12 checks; DIALOG
  144 and 145 from the game's resources).
* **System commands and window states** (phase 4, P4-5; `user_syscmd.c`, the states and their
  drawing in `user_wnd.c`, the system menu in `user_menutrack.c` and `user_menu.c`, the icon image
  in `user_misc.c`). DefWindowProc: a click on the caption sends SC_MOVE | HTCAPTION, on a sizing
  border SC_SIZE + 1..8, on the system-menu box SC_MOUSEMENU | HTSYSMENU; the minimize and
  maximize boxes are tracked as buttons (drawn pressed) and send SC_MINIMIZE, SC_MAXIMIZE or
  SC_RESTORE on release; a double click on the caption maximizes or restores, on the system-menu
  box closes; Alt+F4 posts SC_CLOSE. WM_SYSCOMMAND: SC_CLOSE sends WM_CLOSE, SC_MINIMIZE /
  SC_MAXIMIZE / SC_RESTORE are ShowWindow's, SC_MOVE / SC_SIZE run a modal loop with an inverted
  outline (mouse, or arrow keys from the system menu; Return accepts, Escape cancels; sizes kept
  within WM_GETMINMAXINFO's tracking sizes) that ends with MoveWindow. Window states: minimized
  windows become 32 x 32 icons in a bottom row drawn from the class icon with their title on the
  desktop, hide the popups they own (WM_SHOWWINDOW with SW_PARENTCLOSING / SW_PARENTOPENING),
  lose the focus (an active icon receives system keys) and get WM_SIZE(SIZE_MINIMIZED); maximized
  windows take WM_GETMINMAXINFO's maximized rectangle and show the restore box. The system menu
  is the bar's first item (Alt+Space, a click on the box, Left/Right from the menu bar) and posts
  WM_SYSCOMMAND. Rules in the files' headers (inferred from the SDK; item set, placement, slots,
  pixels and key steps are choices, FIDELITY F78). Tests: `test_user` (caption double clicks, the
  minimize box and the icon, system keys of an active icon, restoring, caption and corner drags,
  the minimum tracking size, Escape, Alt+Space and a keyboard move, Alt+F4, a double click on the
  system-menu box).
* **Scroll-bar tracking** (phase 4, P4-4; `user_scrolltrack.c`, drawing in `user_wnd.c`). A click
  on a standard bar (WM_NCLBUTTONDOWN on HTVSCROLL / HTHSCROLL) sends WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_VSCROLL |
  HTVSCROLL / SC_HSCROLL | HTHSCROLL, which runs a modal loop with the capture on the window: an
  arrow sends SB_LINEUP / SB_LINEDOWN at once, after 400 ms and every 100 ms measured with the
  tick count (54.925 ms steps) while held on it (drawn pressed); the track sends SB_PAGEUP / SB_PAGEDOWN the same way while the
  pointer stays on that side of the thumb; the thumb follows the pointer and sends SB_THUMBTRACK
  for each new position and SB_THUMBPOSITION on the release; SB_ENDSCROLL comes last. Other
  messages are translated and dispatched. WM_NCLBUTTONDBLCLK on a bar acts as a click (F15). The
  loop also ends when the window loses the capture or when its release was taken by another
  PeekMessage; one system modal loop (menu, scroll bar, move/size, box) runs at a time (F15).
  Rules in the file's header (inferred from the SDK; timing, pixels and rounding are choices,
  FIDELITY F77). Tests: `test_user` (arrow repeat and pressed drawing, a quick second click, a
  release taken elsewhere, Alt during the loop, track, thumb, the horizontal bar).
* **Menus** (phase 4, P4-3; `user_menutrack.c`, with `queue.c`, `user_wnd.c`, `user_menu.c`,
  `user_misc.c`). Rules in `user_menutrack.c`'s header (platform facts (inferred) from the SDK;
  pixels and the marked points are choices, FIDELITY F76).
  * **System keys:** a key message posted while Alt is down (the posted VK_MENU messages), and
    VK_MENU and VK_F10 themselves, is queued as WM_SYSKEYDOWN / WM_SYSKEYUP, bit 29 set while Alt
    is down; nothing is converted while Ctrl is down (choice). The Alt state is header byte 15
    bit 0x40 of the queue's snapshot.
  * **Entry:** DefWindowProc passes a child's system keys to its parent; for a top-level window
    Alt or F10 pressed and released alone, Alt with a character (WM_SYSCHAR) and a click on the
    menu bar (WM_NCLBUTTONDOWN on HTMENU) send WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_KEYMENU / SC_MOUSEMENU, which runs
    the modal menu loop inside the call.
  * **Loop:** WM_INITMENU, the capture on the window, PeekMessage(PM_REMOVE) for every message
    (one API step per iteration, so the harness's timed input and watchdog work as in the game's
    own loop); mouse and keyboard input belongs to the menu, other key downs go to
    TranslateMessage for mnemonics, WM_PAINT/WM_TIMER/other messages are dispatched; WM_QUIT
    ends it and is posted again. Keys, mnemonics, mouse tracking, WM_INITMENUPOPUP and
    WM_MENUSELECT as in the header. A chosen enabled item posts WM_COMMAND(id, 0) after the menus
    close (`w16_queue_post`, ahead of the input); grayed items give nothing.
  * **Popups** are drawn straight into the virtual screen and registered as overlays that every
    visible region and the desktop's repainting exclude, as a topmost window would be. Since P4-8
    (2026-10-09) a popup saves the screen under it (its rectangle and shadow) when it opens and puts
    it back when it closes, so the windows it covered get no WM_PAINT (platform fact (inferred):
    Windows 3.1's popup menus save the bits under them, CS_SAVEBITS); window output is clipped away
    from the open popups, so the saved bits stay valid (choice: the game draws nothing during the
    loop; Windows would discard bits made stale and send WM_PAINT). Before, closing a popup
    exposed what it covered, and after Civilopedia 601..606 the game's list-box dialog loop
    (1098:1BEE), which drops every message that is not the dialog's, returned the exposed map
    window's WM_PAINT for ever. The selected bar item is highlighted.
  * **Accelerators (Q5, decided):** TranslateAccelerator takes the key without any message for a
    grayed or disabled item or a disabled window; for an enabled item it sends WM_INITMENU and
    WM_INITMENUPOPUP (the bar popup holding the item) before WM_COMMAND, unless a capture is set.
  * Tests: `test_queue` (system keys, the Alt state, `w16_queue_post`) and `test_user` (Alt and
    arrows with Return, the popup's pixels and overlay, Alt+letter and mnemonics, a grayed item,
    a cascade, clicks on the bar and on an item, a click outside, Q5's messages).
* *Open (phase 4):* the window manager, controls, indexed GDI, palette manager, raster fonts,
  dialogs, menus and common dialogs; the platform-fact tables (metrics, device caps, dialog base
  units, double-click time/rectangle) are recorded in section 10 as they are decided.
* **Open questions and their owning phases** (`claude/docs/open-questions.md`; review finding

| Question | Subject | Owner |
|---|---|---|

| Q6 | TranslateMessage results | phase 4: **decided** (phase 3a, choice: 1 for WM_KEYDOWN, WM_KEYUP, WM_SYSKEYDOWN and WM_SYSKEYUP, also for keys without a character such as the arrows, PgUp/PgDn and Home/End, 0 for other messages; `MenuZ`'s Up/Down and ProcessKeyboardMessage's arrow digits (1098:0330) depend on it; `queue.c` header, FIDELITY F32) |

| Q10 | IsDialogMessage Enter/Escape conversions | phase 4: **decided** (choice following the SDK's description of IsDialogMessage: Enter sends WM_COMMAND with the focused push button's id, else DM_GETDEFID's id, else IDOK; Escape sends IDCANCEL; both without TranslateMessage; `user_dlg.c` header) |
