Font index test (P4-9, `tests/harness/p4tests.py fonts`, the harness's `fonts.txt`): after start-up `g_civFonts` 0..13 are the faces of FONT resources 200..206, 208..212, 214 and 215 (the file has no 207 or 213), 14..20 are CIVTIMES10, 12, 14, 18, 24, 30 and 36, and 16 is CIVTIMES14. Prompt 5.5's "heights 10, 12, 14, 18, 24, 30 and 36" are these faces' point sizes: their tmHeight (dfPixHeight in the file) is 11, 12, 15, 18, 24, 34 and 38, which the test checks |

## 6. Time model (7)

1. **Virtual clock.** One emulated clock in microseconds since the virtual "Windows start";
   it advances only by the poll costs below and by waits inside emulated modal loops. The
   emulated GetTickCount counts from a boot offset: injected by tests; in play drawn once per
   process uniformly from [0, 32768) TickCount units (converted to milliseconds), and recorded
   in the run log.
2. **Tick granularity.** **Platform fact (inferred):** Windows 3.1's GetTickCount advances with
   the 18.2 Hz timer interrupt (55 ms steps; general Win 3.x knowledge). The emulated GetTickCount
   is `boot_ms + floor(ticks * 54.925)` where `ticks` = whole 65536/1193182 s periods of the
   virtual clock (computed exactly in integers: `ticks * 54925 / 1000`).
3. **Poll costs** (**choice**, fidelity entry "CPU-speed-dependent draw counts"): PeekMessage
   returning FALSE 1,000 µs; GetTickCount (hence TickCount 1100:02E1) 50 µs; GetKeyboardState
   and GetCursorPos 50 µs. Button (1100:0A5D), which prompt 7 lists among the polls, is game code
   whose only API call is PeekMessage at 1100:0A94, so it costs what that PeekMessage costs (the
   earlier separate Button cost was wrong; review finding m11). Every pure spin loop therefore
   ends (11B0:1F10 waits at most 6 TickCount units).
4. **Timers** are checked on each emulated tick; intervals are rounded up to whole ticks
   (**platform fact (inferred)**: the Windows 3.1 timer runs off the 8254 PIT at 18.2 Hz). WM_TIMER
   follows the queue rules of 5.5.
5. Win32 mode uses `--timing classic` by default (prompt 5.5; phase 8 audit A9 corrected the
   earlier default `host`). `--timing host` makes the emulated clock follow the host clock.
   `--timing classic` sleeps until real time catches up with the emulated clock only where the game waits (PeekMessage
   returning FALSE, a GetTickCount spin with no other work in between; the game imports no
   WaitMessage), never for
   poll costs charged during CPU-bound work. **User direction (2026-10-09, `STATUS.md` "User
   directions")**: world generation completes at full host speed in every mode; the Birth movie
   that runs beside it can be skipped with any key as soon as GenerateWorldMap returns.
   Presentation never changes the emulated clock.
6. **Implementation** (`platform/w16/clock.c`, phase 2; `test_queue`):
   * ticks = floor(us · 1193182 / 65536000000).
   * GetTickCount = boot_ms + floor(ticks · 54925 / 1000), modulo 2^32. It returns the value,
     then charges its poll cost (**choice**: the cost follows the call's effect, likewise for
     GetKeyboardState and GetCursorPos).
   * **Boot offset.** u TickCount units (1/60 s) become boot_ms = (u · 1000 + 59) / 60, the
     smallest offset for which TickCount (1100:02E1, GetTickCount · 60 / 1000) equals u at time
     0. This was checked for all 32,768 values of u. The harness's `--boot-units` takes u; the
     run log records u and boot_ms.
   * **Timers.** An interval is ceil(ms · 1193182 / 65536000) ticks, at least 1. The first
     WM_TIMER is due `interval` ticks after the tick in which SetTimer ran; each later one is due
     `interval` ticks after the tick in which the previous one fell due. A timer whose WM_TIMER
     is still pending queues no other, so missed periods are skipped. SetTimer with a window
     replaces an existing (window, id) timer and keeps its pending flag. Window-less timers get
     ids 2, 4, 6, … in creation order (**choice**, prompt 5.2 item 5), and a full table (16)
     returns 0. KillTimer removes the timer and its pending WM_TIMER and returns TRUE; an unknown
     pair (the C1-clobbered id) returns FALSE. Timers are checked in slot (creation) order.
7. **Win32 mode implementation** (`platform/w16/host_sync.c`, phase 5; choices). The harness and
   the unit tests leave the timing at "none": nothing below runs and the clock stays virtual.
   * **Sync points** are the polls: every PeekMessage (the emulated modal loops call it too),
     GetTickCount, GetKeyboardState and GetCursorPos. Each first moves the host's pending input
     into the emulated queue in arrival order, so input reaches the core only where the game
     polls (prompt 5.5: presentation and input never change emulated state). Exception
     (FIDELITY F86): a PeekMessage without a message range takes host input only when it ends
     finding nothing, so input that arrived while the game waited goes to the game's next call;
     one restricted to a range (the key and mouse pumps 1098:02D1 and 1098:03E3) takes it when it
     starts.
   * **Host timing:** at a sync point an emulated clock behind the host clock (time since start)
     is moved forward to it, and timers fall due as it passes them. **Classic:** never moved
     forward.
   * **Waits** are PeekMessage returning FALSE (after its poll cost) and a GetTickCount spin: a
     GetTickCount at most 16 statement markers after the previous clock query (`W16_SPIN_STEPS`;
     a spin loop such as 11B0:1F10 runs a few statements per query, CPU-bound work far more). At a
     wait an emulated clock ahead of the host clock sleeps until the host catches up. Nothing
     else sleeps, so world generation and AI turns run at full host speed in both timings (user
     direction 2026-10-09). At a sync point that is not a wait, an emulated clock ahead of the
     host clock moves the host origin forward instead, so time charged during CPU-bound work is
     never slept off at the next wait either (review P5MT-11: world generation charges about
     1.7 s of poll costs, which would otherwise have been slept before the Birth movie's next
     frame). Each wait therefore sleeps about what it charged (PeekMessage FALSE 1 ms, a spin's
     GetTickCount 50 µs), and input arriving during such a sleep waits at most that long.
     Statement markers are counted in every build for this (`vm_steps`).
   * **Key messages** are posted without a window, so the queue gives them to the focus window
     (else, as system keys, to the active window) when they are read, as Windows 3.1 did (review
     P5MT-1: they were first posted to the active window, which bypassed the focus control; with
     it, activation gives a dialog the control CreateDialog chose even when its procedure keeps
     WM_ACTIVATE from DefDlgProc, so the first Enter at the title menu presses OK, FIDELITY F85). They
     carry the Win16 lParam: repeat count 1, scan code (bits 16..23), extended bit 24, previous
     state bit 30 from the keys already posted, transition bit 31 for key up; the queue makes them
     WM_SYSKEYDOWN/UP and sets bit 29 while Alt is down. While the emulated NumLock toggle is set
     (WinMain forces it on, 1008:02E0) and neither Shift, Ctrl nor Alt is down, a keypad key (scan
     0x47..0x53 not extended, '-' and '+' excepted) becomes VK_NUMPAD0..9 or VK_DECIMAL with its
     digit or '.', whatever the host NumLock (prompt 5.5 "Input"); with a modifier the host's key
     passes unchanged (choice, P5MT-3: Shift+keypad keys are then the host's navigation keys, as
     under Windows 3.1 with NumLock on). A key up takes the key that the same scan code's key down
     was posted as (P5MT-4). Mouse messages are posted raw (no window, virtual-screen position) for
     the window manager's routing and capture. Input the full emulated queue cannot take is dropped
     and logged once (`w16_host_dropped`).
   * **Close:** the host window's close request posts WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_CLOSE to the active
     emulated window (as Alt+F4 on it would); the game's handling decides what follows. *(Since UX1
     this holds for WM_QUIT only: a WM_QUIT in the host pump still becomes HOST_EV_QUIT
     (`pi_arrive_quit`), which posts this SC_CLOSE; the host window's close request, the close button
     and Alt+F4 are the host's exit and no longer produce it, 5 "Input" and 12.2 "Exit".)*

## 7. Translation approach (6, 9)

* **Mechanical first pass** (`gen/translate.py`, phase 3a): translates each recovered C file with
  the port's own front end (`gen/cfront`) and a typing pass. It applies rules 6.1..6.13 by type:
  every `int` operation that the 16-bit target performs in 16 bits is computed in 32-bit host
  arithmetic and wrapped (`w16`/`u16`/`w8`/`u8`/`w32`); every division and variable shift goes
  through the 386-exact helpers; every global/field/pointer access through `acc.h`; every
  string literal becomes `DSTR(0xOOOO)`; every call becomes listing-ordered pushes plus a call
  with `RA(...)`, and a cdecl call is followed by the listing's SP adjustment.
* **Listing cross-checks.** The translator matches each function's calls (in emitted order)
  with the listing's `call` instructions, which yields the return addresses and verifies call
  order (verification check 2); its pushes and pops with the listing's, argument pushes and body
  pushes alike (3.4, review finding M2); its SP adjustments with the listing's. Mismatches are
  reported for manual resolution.
* **Switches.** Jump-table dispatches and sparse-switch value scans through `cs:` become C
  `switch` statements with the listing's case values and order of tests (section 0, review
  finding m1).
* **Stores** whose address and value expressions could affect each other are linearized into
  temporaries in listing order before the accessor call (3.2, review finding m5).
* **Verification.** An independent verifier per unit then compares the port unit with the
  listing (checks 1..5 of `claude/work/AGENT_GUIDE_VERIFY.md` plus section 6) and writes
  `docs/verify/<unit>.md`; recovery defects go to `RECOVERY-ERRATA.md`.
* Dead functions (D1) are not translated; `TRACE.md` lists them.

## 8. Known hard parts (prompt section 8): design notes

* **Giant functions** (ProcessCity 1030:0000, ChooseAiUnitOrder 1078:1480,
  HoldDiplomaticAudience 1160:0000, MoveCivUnits 11F0:0000): translated by fragment using the 16
  fragment units of `claude/work`; each fragment is a section of the one C function (labels at
  the seams), so the single frame, the shared epilogue and every `goto` across fragments are
  kept. A seam check compares the live register variables, stack-resident locals and SP at each
  seam with the listing.
* **Re-entrancy.** Ported functions are re-entrant by construction: their state is C locals,
  the emulated stack and images; there are no hidden statics in the translation. ProcessCity's
  re-entry (G11) happens through message pumps that call back into core on the host stack;
  the host stack depth follows the emulated call depth (the emulated stack is 9 KB, so the host
  depth is bounded). Messages reach game procedures only synchronously inside API calls and
  pumps (5.5).
* **Host stack size** (review note N5). The bound above is in emulated bytes; the host frames of
  the giant functions are much larger than their emulated frames. Phase 3 measures the deepest
  host nesting in the harness (scripted play plus the re-entrant ProcessCity path) and sets the
  `/STACK` reserve of `civ64.exe` and of the harness to at least four times that depth
  (initially 16 MB); the measurement goes into the phase-3 acceptance record.
* **Picture codec (10B8)** (review finding m9). `CvPc`/GIF-LZW decoding must be byte-exact
  (prompt 8): it is ported as game code, with the bit reader, code-size growth and table reset
  exactly as the listing does them, and the harness hashes every decoded picture of the run's
  `.rsc` files (prompt 7). No host codec is used.
* **God palette** (m9). Its reserved ranges (236 PC_RESERVED entries realized in the
  foreground, prompt 5.5) are decided with the phase-4 palette manager.
* **16-colour mode** (m9). Model A (256 colours) is the required path, and civ64 always reports
  it (FIDELITY F25). The game's own 16-colour code (DetectDisplayCaps 1008:059A, ConvertArtwork-
  For16ColorDisplay 10B8:0000 with dialog 666, DitherPictureTo16Colors and the dither kernels in
  DGROUP 1D40..4A91) is translated game code like the rest; the harness reaches it only through
  its test switch `--display 16` (section 9). The Win 3.1 display-driver rule for non-static solid
  brushes on a 16-colour desktop is not modelled, and what the port draws there is unverified:
  after the conversion, the title picture behind the menu came out black (P4-8, port output; not
  investigated, since no civ64 path reaches it).
* **The resource manager (1190)** reads each `.rsc` file whole into the 1,500,000-byte block
  (23 tiles) with `_lopen`/`_hread`; `_hread` crosses tiles (huge pointers); the big-endian
  readers are game code. The stdio fallback is ported with its missing EOF check.
* **Colour matching**: the k-d tree (10C0) calls rt's Borland qsort with the comparators through
  dispatch; equal-key order follows qsort's algorithm.
* **Start-up and shutdown**: section 4 (start-up), WinMain and RunIntroAndTitleMenu are game
  code; ShutdownGame's KillTimer receives the C1-clobbered id and returns FALSE (5.5).

## 9. Determinism, harness and checkpoints (7)

* **Checkpoints** (named; each takes a snapshot): `after-worldgen` (GenerateWorldMap 1028:0000
  returns), `after-setup` (new-game setup complete, before the first turn), `turn-N` (after each
  scripted turn), `after-load` (CivLoadGame returns), `before-save` (CivSaveGame entry).
* **Snapshot**: every segment image (selector, limit, bytes), every live global block keyed by
  allocation id and by the global or field that points to it, the emulated stack, `cpu`, the
  virtual clock and the queue state. Hashes per image and per global/field (through the layout
  headers) give the diffs.
* **Comparison mask** (applied only when comparing with anything other than another port run):
  the instance header 1420:0000..000F (3.1); the emulated stack 1420:B4BE..D8BD and local heap
  1420:D8BE..E4BD; handle and selector values (3.6) wherever the layout headers type a field or
  global as a handle or pointer ([inventory/pointer-handle.md](inventory/pointer-handle.md)).
  * **RTL state that cannot be kept identical** (phase 2; prompt 5.4 "listed by address"). These
    are the selector and handle words that the port's allocators assign:
    * `_psp` 1420:0016, `_hInstance` 1420:0018 and `_envseg` 1420:4FBA;
    * `g_subHeap`'s selector 1420:B4BA;
    * the buffer and curp selector words of the 20 FILEs, 1420:4CBC + 14h·i + 0Ah/0Eh
      (i = 0..19);
    * the `tzname` selector words 1420:4F7A and 4F7E;
    * the `_atexittbl` selector words 1420:B418 + 4·i + 2 (i = 0..31);
    * in each far-heap segment (found from `g_subHeap` along the segment headers' next
      pointers), the next and prev selector words at +2 and +6.

    The far-pointer globals' selector words (`_argv0`, `environ`, `_exitbuf`, `_exitfopen`,
    `_exitopen`, `g_fpMathEntry`, `g_qsortCompare` …) are masked by the general far-pointer
    rule. Offsets are not masked: the exact heap and stream algorithms reproduce them. The
    signal table `g_sigHandlers` and `_atexittbl` stay zero in every run, because `signal` and
    `atexit` are dead. The K1 path's stray decrement (`rt/rt_heap.c`) lands at an address that
    depends on a selector value; it is logged when it runs and is a fidelity entry, not masked.
  * **Implemented** (`tests/headless/harness.c`, phase 2): the instance header, stack and local
    heap; the selector words of the 46 far-pointer globals; the RTL list above; block selectors
    (blocks are matched by allocation id and tile); and the register, clock, queue and
    checked-build records. `test.cmd` alters every RTL position in a snapshot copy
    (`tests/harness/snapmod.py`) and expects a masked comparison to be equal. As a control, an
    altered offset word must still differ.
  * *Open (before the first comparison with a non-port snapshot, phase 7 at the latest):*
    handle-typed game globals (such as `g_hInstance` 1420:54FA), and the pointer and handle
    fields inside structures and arrays. `gen/layout.py` will carry the pointer-handle
    inventory's types into `civ_gtable.c`.
* **Step watchdog** (prompt 5.3 "Hangs", prompt 7; review finding m9). The harness counts
  statement markers (`VM_SITE`, executed in the Checked build that the harness runs) and API
  calls. When a run exceeds its budget (`--max-steps`, default 2^32 markers) or its virtual
  time limit (`--max-virtual-ms`), the harness stops at that statement boundary, takes a
  snapshot and reports `WATCHDOG` with the site; a test that expects a hang (findings C9,
  TextBoxSetup 1068:2072 with a word wider than the box) passes on that result. Win32 play has
  no watchdog: the original's endless loops are kept.
* **Frame hashes** (prompt 7; m9). On every present the headless back end hashes the virtual
  640x480 screen (palette indices) together with the 256-entry palette (SHA-256), and records
  them with the virtual time; scripts compare sequences or single named frames.
* **Event trace** (phase 4, P4-8; `tests/headless/harness.c`, `W16_TRACE` in `w16_int.h`). Every
  harness run writes `<out>/events.log`. It records these events, each with its virtual time:
  * dialogs created from a template (DIALOG id);
  * commands chosen in the menu loop ("menu") or by an accelerator ("accel");
  * popups opened by the menu loop ("popup": the menu handle, the window, and each item as "id+"
    when enabled or "id-" when grayed or disabled, 0 for a cascade), which shows which Orders
    items `UpdateOrdersMenu` enabled for the unit;
  * list-box items as inserted ("lbitem", P4-9: the index, the list box, the item's bytes in hex),
    which give the order of the game's lists (Find City);
  * palette animation ("palette": AnimatePalette's first entry and count) and
    realizations ("realize": RealizePalette's entries changed, the window); one equal to the event logged just
    before it is counted, not logged;
  * sounds;
  * changes of the set of visible top-level windows, with their texts;
  * the first hit of each statement marker named with `--mark NAME=SSSS:OOOO` or `--marks FILE`.

  Each event gets its **first stable frame**: the first present that can show it and that is
  not replaced for the stable time (`--stable-ms`, default 200 ms of virtual time). That default
  is a choice: each phase of the active unit's 220 ms blink counts as stable. An event that no
  such present follows before the run ends is "unresolved"; `features.py` reports it as animated,
  with its first present. With `--dump-frames`, each stable frame is also written as
  `stable-NNNNN.bmp`.

  The trace only observes. Its callbacks read window texts and the screen, and change no
  emulated memory, clock or queue state. Start-up g-45 reproduced g-44's snapshot, run log and
  frames exactly. `tests/harness/features.py MAP OUTDIR...` matches the events against
  `tests/harness/features.map` and reports, for each `FEATURES.md` row, the first run and event
  that reach it and its first stable frame. A reached row says nothing about its appearance.
* **World-generation vectors** (prompt 7 "Seeds"; m9). A harness option stops at GenerateWorldMap
  1028:0100 (a statement marker of the translated function) and writes the 32-bit LCG state into
  `g_randSeed` (1420:4EE0) and the four option words into `g_worldParams` (12D8:38F4..38FB) before
  the run continues, as the original runs did. `time()` injection cannot reach these states
  (`srand` zeroes the high word).
* **Snapshot file** (phase 2). `<name>.snap` is little-endian: "CIVSNAP1", a u32 record count,
  then records of five u32 words (kind, selector, allocation id, tile index, byte count)
  followed by the bytes, padded to 4. The record kinds are:
  1. an NE data-segment image;
  2. one tile of a global block;
  3. the registers (11 u16: SP BP SI DI DS AX BX CX DX ES SP_min);
  4. the clock and timers (`w16_clock_serialize`);
  5. the queue (`w16_queue_serialize`);
  6. the Checked build's out-of-range records.

  The index `<name>.txt` lists every record and every global (`core/gen/civ_gtable.c`, 806
  globals, 46 of them far pointers) with its SHA-256, labels each block with the far-pointer
  globals that point to it (for example `*g_lpScreenBuffer`), and gives the SHA-256 of the
  `.snap` file.
* **Comparison** (`civ_harness --compare A B [--mask]`). Differences are printed as runs of
  bytes named through the layout headers: the narrowest global that contains them, with the
  offset into it (for example `1420:54F0..54F2 g_msg+0x0A`). Block and register records are
  summarized. `--expect-equal`/`--expect-differ`, and `--expect-only GLOBAL[+OFF[:LEN]],...`,
  which requires that the snapshots differ only inside the named ranges, make a comparison a
  test.
* **Harness options** (phase 2; `tests/headless/harness_main.c`):
  * `--gamedata DIR` (the verified copy; `\original\CIVWIN` is refused) and `--run DIR`, a
    fresh directory into which the data is copied (an existing one is refused). The pinned
    `civ.exe` SHA-256 is checked.
  * `--out DIR` for the snapshots, the index and `run.log`.
  * `--boot-units N` (section 6) and `--datetime "Y-M-D H:M:S"` (INT 21h 2Ah/2Ch and INT 1Ah;
    replaces the planned `--seed-time`); `--env NAME=value`, repeatable, for an injected
    environment block.
  * `--script FILE` with lines `hwnd <hex>` (`hwnd 0`: raw input, routed by the window manager;
    `hwnd active`, P4-8: each following event goes to the window that is active when it is
    posted, for a dialog whose handle the script cannot know) and
    `at <ms> keydown <vk> <scan> [<char>]`,
    `keyup <vk> <scan>`, `char <code>`, `move|ldown|lup|rdown|rup <x> <y>`. Events are posted at
    the first API call at or after their virtual time. A key down's optional character is posted
    by TranslateMessage when the game translates that key down (FIDELITY F32); a whole key press
    is `keydown <vk> <scan> <char>` and `keyup`, and a `char` line alone is a character message
    without a key down.
  * `--checkpoint NAME=SSSS:OOOO` takes a snapshot at a statement marker (NAME, then NAME-2 …
    on later hits).
  * `--probe NAME=SSSS:OOOO,DISP` (checked build) logs, at every hit of the statement marker, the
    word at 1420:(BP+DISP) and the last writer of each of its bytes (below).
  * `--max-steps N` and `--max-virtual-ms N` set the watchdog.
  * `--file-answer NAME|-` (phase 3b, repeatable): the answers of the common file dialogs, in
    order; `--seed-file SRC=NAME` copies a file written under `build\` into the fresh run
    directory before the start (an edited save, a hall of fame of an earlier run).
  * `--mark NAME=SSSS:OOOO`, `--marks FILE` and `--stable-ms N` (P4-8): the event trace (above).
  * `--display 256|16` (P4-8, test switch): with 16, GetDeviceCaps reports a 16-colour planar VGA
    desktop (BITSPIXEL 1, PLANES 4, NUMCOLORS 16, RASTERCAPS without RC_PALETTE, SIZEPALETTE 0,
    NUMRESERVED 0; `w16_caps_16_colours` in `w16_int.h`, set after `w16_reset`), so that the
    game's artwork conversion (dialog 666) can be reached; nothing else changes. civ64 never sets
    it (FIDELITY F25).
  * `--boot-ms N` (P4-9): the boot offset in milliseconds (0..2^32-1), for offsets beyond
    `--boot-units`' 32,767 TickCount units, such as the TickCount wrap at 71,582,788 ms (findings
    H10); the last of the two options counts.
  * `--hash-pictures 1` (P4-9): `<out>/pictures.txt`, the SHA-256 of the pixels that LoadGifPicture's
    GIF-LZW decoder produced for each picture reaching DitherPictureTo16Colors (10C8:0000; its
    stack arguments at 10C8:0012, read through huge pointers), before dithering. With
    `--display 16` the artwork conversion passes every picture it converts there: scenario
    display16 hashes 198 pictures, one per `.16c` file. The same file also gets a "decoded" line
    for every picture that LoadGifPicture (10B8:11BC) decodes, in any display mode. The id comes
    from its first statement (10B8:11CB). The SHA-256 of the pixels comes from PicDecompress's
    last statement (10B8:1B3D: dest, destStride, width, height), followed by the colorBase that
    the decoder adds to every pixel.
    The run's `.rsc` files hold 199 CvPc pictures. `scenarios.py` `rsc_pictures` lists them,
    reading the resource maps as OpenResFile 1190:0000 does. The conversion's id ranges
    (10B8:01D3..) leave out picture 137, the EARTH map that GenerateWorldMap reads (1028:0096),
    so scenario earth also runs with `--hash-pictures 1`. Together the two runs hash all 199
    pictures, with one hash per picture and colorBase (`build/harness/decoded.txt`).
  * At the end of every game-core run the harness writes `<out>/fonts.txt` (P4-9): each
    `g_civFonts` entry with its face's FONT resource id, tmHeight and face name (observation
    only). `--mark`/`--marks` take up to 1,024 markers; `tests/harness/features.marks` holds the
    feature marks and, from `tests/harness/findmarks.py`, the statements of the G, C and R
    findings cited in `core/*.c`, which `tests/harness/findcov.py` reports.
  * Targeted findings scenarios (P4-9; `tests/harness/p4tests.py findings`): edited copies of
    `CAE3540B.SAV` set up a case and a short script plays it (AI cities completing a Diplomat, a
    Nuclear, a Caravan and a Palace; a starving one-city civ; a walled city attacked and an
    undefended one captured; Diplomat incite, sabotage and bribe; a Caravan entering a foreign
    city; SS Structurals with the Apollo Program standing; turn 60; an AI Fighter; the unit stack
    list; Settlers ordered to build a fortress; the capture of a five-city civ's capital, its
    four extra cities founded by the edit; four Triremes in map column 0; a Diplomat meeting the
    Zulu king with a military proposal; civ 0's unit table full at a sea uprising; a crowded
    world). Each checks that the finding's statements ran and, where the snapshots show it, the
    documented effect (G2, G6, G10, G12, G13, G16, G19, G31, G33, G44, G53, C6).
    The crowded world (`crowd()`) fills city slots 5..127 with size-21 AI cities, makes the map
    visible to every civ (ProcessCity drops the squares a city's owner has not seen, so the
    citizens would all be entertainers and the unrest test would compare 0 with 0) and puts the
    AI civs in contact with the human (no first-contact audience stops the turns). The random
    city picks of ProcessGameTurn draw from the stream that srand(time()) seeds at start-up, so
    the virtual date steers them: G6 on 1992-05-10, C7 on 1992-05-15; G44 is reached on every
    date tried once the AI civs know every advance. `p4tests.py c2` covers C2 and the C2 bytes
    (civ 3's grid built from `g_spaceshipLayout`, read from the running game, so that the
    estimate is 66 % and the earlier launch triggers fail; mask 0xBF reads 12D8:45C2, mask 0xFF
    faults at 12D8:4642). With the other runs all 93 findings cited in the core have a
    statement executed (`findcov.py`). G6's second site, TransferCityByRevolt's receiver loop
    (11B8:0F95), runs only for newCiv -1, which no caller passes; it is counted through G6's
    first site and is unreachable as far as the code shows.
  * `--core game|stub` (phase 3a): the translated game's WinMain (default) or the phase-2 stub
    core.
  * `--expect EXIT|FAULT|WATCHDOG`, `--expect-code N`, `--expect-site SSSS:OOOO` and
    `--expect-dropped N` turn a run into a test.

  The run ends with an `exit`, `fault` or `watchdog` snapshot and a one-line summary.
* **Last writers and UNINIT probes** (checked build; rule 6.6 tests, added 2026-10-09). `vm.c`
  records, for every DGROUP byte, the step, site and kind of its latest write (`vm_last_write`,
  `VmWriteInfo` in `vm.h`): `code` (a ported statement; site = its statement marker), `prolog`
  (fr_enter's frame words; site = the entered function) or `platform` (platform code inside an API
  call: between the generated adapter's `VM_API_ENTER`, after it pushes the return address, and
  `VM_API_LEAVE`, outside the frames of ported procedures that the call re-enters). Every
  `VM_API_ENTER` also records the emulated SP, in a monotonic list (the highest SP of all later
  calls) and in a ring of the latest 262,144 calls (the call closest above a byte). `api_after`
  says that an API call made after the byte's last write had its SP above the byte: in the
  original, that call's USER/GDI/KERNEL frames, which the port does not model (prompt 5.2 item 7,
  FIDELITY F3), ran below its SP and may have overwritten the byte, which rule 6.6 defines as
  class (b). `vm_frame_steps` gives the innermost frame's entry step, so a probe tells a stale word
  (last written before the frame was entered) from one the frame wrote itself.
  `tests/harness/uninit.py` runs S2, S3 and the soak scenarios S4/S5 (`tests/harness/mksoak.py`:
  Space and Enter every 500 ms from the saved game or a new game until the human civ is destroyed
  and the game exits) with one probe per UNINIT site, and checks every stale read of a class (a)
  site: a ported writer and no API call above it since. This is a statement about the port's
  model (self-consistency): that the original's last writer is the same code rests on the frame
  layouts being the original's, not on an observation of the original. The release build has none
  of this (`VM_API_ENTER`/`VM_API_LEAVE` expand to nothing). The soaks also run the checked
  build's out-of-range triage over about 200 turns of play.
* **Edited saves** (P4-8; `tests/harness/saveedit.py`). Rows that a short run cannot reach
  (research completion, the quiz, spaceships, the conqueror ending and replay, the arrival,
  Clean Up Pollution, Unload) use copies of `CAE3540B.SAV` with named fields changed
  (`saveedit.py`'s field table, from `claude/docs/structs/misc.md` 3, or `byte[OFFSET]`), written
  to `build/p4/saves` and seeded into the run with `--seed-file`; `tests/harness/scenarios.py`
  lists the edits. The image is decoded and re-encoded with `savecheck.py`'s RLL model, and each
  edited file must decode back to its image; a no-change round trip rebuilds `CAE3540B.SAV` byte
  for byte. The edits are test input: what the port does with them is not a statement about the
  original.
* **Stub core** (phase-2 gate). `tests/headless/stub_core.c` is a WinMain that is not game code
  (`StubWinMain`). It exercises the real start-up, rt, the clock, timers, queue, dispatch and the
  harness through the core call model; its markers use the free offsets 1008:F000... Since the
  translated WinMain exists (phase 3a), the task entry calls WinMain through `rt_winmain`
  (`rt/rt_int.h`), which is the game's WinMain unless the harness runs with `--core stub`; the
  phase-2 gate runs of `test.cmd` pass `--core stub`. The world-generation vector option (above)
  needs the translated GenerateWorldMap's marker and comes with phase 3a.

## 10. Platform facts (inferred)

| Fact | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Global-heap / selector-limit granularity | 32 bytes | general Win16 knowledge (KERNEL global arena); satisfies C1; makes C2 masks 0xEF..0xFF fault (3.1). No Windows 3.1 observation in this project; OTVDM is not Windows 3.1 evidence |
| Timer tick | 54.925 ms (65536/1193182 s), intervals rounded up to whole ticks | general PC/Win 3.1 knowledge (8254 PIT 18.2 Hz) |
| GetTickCount granularity | one timer tick (≈55 ms) | general Windows 3.x knowledge |
| DGROUP order | data, stack, local heap | data-structures.md inference; prompt 5.1 |
| Instance header 1420:0000..000F | zero word, saved SS:SP, pLocalHeap, pAtomTable, pStackTop, pStackMin, pStackBottom; InitTask writes the stack bounds | data-structures.md 2.2 (inferred); general Win16 knowledge of the instance data |
| Largest global block (386 enhanced mode) | 16 MB − 64 KB | Windows 3.1 SDK description of GlobalAlloc (general knowledge, not verified here) |
| Screen | 640x480, 8 bpp, 256-entry palette, 20 static colours | prompt 5.5; Win 3.1 SVGA 256-colour driver class |
| Double click | 500 ms (GetDoubleClickTime default), the SM_CXDOUBLECLK x SM_CYDOUBLECLK = 4 x 4 rectangle centred on the first click (offsets -2..1: a half-open RECT, choice of the edge) | Windows 3.1 defaults (general knowledge, not verified here); P4-1, F14 |
| Caption boxes and sizing corners | system-menu, minimize and maximize boxes SM_CXSIZE (18) wide; corner zones SM_CXSIZE x SM_CYSIZE | metrics table (`user_misc.c`); the exact Windows 3.1 zones are not documented (choice), P4-1 |
| System keys | WM_SYSKEYDOWN / WM_SYSKEYUP for F10 and for keys pressed while Alt is held (lParam bit 29 while Alt is down); Alt or F10 pressed and released alone opens the menu bar | Windows 3.1 SDK description of the keyboard messages and DefWindowProc (general knowledge, not verified here); Ctrl+Alt left unconverted (choice); P4-3 |

*Open:* whether the non-last tiles of a huge block have limits that run to the end of the
block (review note N3, 3.1). *Open (phase 4):* system metrics, dialog base units, device caps
beyond the prompt's list, the WM_QUERYNEWPALETTE /
WM_PALETTECHANGED rules.

## 11. Process record

* Session 1 (2026-10-08): phase 0 inventories; phase 1 generators, NE loader, interfaces and
  this design. See `STATUS.md`.
* Session 1, phase-1 review (2026-10-08): an independent reviewer (subagent, read-only) found 2
  blockers, 4 major and 11 minor findings and 5 notes; the report and the resolution of every
  finding are in [phase-1-review.md](phase-1-review.md). The resolutions changed `gen/frames.py`
  (B1, m3, N1), the selector allocator and new handle pools (B2, m2, m8), the accessor tags and
  checked forms (M3, m4, m5), the parameter model of structures passed by value (M4), the fault
  fallbacks (m6), floating point (m7, `rt/rt_float.c`), the NE loader bounds (m10) and this
  design (M1, M2, m1, m9, m11, N2, N3, N5). Selector and handle reuse (B2) deviates from the
  prompt's literal "never reused" and awaits the user's confirmation.
* Session 1, phase 2 (2026-10-08). Work done:
  * all 155 rt entries (`rt/README.md`);
  * the w16 skeleton: memory, selectors, handles, the KERNEL global heap, task state and
    start-up queries, the virtual DOS, clock, timers and the message queue;
  * the headless back end, with frame hashes and coverage;
  * the harness and the stub core.

  Decided in this phase: Q7, Q8, Q14 and Q16; the x87 state words; the RTL comparison mask; the
  snapshot format and the harness options. The gate record is
  [phase-2-acceptance.md](phase-2-acceptance.md).

## 12. Extension: host scaling, map zoom, DOS profile

The extension track (`claude/docs/x64-port-scaling-dos-prompt.md`; ledger section "Extension track" in
STATUS.md; gate records `ext-<phase>-acceptance.md`). This section grows phase by phase.

### 12.1 Layering (S1)

* `present/` is portable C under the rules of section 1 and is compiled with the same three language
  checks (`build.cmd` adds `present\*.c` to the portable loop and `/I present` to every compile). It reads
  the emulation and makes pictures; it changes no core-visible state, no emulated clock and no base trace
  (invariant P0).
* `present/frame.[ch]`: logical frames (W x H XRGB, damage rectangle). `present/scale.[ch]`: the layouts,
  filters and inverse mapping of extension 5.7 (definitions in `scale.h`). `present/present.[ch]`: the state
  and entry points.
* **The present path.** `w16_present` (`gdi_pal.c`) keeps its dirty rectangle and throttle exactly; where
  it called `host_present` it calls `present_core(screen, palette, rect)`, which (1) converts the rectangle
  into the Windows profile's logical frame, (2) scales the damage into the back end's picture buffer and
  calls `host_show(rect)`, then (3) calls `host_present` with the unchanged arguments. So the core frame
  trace (the headless core frame hash, the frame numbering, `--dump-frames`) is the same call sequence as
  before; the presented frame is built from the presented screen inside `w16_present`, never from the live
  screen (review D13). Since D3, steps (1) and (2) are `present_core_frame`, run inside the observer guard
  on a copy of the rectangle, and `w16_present` then calls `host_present` itself, unconditionally (12.5
  "As implemented (D3)", 12.10). Since TLD (2026-10-17, the user's report of 2026-10-16 about 20:03 UTC; FIDELITY
  F192; [ext-TLD-record.md](ext-TLD-record.md)), `w16_present_view` (`gdi_pal.c`, a w16 service in `host.h`) gives
  the pending rectangle (the whole screen after a palette change) to `present_core_frame` inside the observer guard
  without `host_present`, leaving the dirty rectangle, the palette flag and the throttle's clock unchanged.
  `commdlg.c` calls it before the file choosers, and the Win32 back end before its modal boxes and loops on the game
  thread (WM_INITMENU, WM_ENTERSIZEMOVE, the exit warning, the profile switch; `view_current`, counted by
  `hw_view_flushes`). So a host box stands over the game's current picture, as under Windows 3.1, and the core frame
  trace is unchanged. It is forbidden inside the observer's guard (`W16_OBS_FORBID`), as `w16_present` is.
* **host.h** (extension 5.1): `host_present` keeps its role and now only records the core frame
  (headless: hash, frame callbacks, dumps; Win32: `--dump-frames` and the message pump). New:
  `host_show(x0, y0, x1, y1, logical, w, h)`, the 1:1 present of a rectangle of the back end's picture
  buffer, with the presented logical frame for hashing. The back end hands its buffer to present/ with
  `present_set_view(bits, cw, ch)`; present/ then draws the whole client into it at once.
* **Win32** (`host_win32.c`): the picture buffer is a 32-bit top-down DIB section of the client's size,
  recreated when the client size changes (WM_SIZE; a minimized window keeps it) and drawn completely by
  present/; `host_show` BitBlts the rectangle 1:1; WM_PAINT BitBlts the whole retained picture. Mouse
  positions go through `present_view_point` (5.7's mapping, clamped). The minimum client is 640x480
  (S-1). The game cursor is still made by `CreateIconFromResourceEx` at 32 logical pixels scaled as the
  image is (S2 replaces this by nearest-neighbour scaling with the hotspot).
* **Headless** (`host_headless.c`): a view buffer (`hh_set_view`; the harness default 640x480,
  `--host-size WxH`); `host_show` counts presents and, when `hh_show_fn` is set, computes the **presented
  frame hash**: SHA-256 of W and H as 4-byte little-endian words, then every XRGB pixel as the 4 bytes
  B, G, R, X (row by row, top-down). The harness (`--presented 1`) writes `<out>/presented.log`
  ("presented N core_frame F virtual_us US w W h H sha256 SHA") and checks at every present that the
  Windows profile's frame is the RGB conversion of the virtual screen through the system palette (the
  relation of extension 5.1; since MZW, 2026-10-16, except, at map zoom -1 and +1, the World Map pixels that MZW's
  patch owns (`pmap_smrect_owns`: the zoom rectangle's border and the hidden core's frame, 12.9), which the check
  admits exactly; every other pixel, level 0 and every run without the zoom keep the relation for every pixel); mismatches are counted ("end presents N relation_mismatches M") and printed
  as HARNESS_PRESENT_MISMATCH. The relation is checked for the shows of `present_core`; the one show of
  attaching the view at run start (before the core has presented, the logical frame still blank) is
  labelled `view_attached` and not compared. `--scale-mode`, `--filter`, `--aspect` set the policy. None of these options
  changes the core's run. Since TLD the headless `host_choose_file` prints, at each file dialog it answers (not at a
  slot screen the `--slot-input` tokens answer), the console line `[chooser] open|save virtual_us US profile P shown N
  SHA windows_frame current|STALE|none`: the visible profile, the shows so far, the last shown frame's hash, and
  whether the Windows frame equals the virtual screen's RGB (the relation above, with MZW's exception);
  `tests/harness/ext_tld.py` reads it.
* **Scaler** (`scale.h` has the exact definitions): `integer` (kx = ky = min(CW/W, CH/H), or the 6/5 pairs
  within 5 % for a 320x200 frame with 4:3, the largest kx*ky, ties by the smaller error, none: square),
  `fit` (exact integer SW x SH; `stretch` was removed in phase G, 12.4; under `integer` a client smaller than the frame takes fit's layout); filters `nearest` (floor(x*W/SW)) and `sharp` (area overlaps of
  each axis as weights round(o*65536/W), the first largest taking the rest so that they sum to 65536;
  a channel is (sum wx*wy*c + 2^31) >> 32, separable, a pixel inside one source pixel copied exactly).
  Placement floor((C-S)/2), borders black. Mouse: (floor((hx-ox)*W/SW), floor((hy-oy)*H/SH)), clamped.
  Damage: the client rectangle of every image pixel whose source area meets the logical damage.
* **Tests** (extension 7.1): `tests/test_present.c` — 5 policy/filter combinations x 3 frame kinds
  (640x480, 320x200, 320x200 at 4:3) x 12 client sizes (the 7.1 list and 641x481, 1001x703, 1599x1199,
  600x400) on a hand-written pattern; each group's digest of the per-case digests is a golden in the
  source (per-case digests and layouts in `build/test/<variant>/present-goldens.txt`); per case: the whole
  rectangle, black borders and no high byte, damage rendering equal to a whole rendering, every client pixel
  (and 3 beyond each edge) maps into the frame, on the image the mapping is the nearest sample and integer
  blocks map back to their pixel; the 6/5 pairs (1920x1080 gives 4x5, 1600x1200 5x6, 640x480 falls back to
  2x2), fit sizes; the facade (the shown frame equals the RGB conversion; a changed pixel appears as a 2x2
  block of a 1280x960 view; view points and clamping). Timing of a whole 3840x2160 frame is printed
  (informational).
* **G-1** (extension 4): `tests/harness/ext_g1.py` compares the core frame lines, the snapshot/result lines
  and `events.log` of 77 harness runs with `ext-g1-baseline.txt`, recorded from the pre-extension Checked
  run (review D3/D4).

### 12.2 Window, full screen, cursor, chords and settings (S2)

* **Options** (extension 5.11, `win64/civ64_start.c`): `--scale auto|1..8`, `--window WxH` (client, at
  least 640x480; not with `--scale k`), `--fullscreen`, `--scale-mode`, `--filter`, `--aspect`,
  `--dump-presented DIR`, `--dos-data DIR|ZIP|none` (phase R), `--graphics windows|dos` (phase D3; the
  start profile, 12.5), `--zoom default|-1|0|+1` (phase M, 12.9; `default` and `0` are the same level;
  anything else exits 2 with the usage; a `CIV_OBSERVER 0` build refuses it with a message box and exit
  2), `--multi-instance` (SI, a test option without a value: the run neither takes nor checks the single-instance
  lock, "Single instance" below). A bad value or combination is a port-level failure (message box, exit code 2).
* **Settings** (`win64/civ64_cfg.c`): `%LOCALAPPDATA%\civ64\civ64.cfg` (SHGetKnownFolderPath), UTF-8
  `key=value` lines, unknown keys and comments kept, written to `civ64.cfg.tmp` and moved over the file.
  Keys: `scale_mode` (integer|fit; a remembered `stretch` reads as absent), `filter` (nearest|sharp), `aspect_4_3` (1|0; anything else reads as absent; since UX3, `civ64_cfg_aspect()` / `civ64_cfg_set_aspect(c, chosen)`: written only when the player changes the item in the run, through `hw_set_aspect_hook`; the keys `aspect` (until UX2) and `aspect_320x200` (UX2: square|4:3, decision 1 of `ext-UX2-record.md`) are not read and are removed at every save; decision 2 of `ext-UX3-record.md`), `layout_screen` (WxH, 12.4), `fullscreen`
  (0|1), `window` (l,t,r,b) and `maximized` (0|1) (F111, below), phase R's `windows_data`, `dos_data`, `dos_ask` (never)
  and, since D3, `graphics` (`windows`|`dos`; anything else reads as absent) and, since M, `zoom` (`-1`|`0`|`1`;
  anything else reads as absent, the level is then 0) and, since LP, `wheel_zoom` (`1` on, `0` off; absent or
  anything else is ignored and the wheel stays on; `civ64_cfg_wheel_zoom()` returns 1, 0 or -1 for absent or
  invalid, `civ64_cfg_set_wheel_zoom()` writes it; written only after a change by the player, by the hook
  `wheel_chosen` of `win64/civ64_main.c`, never from the start state; a remembered `0` is applied with
  `hw_set_wheel_zoom(0)` once the window exists; a `CIV_OBSERVER 0` build neither reads nor writes it). The
  command line wins for the run and is not written; the player's changes through the system menu or Alt+Enter are written at once (the
  back end's settings hook). (Pre-existing, noted in UX2 and kept by it: "not written" holds for `graphics`, `zoom` and
  `wheel_zoom` only. The settings hook, `save_settings`, runs at every presentation change by the player and at every
  exit (`host_exit`, F111) and writes the presentation keys `scale_mode`, `filter` and `fullscreen` from the current
  state each time, so a command-line value of these is written at that run's next save. Until UX3 it wrote the aspect
  the same way; since UX3 "The command line wins for the run and is not written" holds for `aspect_4_3` too:
  `save_settings` passes the player's menu choice of the run (`g_aspect_chosen`, -1 until a choice) to
  `civ64_cfg_set_aspect`, which leaves an existing `aspect_4_3` as it is for -1. For the other three the
  pre-existing gap stays.) `graphics` is written from the first accepted profile switch of a run on
  (chord or menu, through a separate profile hook), never from `--graphics`; a refused switch writes
  nothing. `zoom` is written when the player changes the level (chord, wheel or menu), never from `--zoom`
  (phase M). With nothing remembered: integer, nearest, windowed (the user's decision D7; F99), aspect
  4:3 (UX3, the user's direction of 2026-10-16 about 10:21; as from D3b1 to UX2, decision X3, F116; square under
  UX2, the user's D4-1 of 2026-10-16), with DOS Birth square (since UX5b from its first picture; `square_only`, 12.5;
  12.7 "As implemented (UX3; changed by UX5 and UX5b)"; until UX5 also the DOS intro and title box); the aspect acts on 320x200 frames only, so the Windows profile is unchanged either
  way; `civ64_start_aspect()`, `win64/civ64_start.c`: `--aspect`, else `aspect_4_3`, else 4:3) and the Windows
  profile.
* **System menu** (`host_win32.c` `add_menu`): the standard items without Close (F112: Close and the
  separator above it are deleted; until UX1 CS_NOCLOSE disabled the caption's close button; since UX1 the class
  has no CS_NOCLOSE and SC_CLOSE returns as the last item, below), then a separator and "Integer scaling"
  (mnemonic t, since UX3; until UX3 "Scale by whole multiples", whose mnemonic W "Windows graphics" shared), "Scale to fit" (radio; "Stretch to the window", 0x1030, removed in phase G), "Nearest-pixel filter", "Sharp filter"
  (radio, grayed under integer, which never filters), "4:3 aspect ratio for 320 x 200 screens" (check, mnemonic a;
  "4:3 aspect for 320 x 200 pictures" until UX3; it affects only 320x200 frames, which arrive with the DOS profile;
  checked by default again since UX3, unchecked under UX2; its choice calls the aspect hook, `hw_set_aspect_hook`,
  with the item's new state, then the settings hook), "Full screen  Alt+Enter" (check), then,
  since D3b2, a separator, "&Windows graphics\tCtrl+Shift+Backspace" (0x1090) and "&DOS graphics\tCtrl+Shift+Backspace"
  (0x10A0) as radio items marking the profile to come (the DOS item stays enabled; a refusal is the
  message, 12.5; since UX2 also from the start: `hw_start_profile(profile)` starts the run's profile through
  `present_start_profile`, the DOS one when requested and registered, else the Windows one, and then calls
  `hw_update_menu()`; civ64_main.c passes `civ64_start_graphics()`, `win64/civ64_start.c`: `--graphics` when given,
  else the remembered `graphics`, else Windows. Before UX2 the creation-time mark "Windows graphics" stayed until the
  player's first switch, the user's D3-9) and "&Locate DOS files..." (0x10B0; grayed in a `CIV_OBSERVER 0` build, which has no DOS
  profile), then, since M, a separator and the zoom items "Zoom &out (half)\tCtrl+Shift+Minus" (0x10C0),
  "&Normal map zoom\tCtrl+Shift+0" (0x10D0; since UX4b "&Normal map zoom\tCtrl+Shift+0, Ctrl+Shift+Home", in both
  branches of `add_menu`, F114) and "Zoom &in (double)\tCtrl+Shift+Plus" (0x10E0), radio
  items marking the level (`hw_update_menu`; "Zoom in" is grayed while +1 is refused; all three are grayed
  in a `CIV_OBSERVER 0` build); a zoom item requests its level with the centre anchor; then, since LP, the
  check item "&Mouse wheel zooms the map" (`SC_CIV_WHEEL`, 0x10F0; shown "Mouse wheel zooms the map", mnemonic M;
  checked by default; directly below "Zoom in (double)" and above the separator before "About..."; the only new
  item, no separator added around it; grayed and inert without the observer, as the zoom items, with the check
  kept); then a separator
  and "About..." (0x1080, the port's About box, F108; the last item until UX1), then, since UX1, a separator
  and the enabled SC_CLOSE as the **last item "&Exit Sid Meier's Civilization 64bit\tAlt+F4"**
  (`L"&Exit " HOST_CAPTIONW L"\tAlt+F4"`, `g_exit_item`; no other item of the menu uses E as its access key),
  the menu's default item (bold, `SetMenuDefaultItem(m, SC_CLOSE, FALSE)`), as the standard Close was; an
  enabled SC_CLOSE is what Windows enables the caption's close button by. The order at the end: ... "Zoom in
  (double)", "Mouse wheel zooms the map", separator, "About...", separator, "Exit Sid Meier's Civilization
  64bit  Alt+F4". Command ids
  0x1010..0x10F0 (multiples of 16 below 0xF000; audited in LP, read-only: the system's `SC_*` start at 0xF000, the
  emulation's `WM_SYSCOMMAND`s live in its own queue and never reach the host procedure, w16 never calls
  `GetSystemMenu`, so 0x10F0, the next multiple of 16 after 0x10E0, is free). `hw_update_menu` also runs on
  WM_INITMENU of the system menu; since UX2 at every WM_INITMENU, whatever menu handle it names (the window has no
  menu bar, so the window menu is the only menu it can name; Windows tracks it through its own wrapper menu, so the
  handle is not the popup `GetSystemMenu` returns, and the old handle comparison skipped the refresh: inference,
  `ext-UX2-record.md`), and at WM_INITMENUPOPUP of the window menu (the system-menu flag, HIWORD(lParam)), so every
  checkable or grayed item (scaling, filters, aspect, Full screen, the profile items, the zoom items, "Zoom in"
  grayed, the wheel item) is current whenever the menu opens; the zoom marks had the same start-up defect, and
  civ64_main.c also calls `hw_update_menu()` right after the start's remembered or command-line zoom request (UX2).
  **Mouse wheel item (LP, the user's request of 2026-10-11):** chosen, it flips
  `g_wheel_zoom`, updates the menu, drops the wheel's partial sum (`pi_arrive_wheel_clear()`, `present/input.h`,
  `present/arrive.c`: drops the half step so that no remainder of an earlier notch survives a switch) and calls the
  wheel hook (`hw_set_wheel_hook`) with the new state; `hw_set_wheel_zoom(int)` sets the start state without the hook
  and `hw_wheel_zoom()` reads it. With the item off, `WM_MOUSEWHEEL` returns 0 at once: no `pi_arrive_wheel`, so no
  zoom command and no event, and the wheel reaches the game in neither state (I8); with it on the path is unchanged
  (12.9). The chords (Ctrl+Shift+Plus/Minus/0), the three zoom items, `--zoom` and the harness `zoom` command do
  not look at the flag. The state is the settings key `wheel_zoom` (above). Host-only: nothing of the core, the
  emulation, the frames or the checkpoints changes (P0). The menu opens by mouse on the caption; Alt+Space and Alt
  stay the game's (SC_KEYMENU is refused, as before). The back end's `player_zoom` runs the chords' and the
  wheel's commands through `pmap_zoom_command`; a change calls the zoom hook (`hw_set_zoom_hook`), which
  writes the `zoom` setting; a refusal is silent.
  The system menu's modal loop runs inside the message pump, so the game thread waits while it is open,
  as it does while the window is moved or sized (review D22; recorded; input is taken at the game's polls
  as before).
  **Since HM** (2026-10-17, the user's directions of about 10:08 and 10:28 UTC, `verify/BRIEF-hm.md`; record
  [ext-HM-record.md](ext-HM-record.md); landed with the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 19:22:16 UTC,
  `build/hm-status.txt`; FIDELITY F258) the window menu has three submenus; the list above is its history. `add_menu`
  builds, after the system's Restore, Move, Size, Minimize and Maximize: a separator, "&Full screen\tAlt+Enter"
  (0x1070, check), the popups "&Display", "Map &zoom" and "&Graphics" (`CreatePopupMenu`, appended with `MF_POPUP` by
  `append_submenu`; a popup that cannot be appended is destroyed; the window menu owns them and Windows destroys them
  with it), a separator, "&About…" (0x1080), a separator and SC_CLOSE "&Close\tAlt+F4" (`g_exit_item`,
  `hw_exit_text(4)`; the default item; UX1's text replaced, its behaviour unchanged). Display: "&Integer scaling (whole
  steps)" (0x1010), "&Fit to window" (0x1020), a separator, "&Nearest-pixel filter" (0x1040), "&Sharp filter"
  (0x1050), a separator, "&Correct 320 × 200 to 4:3" (0x1060). Map zoom: "Zoom &out (half)\tCtrl+Shift+Minus"
  (0x10C0), "&Normal zoom\tCtrl+Shift+0, Home" (0x10D0; UX4b's "&Normal map zoom\tCtrl+Shift+0, Ctrl+Shift+Home"
  replaced by the user's correction), "Zoom &in (double)\tCtrl+Shift+Plus" (0x10E0), a separator, "&Mouse wheel zooms
  map" (0x10F0). Graphics: "&Windows graphics" (0x1090) and "&DOS graphics" (0x10A0) without accelerator text, a
  separator, "&Switch graphics\tCtrl+Shift+Backspace" (0x1100, the new `SC_CIV_SWITCH`:
  `player_profile(PRESENT_PROFILE_TOGGLE)`, the chord's very call), a separator and "&Locate DOS files…" (0x10B0) or
  "DOS files present" (disabled, no mnemonic). The texts are written with `…` and `×` escapes, so the
  sources stay ASCII. Mnemonics are unique within each menu: the top level f, d, z, g, a, c besides the system's own
  ("Map &zoom" because Move has m; the system items' letters follow the Windows display language); Display i, f, n, s,
  c; Map zoom o, n, i, m; Graphics w, d, s, l; UX3's t and a, LP's M and UX1's E leave with the old texts. 0x1100 is
  the next free multiple of 16 (LP's audit repeated: 0x1010..0x10F0 with 0x1030 retired, the system's ids from
  0xF000 up, the emulation's WM_SYSCOMMANDs never reach this window procedure, w16 never calls `GetSystemMenu`).
  Observed with HM's stand-alone probe, its menu loop on a private desktop (`ext-HM-record.md` Findings 1..4): a
  submenu's command reaches the window procedure as WM_SYSCOMMAND with its id, so `civ_command((unsigned)(w &
  0xFFF0u))` serves it (every id a multiple of 16); each submenu's opening sends WM_INITMENUPOPUP with the
  system-menu flag, so `hw_update_menu` also runs as each submenu opens; WM_INITMENU names a handle other than
  `GetSystemMenu`'s (UX2's wrapper-menu inference, now observed); by-command calls through `GetSystemMenu`'s handle
  reach the submenus' items, so `hw_update_menu` keeps them (radio marks for the scaling, filter, zoom and profile
  groups; check marks for the 4:3 item, the wheel item and Full screen). In observer builds `dos_item()` sets the
  Locate item's text and state from civ64's hook `hw_set_dos_present_hook` at each update, only when its grayed state
  differs from the answer (see "Chord, menu and Locate" in 12.5). The app's dark mode applies to the submenus as to
  the window menu (inferred; user check HM-8). The keyboard rules are unchanged: the menu opens by the mouse, and
  Alt, Alt+Space and F10 stay the game's (SC_KEYMENU refused).

  **Since WL, A43, CRT and AMS** (session E5, 2026-10-18/19; records [ext-WL-record.md](ext-WL-record.md),
  [ext-A43-record.md](ext-A43-record.md), [ext-CRT-record.md](ext-CRT-record.md),
  [ext-AMS-record.md](ext-AMS-record.md); FIDELITY F325, F326, F327, F329, F332) Display holds 12 entries:
  "&Windowless play" (0x1110, `SC_CIV_WINDOWLESS`, check; WL) and a separator first; HM's "&Integer scaling (whole
  steps)", "&Fit to window", a separator, "&Nearest-pixel filter" and "&Sharp filter"; a separator, "&CRT scanlines"
  (0x1130, `SC_CIV_CRT`, check; CRT), a separator; then "4:3 &aspect ratio for 320 x 200 DOS intro screens" (0x1060,
  HM's "Correct 320 × 200 to 4:3" renamed by A43, its scope unchanged) and "4:3 aspect ratio for 320 x 200 DOS
  &gameplay" (0x1120, `SC_CIV_ASPECT_PLAY`, check; A43). The top level gains "A&lternative music/soundfx" (0x1140,
  `SC_CIV_ALT_SOUNDS`, check; AMS) between Graphics and the separator above About… (15 items). Display's mnemonics are
  w, i, f, n, s, c, a, g; the top level's f, d, z, g, l, a, c. Each id is the next free multiple of 16 (the id audits
  of WL, A43, CRT and AMS: 0x1010..0x1140 in use, 0x1030 retired). Each new item calls its own hook
  (`hw_set_windowless_hook`, `hw_set_aspect_play_hook`, the CRT and sound hooks), then the settings hook; civ64 writes
  its key (`windowless`, `aspect_play_4_3`, `crt_scanlines`, `alt_sounds`) only after the player's choice. Without the
  observer (`CIV_OBSERVER 0`) Windowless play and the gameplay 4:3 item are grayed; "Alternative music/soundfx" is
  grayed without a valid MusicSfx source. The sections 12.12..12.15 below describe the four features.
* **Full screen**: WS_POPUP (the WS_OVERLAPPEDWINDOW bits removed) over `rcMonitor` of
  `MonitorFromWindow(MONITOR_DEFAULTTONEAREST)`, `HWND_NOTOPMOST`, the cursor not clipped; leaving restores
  the style and the `GetWindowPlacement` record (maximized stays maximized; a hidden window stays hidden).
  `WM_GETMINMAXINFO` does not constrain a full-screen window; `WM_DPICHANGED` in full screen covers the
  monitor again.
* **Placement** (F111, the user's direction of 2026-10-11): the settings hook also runs on
  WM_EXITSIZEMOVE, on a change between maximized and not (WM_SIZE, visible window) and in `host_exit`;
  civ64 then writes `hw_placement` (GetWindowPlacement's normal rectangle and the maximized state; in
  full screen the placement it returns to) as `window` and `maximized`. At the next start
  `remembered_placement` gives it to `hw_set_placement`; `hw_create_window_ex` takes it unless
  `--window` or `--scale k` gives the first size and only when `MonitorFromRect(MONITOR_DEFAULTTONULL)`
  finds a monitor: SetWindowPlacement with SW_HIDE before the first show, then SW_SHOWMAXIMIZED or
  SW_SHOW (full screen: its own setting, leaving it restores the remembered state). *(LP, below, supersedes the
  start-up part of this bullet: the rectangle is in screen coordinates, the "meets a monitor" test is the
  positive-area overlap rule, and the window is placed with `SetWindowPos`, not `SetWindowPlacement`. The
  remembering moments and the keys are unchanged.)*
* **Start-up placement** (LP, F111 as revised; the user's requests of 2026-10-11 22:15 and 22:20: first start
  centred, remembered afterwards, "entirely off-screen" returns to the top-left of the primary screen). "The
  screen" is the primary monitor's work area (`rcWork`), so a top or left taskbar is never covered; "top left" is
  the work area's top-left corner; the display check is the user's rule, **entirely off-screen** (no overlap
  with any monitor rectangle) moves the window, while a window only partly off-screen or straddling two monitors
  keeps its position (the coordinator's first draft, "any part outside", was replaced by this at 22:20). The pure
  decision is `hw_place_decide(mons, nmon, work, have, want, def_w, def_h, &out)` (`host_win32.h`, `HwPlaceRect`,
  screen coordinates) and returns `HW_PLACE_CENTRED` (nothing remembered, or `--window`/`--scale` gave the size:
  the default outer size centred horizontally and vertically in the work area, never above or left of it),
  `HW_PLACE_KEPT` (the remembered rectangle meets some monitor with a positive-area overlap: position and size
  kept) or `HW_PLACE_TOPLEFT` (it meets no monitor at all, no monitor known included: the work area's top-left
  corner, the remembered size if it fits the work area, else the default size clamped to it). `hw_create_window_ex`
  creates the window at the primary work area's top-left (so on the primary monitor, at its DPI; no
  `CW_USEDEFAULT`), takes the default outer size with `AdjustWindowRectExForDpi` at that DPI, gets the monitor
  rectangles from `EnumDisplayMonitors` and applies the decision with `SetWindowPos` before the window is shown.
  The maximized state is applied as before: it also applies when the rectangle went to the corner (the window then
  maximizes on the primary monitor), while a remembered rectangle on an on-screen secondary monitor, maximized,
  maximizes on that monitor. **Remembering** keeps its mechanism (WM_EXITSIZEMOVE, maximize/restore, the game's
  end; keys `window` and `maximized`), but the rectangle is now in **screen coordinates**: `hw_placement` converts
  `WINDOWPLACEMENT`'s workspace coordinates by the primary monitor's work-area offset (`ws_offset`). A `window`
  value written by an earlier version under a top or left taskbar is read as screen coordinates once (shifted by
  the taskbar's size, then rewritten; the display check protects it). `fit_scale` (the default size) now follows
  the primary monitor's work area, as the centring does (it used the monitor under the cursor).
  *(UX4, the user's LP-8 result of 2026-10-16; F111 as extended: the decision is now
  `hw_place_decide(areas, narea, work, have, want, def_w, def_h, fit, &out)`, with the monitors' **work areas**
  (`mon_cb`) in place of their rectangles and `fit` (`HwPlaceFit`: the largest window, `WM_GETMINMAXINFO`'s
  `ptMaxTrackSize` recorded at the window's creation, else `SM_CXMAXTRACK`/`SM_CYMAXTRACK` at its DPI, and the title
  bar strip's height from `AdjustWindowRectExForDpi` at the window's DPI, 31 pixels at 100 %). A remembered
  rectangle is `HW_PLACE_KEPT` only when its size is positive and not larger than the largest window and
  `hw_place_usable` finds a usable part of its title bar strip in one work area (at least half the strip's height and
  at least twice its height in width, or the whole strip when the window is narrower); partly off-screen windows and
  windows straddling two monitors are kept as before when their title bar is usable. Else `hw_place_corner`: the
  primary work area's top-left corner, LP's size rule unchanged. A NULL `fit` gives LP's any-overlap rule. After the
  move `hw_create_window_ex` reads `GetWindowRect` and checks the strip again at the window's DPI; if it is not
  usable the window goes to the corner by the same rule (`HW_PLACE_MOVED`, a safety net that no test reaches on a
  real window); `hw_place_last` reports the outcome. What is written back at exit is unchanged (`hw_placement`, the
  window's real rectangle), so an unusable value repairs itself at the first exit. The key's format is
  `window=left,top,right,bottom`: the outer rectangle in screen pixels, the primary screen's top-left 0,0, right
  and bottom exclusive.)*
* **Titles, captions, licence note and version** (LP, the user's requests of 2026-10-11 20:15, 22:00, 22:05 and

  `CreateWindowExW`) is `HOST_CAPTIONW` (it was "Sid Meier's Civilization", and "Civilization" before that). The
  About box's title (`g_about_title`, read through `hw_about_title()`) is `L"About " HOST_CAPTIONW L" v1.0"` =

  the 20:15 request is superseded** (replaced by the user at 22:00) and is not in the product. The licence note
  (`g_license`, read through `hw_about_license()`) now closes "The licence covers this port's own code (Sid Meier's
  Civilization 64bit). Sid Meier's Civilization, its program and its data belong to their owners; ..."; the MIT
  body, the copyright line and the second sentence (it names the original game) are unchanged. The accessors
  exist so that the quiet-mode tests (the box does not open) can pin the strings. **Captions:** every port-level
  message box takes `HOST_CAPTION`/`HOST_CAPTIONW`: `platform/win32/host_win32.c` (the `host_message` calls "Out of
  host memory." and the host fault message), `present/present.c` (the two `host_message` calls of the profile

  Windows files not found, DOS files not found, "Ask again ..."): four files, nine call sites. Left unchanged
  (judged out of scope): the game's own dialogs and everything inside the emulation (`w16_MessageBox`, the

  `FILEVERSION` and `PRODUCTVERSION` 1,0,0,0, `FILEOS VOS_NT_WINDOWS32`, `FILETYPE VFT_APP`; block `040904B0`
  (English US, Unicode) with `FileVersion` "1.0", `ProductVersion` "1.0", `ProductName` "Sid Meier's Civilization
  64bit", `FileDescription` "Sid Meier's Civilization 64bit (civ64)" (the "64bit" here is an interpretation: the
  one string that would otherwise still say x64), `InternalName` "civ64", `OriginalFilename` "civ64.exe" and
  `LegalCopyright` "Copyright (c) 2026 Onur BUYUKCAGLAR" (the About box's own line); civ64's own names only, no
