* **Colour preference** (F109, the user's direction of 2026-10-11): `theme_apply` reads
  `AppsUseLightTheme` (HKCU ...\Themes\Personalize; absent: light) before the window is created and on
  WM_SETTINGCHANGE "ImmersiveColorSet": uxtheme's ordinal 135 (SetPreferredAppMode) ForceDark or
  ForceLight, 104 and 136 refresh the menus (build 18362 on, all four ordinals present), 133
  (AllowDarkModeForWindow) and DWMWA_USE_IMMERSIVE_DARK_MODE (20, 19 before 18985) set the title bar,
  SWP_FRAMECHANGED repaints it. The About box follows on WM_INITDIALOG and the same message: dark
  WM_CTLCOLORDLG/STATIC colours, DarkMode_Explorer for the licence's scroll bar, an owner-drawn OK
  button (BS_OWNERDRAW; Enter still chooses IDOK, the dialog manager's default). `hw_set_theme` forces
  a mode for tests.
* **DPI**: `WM_DPICHANGED` takes the suggested rectangle; the new client size arrives as WM_SIZE, which
  recomputes the layout (the factors) and remakes the cursor when its factors changed.
* **Cursor** (5.7): the game's cursor resource (Windows 3.x: hotspot, BITMAPINFOHEADER, 1-bit XOR and AND
  bitmaps) is scaled by kx x ky by nearest neighbour, kx and ky the whole numbers nearest to the image's
  factors SW/W and SH/H (at least 1, at most 16; halves round up), its hotspot multiplied the same way, and
  created at exactly that size (`CreateIconFromResourceEx`, no system stretching). Another kind of cursor
  resource falls back to the system's scaling at 32kx x 32ky. Remade when the factors change. Since UX1 this
  rule is `present_cursor_factors_of(layout)` (`present/scale.c`, moved unchanged from `host_win32.c`).
  **A profile's own pointer** (UX1, the user's direction of 2026-10-15; FIDELITY F159): `pdos_cursor` gives
  the shape's own pixels on both frames (one argb pixel per DOS pixel; the A frame's 2x is gone), and the back
  end makes it at `present_pointer_factors(&kx, &ky)` (`present/present.c`) = `present_pointer_factors_of(V,
  W)` (`scale.c`), V the visible frame's current layout, W the layout of the Windows frame at its current size
  (640x480, or the game screen of F105/F110) in the same client with the same policy, filter and aspect:
  kx = clamp(1, 16, (2·W.sw + W.w) div (2·W.w)), the Windows game cursor's horizontal factor; ky = clamp(1,
  16, nearest(kyW · a)), kyW = (2·W.sh + W.h) div (2·W.h) the Windows cursor's vertical factor and a = (V.sh ·
  V.w) / (V.h · V.sw) the visible image's pixel aspect (num = kyW · V.sh · V.w, den = V.h · V.sw, q = num div
  den, q + 1 when 2·(num − q·den) > den or, at an exact half, when q < kyW). a is 1 except for a 320x200
  frame at 4:3 (1.17..1.25), so on square pixels the pointer has exactly the Windows cursor's factors. One DOS
  pointer pixel is a block of whole host pixels, never filtered; the hot spot is multiplied by the same factors
  (`pixel_cursor`). The game's own cursor (the Windows profile, the DOS profile's scroll cursors 136..143)
  keeps the visible layout's cursor factors. The factors are computed again on WM_SIZE (resize, maximize,
  full screen, WM_DPICHANGED), an option change (`hw_set_presentation`), a frame-size change (N and A) and a
  profile switch; `hw_cursor_factors` reports the factors of the cursor shown. Mouse mapping is unchanged.
  **Since UX1b** (the user's correction of UX1-1, 2026-10-15 about 23:15 UTC: "the DOS torch pointer is too small
  and shall be on the size between the previous and the current one"; [ext-UX1-record.md](ext-UX1-record.md)
  "UX1b"; FIDELITY F159) `present_pointer_factors_of(V, W)` returns, per axis, k = clamp(1, 16, (k_before +
  k_UX1 + 1) div 2): the mean of the factor before UX1 and UX1's factor above, a half rounded up. k_before is,
  on a frame of exactly 320x200 (the N frame), that frame's own cursor factors `present_cursor_factors_of(V)`,
  and on any other frame (the A frame) 2 · `present_cursor_factors_of(W)`, the old 2x image at the Windows
  layout's factors. So on square pixels the pointer is no longer exactly the Windows cursor's factors: it lies
  between its size before UX1 and UX1's, never smaller than UX1's and never larger than before UX1, and where
  the two differ by 1 it has its size from before UX1 (2x2 at 640x480; on the A frames under integer up to
  1366x768). At civ64's defaults with the 4:3 aspect (the default again since UX3, square under UX2; DOS Birth is square, and until UX5 the DOS intro and title box were): 320x200 frames 2x2 up to 1366x768, 3x4 at 1920x1080, 5x5 at 2560x1440, 6x8 at
  3840x2160; A frames 2x2, 3x3, 5x5, 6x6. The block rule, the hot spot, the game's own cursor and the moments of
  recomputation are UX1's; `present/present.c` and `host_win32.c` are unchanged (their comments still name
  the Windows cursor's size, record deviation 4). Tested: `test_present` 925 checks (UX1b's goldens
  `g_ptr_golden`), `w32_test_host_win32` 279.
* **Mouse** (5.7): moves outside the image clamp to its nearest pixel; a press outside the image is not
  delivered, nor its release (the capture is taken so that the release is seen), and the button is left
  out of the button state of moves meanwhile; a release outside after a press inside is delivered at the
  clamped point (FIDELITY F100). Since D3b2 these rules are `pi_arrive_mouse` in `present/arrive.c`
  (12.5), called by the Win32 back end.
* **Chords** (5.6 I7, 5.11). Audit for S2: the translated core handles no WM_SYSKEYDOWN or WM_SYSCHAR
  (the constant 0x104 occurs in the core only as a buffer size: 1028:12D5, 1128:09C2, 10F8:0DC1);
  `GetKeyboardState` is read only to save and restore Num Lock (1008:033D, 1008:02E6); ACCELERATOR 128 has
  no ALT entry (24 entries: plain D H J K N P, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+0..9, Ctrl+D P F L Q S T); Alt+Enter in the
  emulated USER would only be a menu key without a mnemonic. So Alt+Enter is free; it is the only chord of
  S2 (full screen). Ctrl+Shift+Backspace (profile) was audited again in D3b2 ([ext-D3b2-record.md](ext-D3b2-record.md),
  "The chord audit"): ACCELERATOR 128 has no VK_BACK entry and none with both Ctrl and Shift, the one menu names
  no Backspace or Ctrl+Shift, and no key handler of the core tests VK_BACK, so the game loses no function. The
  zoom chords were audited in M ([ext-Ma-record.md](ext-Ma-record.md), "The chord audit"): ACCELERATOR 128
  has 24 entries and none with Shift, and w16's `TranslateAccelerator` matches the Shift, Ctrl and Alt flags
  exactly, so Ctrl+0 (World Map) needs Shift up and Ctrl+Shift+0 would not match it even if delivered; MENU
  128's shortcut texts use no Shift; no core handler reads `GetKeyState`. The chords are Ctrl+Shift with
  `VK_OEM_PLUS`/`VK_ADD` (zoom in), `VK_OEM_MINUS`/`VK_SUBTRACT` (zoom out) and `'0'`/`VK_NUMPAD0` (reset),
  taken by `present/arrive.c` exactly as Ctrl+Shift+Backspace is (the hold rule; only a press without the
  repeat bit gives the command; the chord's presses, repeats and releases and the held modifiers' releases
  never reach the game). The plain keys `=`, `-`, `0` and the keypad keys reach the game unchanged. Under
  `CIV_OBSERVER 0` these keys are not chords (they reach the game). With Num Lock on, Shift turns keypad 0
  into `VK_INSERT` on usual layouts, so the keypad reset may not arrive (the main-row 0 does). *(UX4, 2026-10-16,
  F114: the keypad's 0 makes the reset as `VK_INSERT` with scan 0x52, not extended, and Windows' own Shift release
  before it is held back and dropped for this chord only (12.5, "Arrival and filter stages"); the gray Insert key
  (0x152) stays the game's. The main row's Ctrl+Shift+0 does not reach the window on the user's PC; the user's
  extra chord Ctrl+Shift+Home is unit UX4b.)* *(UX4b, 2026-10-16, F114, F188: Ctrl+Shift+Home is one more reset chord,
  `VK_HOME` whatever its scan code (the gray Home 0x147 and the keypad's 7 with Num Lock off 0x47), taken exactly as
  Ctrl+Shift+0 (12.5, "Arrival and filter stages"); the keypad's 7 with Num Lock on is the digit 7 and no chord. Its
  audit ([ext-UX4b-record.md](ext-UX4b-record.md) Finding 2): no civ64 chord uses Home, ACCELERATOR 128 has no Shift
  entry and the machine's IME hot keys do not use Home. Recorded deviation (F188): in the Windows graphics' name
  boxes Ctrl+Shift+Home no longer selects to the start of the text (Windows 3.1's EDIT takes it as Shift+Home), and the
  list boxes and menus no longer receive it; Shift+Home and Home do as before.)* The wheel
  over the map client zooms (12.9).
  **The Alt rule (choice):** an Alt press is held back until the next key event. Alt+Enter: neither the
  Alt nor the Enter press, its repeats, nor the Enter and Alt releases reach the game. Another key while
  Alt is down (also after a chord): the Alt press is delivered first, then the key, as Windows would have
  sent them. Alt released alone: its press and release are delivered together (the game's menu-bar key
  keeps working). A mouse press delivers a held Alt first. Focus loss drops a held Alt (it was never
  delivered). The game therefore sees an Alt press late, at the next key; `GetKeyState(VK_MENU)` in
  between reads up (FIDELITY F101). Since D3b2 the Alt rule lives in `present/arrive.c` (`pi_arrive_key`,
  12.5) together with the Ctrl and Shift rule of decision K3 (F114): a Ctrl or Shift press is held back like
  Alt while Alt is up, and Ctrl+Shift+Backspace (Backspace while Ctrl and Shift are down and Alt is not) is the
  profile command; the held presses, the Backspace press, its repeats and release and the held keys' later
  releases never reach the game, while Ctrl+Backspace alone still does.
  **Alt+F4 (UX1, the user's direction of 2026-10-15)** is a host chord of the arrival stage (`present/arrive.c`,
  `PI_VK_F4`, `PI_CMD_CLOSE` after `PI_CMD_ZOOM_RESET` in `present/input.h`), taken exactly as Alt+Enter: only a
  press with the host's Alt (the WM_SYSKEYDOWN context bit) and without the repeat bit gives the command; the
  held-back Alt it serves and F4's press, repeats and release never reach the emulated queue, so the emulated key
  state stays as if nothing was pressed; an F4 already delivered down (F4 held, then Alt) makes no chord, so its
  release still reaches the game; F4 without Alt reaches the game. The back end's `arrived` calls the exit path
  ("Exit", below). The audit, updated: before UX1 Alt+F4 in the host window reached the game as keys
  (WM_SYSKEYDOWN VK_MENU, WM_SYSKEYDOWN VK_F4 with the context bit); the emulated DefWindowProc
  (`platform/w16/user_syscmd.c`, P4-5, FIDELITY F78) posted WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_CLOSE to the window with the focus,
  whose default sends WM_CLOSE: on the map window that reaches the game's Quit command (1208:05E1, F82), and in
  the game's own message boxes and dialogs WM_CLOSE ends them (5 "MessageBox"). The host window's close request
  (only WM_CLOSE from elsewhere while F112 held) posted the same SC_CLOSE (F82). The translated core handles no
  WM_SYSKEYDOWN of its own and ACCELERATOR 128 has no Alt entry and no F4 (above). So the game loses exactly the
  Windows behaviour the host now takes over: Alt+F4 as "close the active window", which on the map window was Quit
  with the game's own question and in a game message box was its Close; the boxes keep their buttons and Escape,
  the game keeps File > Quit.
* **Exit** (UX1, the user's direction of 2026-10-15 about 14:03 UTC; FIDELITY F160, F112 reversed for the close
  button and the Close command). SC_CLOSE (the close button, the Exit item, a double click on the window icon),
  Alt+F4 and WM_CLOSE from elsewhere (Task Manager's End task, a window message; before UX1 it became
  HOST_EV_QUIT) all call `exit_request`, which asks the pure `hw_exit_decide(state, request)` (`host_win32.h`;
  requests `HW_QUIT_SYSCOMMAND`, `HW_QUIT_ALT_F4`, `HW_QUIT_WM_CLOSE`): in `HW_IN_SETUP` (intro, title, Birth,
  setup screens) exit now; in `HW_IN_GAME` (from the game window's opening) ask; in `HW_IN_HOST_BOX` (one of the
  back end's modal boxes open: the About box, the warning itself, a host message, the file choice, the overwrite
  question or "Locate DOS files...", a nested count `g_host_box`) ignore, so a WM_CLOSE from elsewhere never opens
  a second box over one of them; an unknown state or request is ignored. **Exit now** is `host_exit(0)`, the
  game's own end: F111's settings and placement written through the settings hook (civ64.cfg with `window=` and
  `maximized=`), the debug log, the files closed, `ExitProcess(0)` from inside the window procedure, so no more
  game code runs; never a save. **"In the game"** comes from the existing G hook (choice): w16 calls
  `host_game_screen` once per run, as WdwMapOpen (1208:0AF1) creates the game's map window (F105; F110's following
  starts at the same moment); the back end sets `g_in_game`, which lasts until civ64 exits (reset only when a host
  window is created or destroyed: a new run in the tests). It works the same in a `CIV_OBSERVER 0` build and covers
  new, loaded, EARTH and customized games alike. **The warning** is a modal box over the host window (class W, like
  the About box: outside the virtual screen and the observer; the game waits while it is open): caption
  `HOST_CAPTIONW` (F106), the system's warning icon and "A game is in progress. If you exit now, any unsaved
  progress will be lost."; buttons "Exit Anyway" and "Cancel" (`IDC_EXIT_ICON/TEXT/ANYWAY` 211..213). Cancel has
  the focus at the start and is the default (Enter); Escape, the box's own close button and Alt+F4 on the box answer
  Cancel; the default follows the focus (BN_SETFOCUS of the owner-drawn buttons sets DM_SETDEFID). The colour
  preference (F109) as the About box: `theme_window`, dark background and text, the owner-drawn face `box_button`
  (the About box's `about_button` made common; in light mode the system's themed push button, PBS_DEFAULTED only on
  the default one); a change while it is open applies at once. Exit Anyway exits now; Cancel returns to the game
  with nothing changed in the emulation or the core input; the host window loses the keyboard focus while the box
  is open, so the focus-loss rule (S2, F101) applies, and Alt+F4's own Alt and F4 are never delivered. Quiet mode
  (the tests) never shows it: `hw_set_exit_answer` sets the answer (Cancel unless set), `hw_exit_asks` counts the
  questions, `hw_exit_state` reads the state and `hw_exit_text` the texts. HOST_EV_QUIT and `pi_arrive_quit` remain
  for WM_QUIT in the pump (6 "Close"); the host window no longer produces them.
* **Single instance** (SI, the user's direction of 2026-10-16 about 19:44 UTC, "Host application shall only be single
  instance"; the orchestrator's ruling and its decision on the record's Decision 9, follow the original; FIDELITY F189;
  record [ext-SI-record.md](ext-SI-record.md)). `win64/civ64_main.c` calls `civ64_single_instance` (`win64/civ64_start.c`:
  the decision over the operations `Civ64SiOps`, the Win32 ones `civ64_si_win32`) right after `civ64_parse_args`,
  before civ64.cfg is read, the game's files are looked for, a ZIP is extracted or the window is made;
  `CIV64_SI_EXIT` ends the process with code 0 (`ExitProcess(0)`). **The lock:** the named mutex
  `Local\civ64.single-instance.{9C2D6E41-7A35-4F08-B1E6-2D84C0F3A957}` (`Local\`: the logon session), owned without
  waiting (`WAIT_ABANDONED` counts as owned) and kept by the first civ64 to the end of its process (`g_si`,
  file-static). **The holder's published block:** the named shared block `...owner` (120 bytes) with its process id
  and creation time (a process id reused after an ended holder is never taken for it) and, right after `ne_load`, the
  game's refusal text and caption read from its loaded DGROUP (`civ64_si_game_texts`: DS:0056, 31 characters, and
  DS:0076, 18 characters, by offset, so the port's sources hold no text of the original; WinMain 1008:0077 `push
  0056h`, 1008:007B `push 0076h`; `civ64_si_publish_texts`), each part with its own "written" mark, so a reader never
  takes half-written values. Windows releases the mutex and the block when the holder's process ends in any way.
  **A later start:** the same program (`QueryFullProcessImageNameW` against `GetModuleFileNameW`: equal ignoring
  case, or the same volume and file index) shows the original's box with the published text and caption, `MB_OK` with
  `MB_ICONHAND`, through `hw_message_box` (owned by no window; the colour preference as every host message); another
  program file shows civ64's own box in its place (caption "Sid Meier's Civilization 64bit", the information icon,
  `civ64_si_text` naming the running program's folder; one box, never two); when the running program's path cannot
  be read, the original's box. After OK: `ShowWindowAsync(SW_RESTORE)` (a minimized civ64 returns to its previous
  state, maximized or full screen included), then `SetForegroundWindow` on the last active popup of the running
  civ64's frame, the one `Civ64Host` window of that process id (before the frame exists another visible top-level
  window of that process), and the start ends with code 0. While the holder has not published itself or its texts,
  or has no visible window, the start looks again every 100 ms for up to 5 s (`CIV64_SI_WAIT_MS` 5000,
  `CIV64_SI_STEP_MS` 100); without the texts by then (a holder at its start-up questions) no box, only the
  activation. Each step, and the step after OK, tries the lock first, so a civ64 that ended meanwhile lets this start
  run. The second start's command line is ignored, even a wrong one; a first start with a wrong command line still
  shows the usage box and exits with code 2. No lock possible (CreateMutex fails): the start runs. `--multi-instance`
  neither takes nor reads the lock (`tests/win32/drive_civ64.py` passes it). The core's own check (WinMain 1008:0070,
  `hPrevInstance`) stays as translated and never fires: the emulated InitTask gives `hPrevInstance` 0
  (`platform/w16/kernel_task.c`). Known limits: a civ64 started as another Windows user in the same session, or a
  plain start while a civ64 runs elevated, may be refused access to the other's mutex and then runs as a second
  civ64; each logon session has its own `Local\`.
  Tests: `w32_test_single` (50 checks: the decision's cases, the switch, the text, the original's refusal from
  civ.exe, the processes).
* **--dump-presented**: each core present also writes the presented logical frame as `presented-NNNNN.bmp`
  (24-bit, its own size, bottom-up rows), numbered as `--dump-frames` numbers the core frames. Since D3b1
  this holds while the visible profile shows the core frame; otherwise `tick-NNNNN.bmp` is written at each
  tick or view show (8-bit when the frame is 8-bit, else 24-bit).
* **Tests** (7.2, `tests/win32/test_host_win32.c` `test_window_s2`): style and maximize box, the minimum
  640x480 client, full screen covering the hidden window's monitor and restoring style and placement,
  Alt+Enter (repeat, releases, Alt+F after it, Alt alone, focus loss with a held Alt), the system-menu
  commands and their marks, presses outside the image, cursor factors and hotspots (2x2, 1x1 after a
  resize, 2x2 under fit at 1600x900 (1.875; it was 3x2 under stretch before phase G)), WM_DPICHANGED, `--dump-presented`; the About item and the colour preference (forced, the system's, the change message); option parsing in `test_start`; `test_placement` (taken, not with a first size, not off every monitor, full screen's maximized return, the hook at the end of a move). UX1b (`ext-UX1-record.md` "Tests (UX1b)") changes `test_present` to 925 checks (UX1b's goldens and properties; UX1's 72 checks "at most the 320x200 frame's own factors" are now the bound "never larger than before UX1") and the pointer expectations of `test_ux1_pointer` (3x3 over the 320x200 frame at 4x in 1280x960, 2x2 at 700x500, 3x3 over the 640x480 frame at 2x) and of `test_window`'s pixel cursor (3x3, 2x2, 3x3); `w32_test_host_win32` keeps 279 checks. The existing D3b2 checks are unchanged. UX4 adds `test_ux4_keys` and `test_ux4_box` (the keypad chord, the placement check and `hw_message_box`; `w32_test_host_win32` 387 checks in Checked, 365 in CheckedNoObs), UX4b `test_ux4b_home` (Ctrl+Shift+Home through the window procedure and the zoom item's text with it; 395 checks in Checked and Release, 366 in CheckedNoObs) and TLD `test_tld_view` (FIDELITY F192: with a pixel of the virtual screen pending and no present, WM_INITMENU, WM_ENTERSIZEMOVE, WM_CLOSE in the game (Cancel) and the profile item each show the pending pixel first, with no present and one view each; 410 checks in Checked and Release, 381 in CheckedNoObs), as RUNNING.md's `w32_test_host_win32` row records them. ### 12.3 Where the game's files come from (phase R)

The user's requirements of 2026-10-10 (STATUS.md "Extension track"; decisions D1, D8, D9 of
`ext-X0-acceptance.md`); `win64/civ64_locate.[ch]`, `res/zip.[ch]`.

* **Order, Windows version (required)**: `--data` (unchanged meaning, checked by `civ64_open_data`);
  `CivWin.zip`, `Civ-Win.zip`, `Civ_Win.zip` next to civ64.exe; a `CivWin` subfolder; the program's folder
  when it holds `civ.exe` (step 4a, the user's decision); `windows_data` of civ64.cfg; the player's choice.
  **DOS version (optional)**: `--dos-data` (`none`: skipped); `CivDos.zip`, `Civ-Dos.zip`, `Civ_Dos.zip`;
  a `CivDos` subfolder; `dos_data`; the player's choice unless `dos_ask=never`.
* **Names** are compared ignoring ASCII case over an enumeration of the program's folder
  (`FindFirstFileExW`); of several matches in a case-sensitive folder the ordinal smallest is taken.
* **Validity**: a candidate is valid when it holds the pinned `civ.exe` (832512 bytes, SHA-256 of
  `sha256.h`) or, for DOS, the `CIV.EXE` of `docs/dos/dos-hashes.txt` (305030 bytes, 22de2748...), in any
  folder of a ZIP (exactly one such entry). A present but invalid candidate (ZIP or folder) stops civ64 with
  a message naming it (exit code 2); a remembered one that is gone or invalid is skipped; a chosen one is
  refused with a message and asked again, and never remembered.
* **ZIP reader** (`res/zip.c`, portable, no third-party code): the end record found exactly at the end of
  the file (comment included), single disk, no ZIP64, methods 0 and 8 only, no encryption, no symbolic
  links, safe relative names only ('\' turned '/'; no drive or ':', no "." or ".." or empty component, no
  component ending in '.' or ' ', no DOS device name, no control character), no two names equal ignoring
  ASCII case, local headers agreeing with the central directory, no overlapping data, at most 4096 entries,
  64 MiB per entry and 512 MiB in all. Inflate after RFC 1951 (canonical codes from code lengths; an
  incomplete code only for a single one-bit code; distances up to 32768 within the output; length and
  distance codes checked; the exact declared size); CRC-32 checked on every extraction. Tests:
  `tests/test_zip.c` (zlib-made streams of `tests/zip_vectors.h`, hand-built fixed and stored streams, one
  changed field per refused archive, bit-flip fuzzing under ASan).
* **Windows ZIP**: every file of the folder that holds `civ.exe` in the archive is extracted and checked in
  memory before anything is written, into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\civ64\CivWin` (the data directory, where saves
  and civ.prf go): the first time into `CivWin.tmp-<pid>`, renamed into place when complete. The manifest
  `%LOCALAPPDATA%\civ64\CivWin.manifest` (outside the data directory, so the game never lists it) records
  the ZIP's SHA-256 and each file's SHA-256. The same ZIP again: nothing is written. A changed ZIP: a
  missing file is written; a file unchanged since the last extraction is replaced (written beside, then
  moved over); a file the player changed (or of unknown origin) is kept; files the ZIP does not hold
  (saves) are left alone. Entries outside civ.exe's folder are not used.
* **DOS ZIP**: read into memory and checked; kept for the DOS profile's phases, never extracted.
* **Asking**: a message box, then the Windows file dialog (IFileOpenDialog: civ.exe or a ZIP file). The
  dialog keeps its own most-recently-used records in the user's registry (user decision D1, FIDELITY
  F102). For DOS: "Locate it now?" Yes/No; No asks "Ask again the next time civ64 starts?", and No to that
  writes `dos_ask=never`.
* **Tests** (`tests/win32/test_locate.c`, quiet, injected program folder and per-user folder under the
  test output): civ64.cfg round trip; nothing found; a ZIP in another case extracted (entries outside
  ignored), a second start writing nothing, a changed ZIP refreshing (the player's change and a save
  kept); CivWin.zip before Civ-Win.zip; a damaged candidate named; the folder; a wrong civ.exe; the
  program's folder; remembered and gone; `--data`; DOS: none, a ZIP, a wrong CIV.EXE, the folder,
  `--dos-data`.

### 12.4 The game screen (phase G)

The user's requirement of 2026-10-10 about 21:00 UTC (STATUS.md, user direction): the startup, credits
and intro, title menu, Birth scenes and setup screens keep the original's 4:3; the game itself uses
the whole client; the virtual desktop takes the host client's size at the beginning of each game;
`stretch` is then removed. FIDELITY F105; gate record `ext-G-acceptance.md`.

* **Size.** The virtual screen is `w16_screen_w` x `w16_screen_h` (w16_int.h; `W16_SCREEN_W`,
  `W16_SCREEN_H` read them), 640x480 (`W16_SCREEN_BASE_W/H`) after `w16_reset`. The buffer holds
  `W16_SCREEN_MAX_W` x `W16_SCREEN_MAX_H` = 2726 x 1600 bytes; rows are `w16_screen_w` bytes apart.
  2726 = 166 + 2560 and 1600: WdwMapOpen caps its default map window at 0xA00 x 0x640 (1208:0C1A), the
  whole 80 x 50 world at 32 pixels, beside the 166-pixel column.
* **Desktop colour** (F107): civ64 sets `w16_desktop_rgb` to black before `w16_reset`; every desktop
  fill (reset, uncovered desktop, a new screen area) uses it. The harness keeps F24's grey unless
  `--desktop black`.
* **When.** One switch per run, at the first `CreateWindow` of a top-level window of class "CIV": the
  only one is WdwMapOpen's (1208:0B4E), called once by WinMain (1008:020B) after RunIntroAndTitleMenu
  (1008:01A8) returned, i.e. after the title, intro, Birth and setup screens, for a new, loaded, EARTH
  or customized game alike (the original plays one game per run). w16 asks the host
  (`host_game_screen`, host.h); a size other than the screen's is clamped to 640..2726 x 480..1600 and
  taken (`w16_set_screen_size`): the 640x480 rows move to the new stride (`w16_screen_resize`), the
  desktop window's rectangle becomes the screen, the uncovered desktop is painted and the whole screen
  is presented again; the harness logs an event `screen W H`. No message reaches the game (Windows 3.1
  has no display-change message); it reads the desktop through GetDesktopRect (1098:0BAC) when it
  places its windows (WdwMapOpen, WdwSmMapOpen, WdwStatusOpen) and centres ports and dialogs
  (CenterWindowPortOnScreen 1098:0B33, MoveWindowPortTo 1098:0A0D). SM_CXSCREEN, SM_CYSCREEN,
  SM_CXFULLSCREEN, SM_CYFULLSCREEN (height - 20), HORZRES and VERTRES (the game reads neither: 1008:0EB3
  stores them unused), WM_GETMINMAXINFO's maximum, the drag limits, menus, message boxes and F96 follow
  the screen. At the switch only window 0x2002 (dialog 0x94, the title backdrop) has existed and it is
  already destroyed in the headless runs (`events.log`), so no window extends past the old screen.
* **The host's size.** `present_game_screen`: the client's size in logical pixels of the current
  layout of a 640x480 frame, floor(CW * 640 / SW) x floor(CH * 480 / SH) (integer: floor(CW / k) x
  floor(CH / k)), clamped. Win32 uses it with the last client that was not minimized (the view is kept
  while minimized), so the game appears at the scale of the title screens and fills the client up to
  less than one factor. Headless: `--game-screen WxH`, or `--game-screen view` (the `--host-size` view
  as Win32 computes it); without it the screen stays 640x480, so every existing scenario, reference and
  G-1 is unchanged.
* **Following the host** (F110, the user's direction of 2026-10-11, superseding "once per run"): from
  that window's creation on, `w16_PeekMessage` without a message range calls `w16_screen_follow`
  (outside the system modal loops: `w16_sys_loop_active`). It asks `host_game_screen_follow`; civ64's
  `present_game_screen_follow` uses the 640x480 layout recorded by the switch's `present_game_screen`
  (its laid-out size, kept until `present_reset`), or the current one when it is smaller (a client that
  cannot hold 640x480 at the game's scale), so the screen grows and shrinks with the client and never
  takes a larger scale. A new size is set (`w16_set_screen_size`); then each visible top-level window,
  listed first: maximized, `max_rect` again (`set_rect` SIZE_MAXIMIZED); an icon on the bottom row
  moves with the bottom; a normal window's right or bottom edge on the old screen's edge keeps it there
  (class CIV by its size, others by their position); a window that lay inside the old screen is moved
  (sized, with WS_THICKFRAME) inside the new one; any change, or a window now wholly outside, goes
  through `w16_MoveWindow` (WM_GETMINMAXINFO limits, F96, WM_SIZE, WM_MOVE). Strips the screen gained
  are exposed (windows reaching into them repaint). Harness event `screen W H` as for the switch. The
  headless host answers with its fixed size or view, so the harness never follows.
* **The layout** (F103, under `w16_fit_layout`): the default map rectangle is 165,0..W,H (World Map
  0,0..166,127 and Status 0,126..166,458 as before). Before the first admission of the run
  (`layout_follow`), a civ.prf layout written on another screen size follows the new screen: the map's
  right and bottom edges that lay on the old screen's right and bottom edges move to the new ones; the
  World Map and Status windows move by the size difference when their right or bottom edge lay there.
  The old size is `w16_layout_screen_w/h` (0: 640x480); civ64 keeps it as `layout_screen=WxH` in
  civ64.cfg, written when w16 writes the rectangles into civ.prf (`w16_layout_saved`); the harness
  takes `--layout-screen WxH`.
* **Presentation.** `present_core` and `host_present` take the screen's size (host.h): a new size makes
  a new logical frame, wholly damaged, a new layout, and one whole-client show (the new borders). Under
  `integer` the factor stays k. A client narrower or lower than the frame (the window made smaller
  during the game) gets fit's layout with the current filter instead of a cut picture (`scale.c`; this
  changed one `test_present` case, 600x400 for a 640x480 frame, re-recorded). Frame dumps (`--dump-frames`,
  BMP rows padded to 4 bytes) and the headless core frame hash (W x H bytes and the palette) follow the
  size.
* **`stretch` removed** (the user's direction once the game screen works): the policy, its system-menu
  item (0x1030), `--scale-mode stretch` (refused: "integer or fit") and the `test_present` stretch groups;
  a remembered `scale_mode=stretch` reads as absent (integer).
* **Same seed.** The size is an input of the run: a larger map window draws more squares, and the map
  dissolve (DrawTilesToPort 1208:1866, RandomBelow at 1208:1A18, the shared rand) draws one random
  number per visible square, as the original does on a larger display, so the same seed plays
  differently at different game screen sizes. Since F110 the size also changes during the game with the
  window, as the player's own sizing of the map window would.
* **Tests.** `test_user` `test_game_screen` (switch at the first CIV window only, metrics, desktop
  window, new area, layout following 640x480 -> 960x540 -> 1200x700, the record and hook, limits, no
  host size, no `w16_fit_layout`); `test_present` `game_screen` (sizes for integer, fit and clamping;
  a present at a new size; the fit fallback; reset); `test_user` `test_screen_follow` (no follow before
  the map window, growth and shrinking with the map window, World Map, Status, inside, across the edge,
  maximized windows, no follow at a ranged PeekMessage or in a system modal loop, the regained strip
  repainted, reset); `test_present` `game_screen_follow` (the recorded scale kept, a smaller one when
  640x480 no longer fits, never a larger one, fit, reset); p4tests p49-prf-game (`--game-screen 960x540`:
  civ.prf's default layout of that screen, one switch, presents 640x480 then 960x540, no relation
  mismatch).

### 12.5 Observer, probes, queries, tick, profiles and input stages (D1 area A; phase D3)

Design of extension 5.1, 5.2 (O1..O7), 5.6 I2 and I7, 5.11, 7.3, 7.4, 7.8 and G-1, recorded in D1
(gate record `ext-D1-acceptance.md`). The detailed design, with every table, call site, line number
and test case, is [verify/ext-d1-draft-A.md](verify/ext-d1-draft-A.md) (Revision 3); this section
summarises it. Phase D3 implements it; D4 adds the message-box and menu queries; M the map hooks.

* **Files.** `present/observe.[ch]` (`ObsEvent`, `ObsProbe`, `ObsDraw`; `obs_init`; dispatch;
  `OBS_PROBES[]`, `OBS_CALLSITES[]`, `obs_callsite`, `obs_probe_phases`, the marker `OBS_AT_EXIT`);
  `present/profile.h` (`PresentProfile`), `profile_win.c`, `present/dos/pdos_profile.c`;
  `present/arrive.c` (arrival stage, always compiled) and `present/input.c` (filter stage, under
  `CIV_OBSERVER`); `present.c` (tick, switching, cursor route, profile-aware view functions,
  `present_core_xrgb`, `present_shown_indexed`); `platform/w16/w16_obs.[ch]` (`w16q_*`, `W16_OBS*`,
  `W16_TICK()`, the Checked guard); hooks in `vm.c`; `tests/test_observer.c`, `test_input.c`;
  `tests/harness/ext_p0.py`, `ext_frames.py`, `ext_adapt.py`, `obs_lint.py`; build variant
  `checked noobs` (`CIV_OBSERVER=0`, `build\x64\CheckedNoObs`).
* **Events (O1).** `obs_event` runs synchronously after w16's state change (before the free for a
  destroy) and calls every registered profile's `on_event` (Windows, then DOS), visible or not, then
  from M on `pmap_on_event`. Window, control, list-box, edit, menu-tracking, `OBS_SYS_TRACK` (M),
  `OBS_DRAW` (the two funnels plus per-API detail), screen damage, bitmap, palette (`OBS_PAL_ANIMATE`
  after the change, with `moved`), cursor, sound, beep and `OBS_SCREEN_SIZE` events; `t_us` is
  `w16_clock_us()`, which costs no virtual time.
* **Probes (O2).** A 2048-slot table keyed by the function start (at most 256 keys); the hook runs at
  the end of `fr_enter` and the start of `fr_leave`, touches neither `vm_steps` nor `vm_site`, and
  passes an `ObsProbe` (id, phase, `far_ret`, `addr`, `ra`, `site`, `t_us`, `arg_base`). Call-site
  precision is a callee probe plus an `OBS_CALLSITES` row (offsets for near callees); B and C resolve
  their rows with `obs_callsite` at reset. Keys: WINDOWS-INVENTORY.md 10 (175) + 26 + 2 = **203**.
  Every function whose exit another area uses has the exit phase, including RV2-1's nine (DrawMapTile,
  DrawUnitAt, DrawCityLabels, WdwMapOpen, RedrawSmMap, DrawSmTiles, RedrawStatusWindow,
  DrawStatusCivPanels, DrawUnitInfoPanel).
* **Queries (O3).** `w16q_*` in w16: clock, reads that never fault, probe arguments and return
  registers, blocks without GlobalLock, windows and visible regions, controls, menus and message
  boxes (D4), DCs, bitmaps, fonts, palettes, the screen, the cursor; `w16q_wnd_client_vis`,
  `w16q_sys_track`, `w16q_draw_on` (M). They refuse `g_randSeed` and return `const` data or copies.
* **O4.** Checked builds wrap every call into presentation in a depth guard and a state sentinel;
  emulated writes, steps, posts, clock advances and screen dirtying fault with "O4". `obs_lint.py`
  checks `present/**` (no `w16_` but `w16q_`, no `vm_`, `g_randSeed`, `post_event` or core
  `RandomBelow` call), the probe list against DESIGN and TRACE.md, every exit phase another area needs
  (`PdosMacro` keys, exit-0 scene rows, `OBS_AT_EXIT` markers) and every `obs_callsite` pair.
* **Tick (O7).** `W16_TICK()` after `w16_present(0)` at queue.c 445, clock.c 193, dos.c 358 and
  user_cursor.c 149. `present_tick` applies a pending switch, advances every registered profile,
  re-issues a changed pointer, re-lays out on a frame-size change (N 320x200 against A) and shows the
  visible profile's damage under a throttle (16,667 µs virtual headless; the monitor's refresh in
  Win32). It never touches `w16_present`'s statics, the frame lines or `events.log`.
* **Profiles (O6, T2).** `PresentProfile`: `available`, `reset`, `on_event`, `on_probe`,
  `on_core_present`, `advance`, `shows_core_frame`, `frame`, `peek`, `cursor`, `indexed`,
  `input_frame`; since UX3 the last member `square_only` (1: the current frame keeps square pixels under the 4:3
  option; NULL: never; read at every layout and tick; the Windows profile's is NULL, the DOS profile's
  `dos_square_only`, since UX5 DOS Birth only, since UX5b from its first picture, 12.7 "As implemented (UX3; changed
  by UX5 and UX5b)"). `obs_init` (civ64 after `w16_host_start`, harness in `hh_run`) registers Windows always
  and DOS when `pdos_assets_status() == PDOS_OK`, fixed for the run. A switch happens at the next tick
  with a whole-client redraw; the host window is never resized. The cursor goes through
  `present_cursor`: the profile's pixels (`host_set_cursor_pixels`, scaled by the back end) or the
  game's RT_CURSOR.
* **Input (I2, I7).** The arrival stage (`pi_arrive_*`) stamps every event with `HostEvent.frame`
  (0 Windows, 1 N, 2 A, 3 layer point, 4 ring square), applies the chords and the modifier hold rule
  (Alt as F101; Ctrl and Shift by K3, MK bits masked while held) and latches each press's class until
  its release (RV2-3). The filter in `w16_host_sync` passes frames 0 and 3, sends 1 and 2 to
  `pdos_adapt_filter` and 4 to `pmap_filter_ring`, routes a later part of a C-decided action to C when
  `pdos_adapt_note` returns 1 (C-11), and releases I4 translations once per input-taking sync. P0 runs
  post scripted core input directly and never reach the filter, except V7, whose chords go through
  the arrival and filter stages (D3b2).
* **5.11.** `--graphics windows|dos` (exit 2 when `dos` is unavailable); the key `graphics` (K7);
  `--aspect` default 4:3 again (UX3; square under UX2; X3's 4:3 before; 320x200 frames only, DOS Birth exempt, and
  until UX5 the DOS intro and title box); system-menu radio items 0x1090/0x10A0 and the chord
  Ctrl+Shift+Backspace; "Locate DOS files..." 0x10B0 (effective at the next start, RV1-6); zoom items
  0x10C0..0x10E0 (M); an unavailable profile gives one message per run, then beeps (RV1-8). Since HM the profile
  items, "Switch graphics" (0x1100) and "Locate DOS files…" (or "DOS files present") are in the Graphics submenu and
  the zoom items in the Map zoom submenu (12.2).
* **Rules met.** P0 as scoped in 12.11; O1..O7; I2 (identity for frames 0 and 3); I7 (chords consumed,
  key state consistent, Ctrl+Shift+Backspace audited against ACCELERATOR 128 and every key handler);
  G-1 on the observer and the `CIV_OBSERVER=0` builds.
* **Choices** (A 15):
  * probes looked up by a hash on `fd->addr`, not a `FrameDesc` field;
  * call sites by callee probe plus `ra` rows, not statement probes;
  * a separate observer channel; damage events in the two funnels, detail per API;
  * the `w16q_*` queries in w16, the only w16 calls `present/` makes;
  * four explicit tick sites; every registered profile advances at every tick;
  * two-stage input, arrival portable and shared by Win32 and 7.8; the frame stamped at arrival;
  * a press latches its class until its release;
  * the Ctrl and Shift hold rule with masked MK bits;
  * registration once in `obs_init`; Locate effective at the next start; one refusal message per run;
  * the back end scales the DOS pointer; re-layout on a frame-size change at the tick;
  * exit phases checked against the consumers' tables at build time.
* **Tests and the D3 gate.** `test_observer.c`, `test_input.c` (dispatch, C-11 routing, the press
  latch, hold rule, masked MK bits, F100), `test_present.c` (tick, switching, re-layout, 4:3 default,
  cursor route, `present_game_screen` equal under both profiles), the 7.2 Win32-headless cases;
  `ext_p0.py` (7.3, V0..V6 with switch assertions from `profiles.log`; every DOS variation passes
  `--dos-data build\dosdata`); `ext_frames.py` (7.4); `ext_g1.py` on Checked and CheckedNoObs; the
  record `ext-D3-probe-verification.md`. D3's gate: 7.3 without zoom, 7.4 and the probe
  verification; Asan deferred (K8).
* **Open** (A 16): A1 `OBS_CALLSITES` generation (D3), A2 emission points in `user_ctl.c` and
  `user_listbox.c` (D3), A3 chord audit against the final adapter keys, A4 S1 read as the start-up
  run, A5 event and hook cost (D3), A6, A7, A9 (M). A8, A10, A11 are closed. A1, A2, A5, A6 and A7
  were closed in D3 (below); A3 stays open for the adapter keys of area C.

**As implemented (D3).** Gate record [ext-D3-acceptance.md](ext-D3-acceptance.md) (2026-10-11): 7.3
without zoom and 7.4 pass with switching and the probe verification is recorded, in Checked,
CheckedNoObs and Release; the Asan run is deferred to the final pass (K8). D3 was done in three units,
each with its record: D3a, the observer core ([ext-D3a-record.md](ext-D3a-record.md), with
[ext-D3-probe-verification.md](ext-D3-probe-verification.md)); D3b1, presentation, switching and the
P0 runners ([ext-D3b1-record.md](ext-D3b1-record.md)); D3b2, the input stages, the chord and menu and
the 7.8 mode ([ext-D3b2-record.md](ext-D3b2-record.md)). The results are compiled, headless and
Win32-headless (hidden window): civ64.exe was not started in D3, no picture was looked at and no DOS
program was executed.

* **Observer and probes** (D3a). `vm.h` has `CIV_OBSERVER` (default 1; 0 compiles the observer out, the
  build variant `checked noobs`). `vm_probe_install` takes at most `VM_PROBE_MAX` = 256 keys into 2,048
  open-addressing slots keyed by `FrameDesc.addr` (hash `(addr * 2654435761u) >> 21`, linear probing); it
  returns -1 and leaves the table empty for more than 256 keys, address 0, a duplicate or a NULL
  function. The entry hook runs at the end of `fr_enter`, the frame complete (`ra` as stored: full for a
  far return, the offset for a near one); the exit hook is the first statement of `fr_leave`, before
  `vm_site` and the control stack are restored and before any pop (`ra` from the control stack, 0 beyond
  its depth). A run without keys pays one load and one branch per frame. The tables are generated:
  `gen/obs_tables.py` (`--check` in `test.cmd`) writes `present/obs_tables.[ch]` with the 203 keys
  (`OBS_PROBES[]`, the row constants `OBS_P_<Name>`, `OBS_NPROBES`: WINDOWS-INVENTORY.md 10, A 4.3's
  additions and area C's two keys) and the 98 call-site rows (`OBS_CALLSITES[]`). Each key and row was
  verified against the listing and `showfunc.py` ([ext-D3-probe-verification.md](ext-D3-probe-verification.md)):
  202 keys are `FR_WIN_FAR` and one (`_ErrorMessage` 1000:4124) is `FR_FAR_BP`; every key has `far_ret`
  1; none is `FR_NONE` or near, so those hook paths and `obs_callsite_in` (the search over a caller's
  table) are tested on synthetic frames. `observe.c` has `obs_init` (resets the listeners and counts and
  installs the 203 keys through `w16q_obs_start`), `obs_listen` (at most `OBS_LISTENERS_MAX`, called in
  registration order), `obs_event`, `obs_probe` (fills `ObsProbe`, finds the call-site row, counts,
  dispatches), `obs_tick`, `obs_probe_hits`, `obs_event_count`. civ64 calls `obs_init()` after
  `w16_host_start` and before the game runs; a refusal ends with "The presentation observer could not be
  set up." `obs_lint.py` (in every `test.cmd`) checks `present/**` and the generated tables as 12.5 lists
  (`OBS_LINT_OK files 33 probes 203 callsites 98`); its gaps without a current violation (uppercase `W16_`
  macros, `vm_` symbols outside area C's files, the include exemption) are recorded in D3b2. No
  "observer probe" row exists yet in DESIGN.md or TRACE.md; when one is added, it starts `| observer probe |`
  and names the address, the function and the phases as one cell, and `obs_lint.py` then checks it
  against `OBS_PROBES[]` (the 203 rows are in the probe verification, section 2).
* **Events and emission sites** (D3a). The macros `W16_OBS_*` (empty under `CIV_OBSERVER 0`) and the
  builders `w16_obs_*` emit synchronously after w16's state change; an event carries
  `t_us = w16_clock_us()`; `w16_obs_draw_init` records the DC's state and `w16_obs_rect32` clamps a
  rectangle. Sites: `gdi_draw.c` (fill, frame, line, polyline, polygon, set pixel, BitBlt), `gdi_font.c`
  (text out, draw text), `gdi_dc.c` (bitmap damage in `w16_target_done`), `gdi_pal.c` (screen damage in
  `w16_screen_dirty`, realize, animate), `gdi_obj.c` (bitmap create, bits, delete, palette select and set),
  `user_wnd.c`, `user_dlg.c`, `user_ctl.c`, `user_listbox.c`, `user_menutrack.c`, `user_syscmd.c`
  (`OBS_SYS_TRACK`, marked M, is emitted already), `user_cursor.c`, `user_misc.c`. As built: `OBS_DLG_CREATE`
  after the template's controls and before WM_INITDIALOG; `OBS_WND_CREATE` when the record is complete,
  just before it is linked into the z-order; `OBS_WND_DESTROY` in `free_tree`, children first, with the
  focus and activation clears also emitted; `OBS_PAL_REALIZE` only when RealizePalette changed entries;
  no `OBS_CTL_CHECK` site (w16 keeps no check state); no `OBS_DRAW` for `DT_CALCRECT`; `OBS_LB_TOP`
  added; `OBS_POLYLINE` and `OBS_POLYGON` copy at most `OBS_PTS_MAX` (256) points; `ObsDraw.rop2` is always 13.
* **Queries and accessors** (D3a). `w16q_now_us`, the readers `w16q_rd8/16/32`, `w16q_read` (follows the
  tiles of a huge block), `w16q_strn`, the argument and return readers, `w16q_block`, the window,
  control, menu-state, GDI, screen, system-palette and cursor queries, `w16q_diag` and `w16q_obs_start`
  (installs a table and clears the diagnostics; refused inside the observer). Readers give 0 for an
  address the emulation would fault on and for `g_randSeed` 1420:4EE0..4EE3, counted in `W16qDiag`
  (which also counts `dropped`, `rgn_overflows` and `nlast`); the window queries give an empty region for
  a window being destroyed. The queries of M and D4 (`w16q_sys_track`, `w16q_draw_on`, `w16q_msgbox`,
  `w16q_menu_item`, `w16q_menu_bar`) are not D3's. `w16q_msgbox` was added in D5b (draft A 5.2's reviewed
  getter: `W16qMsgBox`, a live visible message box's laid-out lines, text origin, icon and up to 3 buttons with
  the default; through the read-only accessor `w16_msgbox_info` of `user_msgbox.c`, in obs_lint's
  `REVIEWED_CALLS`; 12.7 "As implemented (D5b; area B)"). D6fS-a (2026-10-17) adds `w16q_wnd_vis_skip` and
  `w16q_wnd_client_vis_skip` (a window's visible region computed without the overlays and without given top-level
  windows and their descendants; -1 for an invalid handle), `w16q_upd_pending` (a shown window meeting a rectangle has
  an update region pending) and `w16q_menu_saved` (a menu level's saved pixels), through the read-only accessors
  `w16_wnd_vis_skip`, `w16_wnd_upd_pending` (`user_wnd.c`) and `w16_menu_track_saved` (`user_menutrack.c`), in
  obs_lint's `REVIEWED_CALLS` (12.9 "As implemented (D6fS-a)"; 12.10). Read-only accessors under `#if CIV_OBSERVER` serve the
  file-static state: `w16_wnd_info`, `w16_wnd_vis`, `w16_wnd_client_vis_obs`, `w16_wnd_desktop`,
  `w16_btn_info`, `w16_edit_info`, `w16_lb_info`, `w16_lb_text`, `w16_menu_track_info` (with `g_obs_mt`)
  and `w16_cursor_info`; inside the observer `rgn.c` counts an overflow instead of logging it (12.10).
* **The guard (O4)** (D3a). `w16_obs_begin(in, id)` / `w16_obs_end()`, one level; `in` is the kind
  (event, probe, tick, other) and `id` the event type, probe id or `W16_OBS_OTHER_*` id. In Checked a
  nested call faults ("O4: probe re-entry", "O4: observer re-entry"), the forbid checks `W16_OBS_FORBID`
  fault "O4: <what>" while it is held (in `vm_api_enter`, `vm_step`, `acc_chk`, `vm_resolve` writes,
  `vm_block_alloc`, `fr_enter`, `fr_leave`, `w16_target_done`, `w16_screen_dirty`, `w16_present`, input
  posting, `PostMessage`, the key state, the focus change, `SetCapture`, `ReleaseCapture`, the clock
  advance and `hpool_alloc`), and the sentinel compares `vm_steps`, `vm_site`, the registers, the clock,
  the queued input count and the counters `vm_obs_writes` and `vm_obs_allocs` before and after every
  observer call ("O4 sentinel: <what>"). In Release a nested call is dropped and counted. The ids
  `W16_OBS_OTHER_PRESENT`, `_CURSOR`, `_COMMAND` (D3b1) and `_INPUT` (D3b2) name the other callers.
* **Tick and profiles** (D3b1). `present/profile.h` has `PresentProfile` (the callbacks of the Profiles
  bullet above, `PresentCursor`, the show reasons `PRESENT_SHOW_CORE/TICK/VIEW`, the tick clocks
  `PRESENT_TICK_VIRTUAL` of 16,667 µs and `PRESENT_TICK_HOST`) and the hooks `PresentHooks`
  (`on_switch`, `on_sample`, `sample_every_us`). The Windows profile (`profile_win.c`) holds S1's frame
  (F105's game-screen rule), shows the core frame, uses the game's cursor, has no 8-bit form and input
  frame 0. The DOS profile (`present/dos/pdos_profile.c`) is available when `pdos_assets_status() ==
  PDOS_OK`; in D3 its frame is the Windows frame with the damage of the core presents accumulated since
  its last take, shown only through the tick (`shows_core_frame` 0), with the game's cursor, no 8-bit form,
  input frame 2 and no event, probe or advance callback yet (B's from D4). `obs_init` calls
  `present_profiles_init()`, which registers the Windows profile and the DOS profile when available for the
  whole run. `w16_present` calls `present_core_frame` (the Windows frame, then every registered
  profile's `on_core_present`, then the visible profile's CORE show when it shows the core frame) inside
  `W16_OBS_GUARD_BEGIN(W16_OBS_IN_OTHER, W16_OBS_OTHER_PRESENT)` on a copy of the rectangle and then
  `host_present` with the unchanged arguments, unconditionally (`present_core` stays as the wrapper
  the unit tests use). `W16_TICK()` sits right after `w16_present(0)` at the four sites (queue.c
  `w16_PeekMessage`, clock.c `w16_GetTickCount` before the poll's cost, dos.c INT 21h AH=2Ch,
  user_cursor.c `w16_SetCursor`) and reaches `present_tick` → `present_tick_at(w16q_now_us())`: (1) a
  pending request switches (the new frame's layout, the whole client redrawn as VIEW without throttle,
  the cursor issued again, the switch hook); (2) every registered profile advances; (3) the visible
  profile's pointer is issued again when it reports a change, or the game's cursor when it stops having
  one; (4) the sample hook runs at the first tick at or after each multiple of `sample_every_us`; (5) a
  profile that shows the core frame returns here; (5a) a visible frame whose size changed (A-B6), or (UX3) whose
  pixel aspect changed (the visible profile's `square_only` under the 4:3 option, `relayout_due`), is laid out
  again, the whole client shown, the cursor issued again; a pixel-aspect change that leaves the picture as it is
  (an integer client with no whole 6/5 pair, such as 640x480) only takes the new layout; (6) the throttle (16,667 µs of virtual time
  headless, the monitor's period from `host_now_us()` in Win32); (7, 8) the visible frame's damage is
  scaled and shown (TICK). A request for an unregistered profile is refused with the reason (the
  store's message, or "The DOS graphics are not in this build." under `CIV_OBSERVER 0`). T6 (F128):
  without polls (CPU-bound core work) no tick runs and the DOS view pauses; Birth inside world
  creation still ticks at `GetTickCount`. The view functions (`present_view_point`,
  `present_view_layout`, `present_shown_frame`, `present_shown_indexed`) follow the visible profile;
  `present_core_xrgb` is always the Windows frame; `present_game_screen(_follow)` never reads the
  profile (K1).
* **Switching and the cursor route** (D3b1, D3b2). A switch is a request applied at the next tick.
  `present_request_profile` serves the harness and the tests. The player's chord and menu call
  `present_player_profile(p)`, `p` a profile or `PRESENT_PROFILE_TOGGLE` (the other one than the profile
  to come: the pending request's, else the visible one): 0 requested, or a pending request withdrawn when
  `p` is the visible profile; 1 when `p` already is the profile to come; -1 refused. So a chord pressed
  twice before a tick leaves the profile unchanged, and the radio marks show the profile to come at once.
  The run's first refusal shows one `host_message` (captioned "Sid Meier's Civilization" in D3, since LP
  `HOST_CAPTION` = "Sid Meier's Civilization 64bit", 12.2: the store's sentence, then
  ` To use them, choose "Locate DOS files..." in the window menu; the DOS graphics become available the next
  time civ64 starts.`; under `CIV_OBSERVER 0` "The DOS graphics are not in this build."); later refusals
  beep (`host_beep`); `present_reset` starts a new run's cadence. Since HM (FIDELITY F118, F258; `ext-HM-record.md`
  Decision 10) the hint reads ` To use them, choose Graphics > "Locate DOS files…" in the window menu; the DOS graphics
  become available the next time civ64 starts.` (the ellipsis `\x85` in the source, `host_message`'s code page 1252)
  and, after a Locate that recorded the DOS files in this run, ` The DOS files you located are used from the next
  start: restart civ64 to use the DOS graphics.`: `present_set_dos_located(int)` (`present/profile.h`, a static flag
  in present.c; civ64_main.c's `locate_dos` sets 1 when `civ64_locate_dos_choose` returns 1; `present_reset` does not
  clear it). `present_cursor(res, len, idc)` keeps
  the game's current cursor and issues it, unless the visible profile has a pointer of its own
  (`host_set_cursor_pixels`); a switch, a frame-size change and a reported pointer change issue it
  again; before the game sets a cursor, the end of a profile's pointer restores the arrow (IDC 32512).
  Win32: `host_set_cursor_pixels` makes the pixels a cursor (`CreateIconIndirect` from a 32-bit colour DIB
  and a 1-bit mask set where alpha is 0), scaled by the cursor factors by nearest neighbour with the
  hot spot and made again when the factors change (since UX1 the pointer factors, `present_pointer_factors`,
  since UX1b the mean of the factors before UX1 and UX1's, 12.2 "Cursor"); the tick clock is the period of the window's monitor
  (`dmDisplayFrequency`, 60 Hz when it reads 0 or 1), set after the window is created, on WM_DISPLAYCHANGE
  and on WM_MOVE when the monitor changed; `present_diag_log()` writes `W16qDiag` and the DOS store's
  accessor diagnostics to the debug log at `host_exit` and `host_fault`.
* **Arrival and filter stages** (D3b2). `present/arrive.c` (always compiled; `present/input.h`) holds the
  rules of S2 that moved out of `host_win32.c` (decision K4): `pi_arrive_key`, `_char`, `_mouse`, `_wheel`
  (produces nothing, I8), `_focus_lost`, `_capture_lost`, `_quit`, and `pi_reset`. Output `PiOut` has
  at most `PI_OUT_MAX` = 264 events, at most one command with its anchor and a capture request. Rules:
  the Alt rule (F101), K3's hold rule for Ctrl and Shift (F114), the outside-image rule (F100), the press
  latch (RV2-3: a delivered press fixes its class until its release; the layer classification is
  `pi_set_layer`, NULL in D3, so only today's third class occurs) and `HostEvent.frame` (K5): the visible
  profile's `input_frame()` for keys, characters, quit, focus- and capture-loss releases and mouse events off
  the layer, 3 and 4 from the latch. Focus loss releases keys only (every delivered key in key order, a Tab
  press first while a delivered Alt is down); the buttons are released at capture loss. A Ctrl or Shift
  press held back has its MK bit cleared in mouse moves and releases (RV1-15). `present/input.c` (body
  under `CIV_OBSERVER`) is the filter: `present_input_filter(ev, out, max)` passes frames 0 and 3
  unchanged (I2), sends 1 and 2 to area C's adapter and 4 to area D's ring translation, asks `adapt_note`
  for frames 0, 3 and 4 while the DOS profile is registered (a 1 sends the event to the adapter, C-11),
  consumes and counts an unknown frame; `present_input_release(out, max)` (I4) is 0 without the DOS
  profile, else `pend_release(out, max, w16q_now_us())`. The four entries are area C's `pdos_adapt_note`
  and `pdos_adapt_filter` (D4c1), D4c2's `pdos_pend_release` (I4's pending translations; it then calls
  D4b4's `pdos_seq_autocontinue_release` with the room left) and M's `pmap_filter_ring`
  (`PiDispatch`; a NULL member of `present_input_set_dispatch` takes its default). `input.c` checks at
  compile time that PDOS_PEND_EVENTS <= PI_MAX_OUT and that area C's frame numbers equal PiFrame's.
  `input.c` also checks at compile time that PDOS_PEND_EVENTS + 2 <= PI_MAX_OUT (a released step and the
  auto-continue's press and release in one sync). Every
  event the filter stage returns for frames 1 and 2 is a Windows-form event (frame 0). `host_sync.c`'s
  `take_input` counts each input-taking sync (`g_input_syncs`), calls
  `w16_host_input_sync_fn(n)`, passes each polled host event through the filter inside
  `W16_OBS_GUARD_BEGIN(W16_OBS_IN_OTHER, W16_OBS_OTHER_INPUT)` (an event is posted unchanged when the
  guard cannot be entered), then posts `present_input_release`'s events; `posted()` gives each message
  actually posted to `w16_host_post_fn`; `W16_TIMING_SCRIPT` (3) takes input this way and returns before
  the clock part. Under `CIV_OBSERVER 0` the ring is posted directly as before. *(UX4, 2026-10-16, F114:
  `PiKey.fake` (`present/input.h`, the last member, 0 for every other source) marks a key message as Windows' own:
  lParam bit 25 (`FAKE_KEYSTROKE`) set, or a Shift release without it when the next key message already in the
  window's queue has the same message time and is a keypad key (scan 0x47..0x53 not extended, not 0x4A/0x4E). The
  arrival stage holds such a Shift release back only while it may serve the chord (observer build, Shift down in
  the arrival's state, Ctrl down, Alt up) until the next event: when that is the keypad's 0 making the reset chord
  (`zoom_key(vk, scan)`: `VK_INSERT` with scan 0x52 not extended, as `'0'` and `VK_NUMPAD0`) the release is
  dropped and Shift serves the chord; every other next event (a key, a character, a mouse event, the wheel, focus
  or capture loss, quit) takes the release first (`fake_release` first after `begin` in every `pi_arrive_*`
  entry), so the events are exactly those of before UX4. The Ctrl+Shift chord condition lives in
  `ctrl_shift_cmd`. No global hook, raw input or `RegisterHotKey`.)* *(UX4b, 2026-10-16, F114, F188: `zoom_key` also
  gives `PI_CMD_ZOOM_RESET` for `VK_HOME` (`PI_VK_HOME` 0x24) whatever its scan code, observer builds only, so
  Ctrl+Shift+Home with the gray Home (0x147) or the keypad's 7 with Num Lock off (0x47) is one more reset chord, with
  Ctrl+Shift+0's rules (a press without the repeat bit gives one reset; its repeats and release and the held Ctrl and
  Shift never reach the game; no chord with Alt down or with Home already delivered down). `ctrl_shift_cmd`, the hold
  rule and UX4's held-back release are unchanged: that release is dropped only before the keypad's 0, so with Num Lock
  on the keypad's 7 (Windows' Shift release, then `VK_HOME` with Ctrl only) gives no chord and Ctrl, Shift, its release
  and Home reach the game.)*
* **Chord, menu and Locate** (D3b2). The Win32 back end keeps the Win32 parts of the input: `PiKey` from
  WM_KEYDOWN / WM_SYSKEYDOWN (Ctrl and Shift from `GetKeyState`), `TranslateMessage` and the
  Windows-1252 character before the arrival, `SetCapture` / `ReleaseCapture` on the request; Alt+Enter
  toggles full screen and runs the settings hook, Ctrl+Shift+Backspace calls
  `present_player_profile(TOGGLE)`. The system menu has the items of 12.2: 0x1090 and 0x10A0 are radio
  items marking the profile to come, refreshed on WM_INITMENU; an accepted switch calls the profile hook
  `hw_set_profile_hook(fn(int profile))` and civ64 saves `graphics`; 0x10B0 calls
  `hw_set_locate_hook(fn)`, which runs `civ64_locate_dos_choose(&where, &cfg, owner)` with the game
  window as the owner of the file dialog and the messages (the game window then waits disabled under
  the dialog): the choose-and-validate loop of the start-up question without its "not found" question; an
  invalid choice is refused with its message and asked again; cancel ends it; a valid one sets
  `dos_data`, removes `dos_ask=never` (`civ64_cfg_unset`), saves `civ64.cfg`, frees the temporary
  `Civ64Found` and says "The DOS files are recorded. The DOS graphics become available the next time
  civ64 starts." The DOS store, the running game and its profiles are untouched (RV1-6, F117). Test
  seams: `hw_messages()`, `civ64_locate_set_ui(pick, say)`. Since HM (FIDELITY F258) the menu's "Switch graphics"
  (0x1100, `SC_CIV_SWITCH`) calls `player_profile(PRESENT_PROFILE_TOGGLE)` as the chord does (the same request,
  withdrawal, profile hook and refusal; nothing reaches the game), and civ64 registers `hw_set_dos_present_hook(fn)`
  after `hw_set_locate_hook(locate_dos)`: `dos_present()` answers 1 while the run's DOS graphics are available
  (`pdos_assets_status() == PDOS_OK`) or a Locate succeeded in this run (`g_dos_recorded`), so the Locate item reads
  "DOS files present", disabled, from the next menu update (at once after a successful Locate); its command then
  returns before the Locate hook. Files removed from the disk while civ64 runs do not change the answer: the asset
  store holds the run's DOS files (`ext-HM-record.md` Finding 6), and the next start without them asks as before.
* **Harness** (D3a, D3b1, D3b2; RUNNING.md lists the options). `--graphics windows|dos`, `--switch-every
  MS`, `--profile-samples MS`, `--dump-presented DIR`, `--probes 0`, `--host-input FILE` and
  `--core-input-log 1`; the `--aspect` default is 4:3 (UX3, civ64's default, as from D3b1 to UX2, X3; square under
  UX2); since UX3 `--presented 2` also writes each show's layout and each cursor line's pointer factors. The script commands `at <ms> profile
  windows|dos|toggle`, `at <ms> policy integer|fit [nearest|sharp] [square|4:3]` (an omitted value keeps
  its current value) and `at <ms> hostsize WxH` are applied in `step()` at `VM_STEP_API` when due, inside
  `W16_OBS_GUARD_BEGIN(W16_OBS_IN_OTHER, W16_OBS_OTHER_COMMAND)`, calling only `present_request_profile`,
  `present_set_options` and `hh_set_view`; `zoom` was refused until M (it is accepted since M, 12.9). `--host-input` is the 7.8 mode:
  host-form lines (`hkey`, `hmouse`, `hwheel`, `hchar`, `hfocus`, `hcapture`, `hquit` and the commands)
  at `at <ms>` or `sync <n>` go through the arrival stage at the first `VM_STEP_API` at or after the time,
  or at the start of the n-th input-taking sync, and the events into the headless ring that the game's syncs
  post through the filter stage; the chord's command acts as civ64's does, full screen is only recorded.
  Logs, all beside `run.log`, never compared by G-1: `observer-probes.log` and `presentation-diag.log`
  (every run of an observer build; the second also has the DOS store's `pdos` line); `profiles.log`
  (`start`, `request`, `switch`, `sample`, the player's `request ... player` / `withdraw ... player` / `refused
  dos: player chord, refusal N` lines, and `end requests R switches S refused F pending P replaced Q
  withdrawn W`); `presented.log` by show reason (CORE keeps S1's format and relation check, now against
  `present_core_xrgb()` while the Windows profile is visible; `tick` and `view` lines carry the profile;
  `cursor` lines; the end line `end presents N relation_mismatches M tick_shows T`); `hostinput.log` (one
  line per host line) and `coreinput.log` (every message the syncs posted; empty without `--host-input`).
  Under `CIV_OBSERVER 0` `--graphics dos`, `--switch-every`, `--host-input`, `--core-input-log` and the
  `profile` command exit 2 ("not in this build (CIV_OBSERVER 0)"). `--dump-presented` writes
  `presented-NNNNN.bmp` while the visible profile shows the core frame and `tick-NNNNN.bmp` at TICK and VIEW
  shows otherwise.
* **The P0 runners** (D3b1, D3b2; all in `test.cmd checked`, V5 in `test.cmd checked noobs`). Each variation
  runs the 27 scenarios (the 23 rows of `scenarios.py` and gate3b's S2, S2b, S2c, S3); "equal" is
  `run.log`'s frame, snapshot and result lines, the whole `events.log` and the exit status. `ext_p0.py`
  (7.3): **V0** none (the 4:3 default again since UX3; square under UX2; 4:3 from D3b1 to UX2), equal to its `ext-g1-baseline.txt` row; **V1** `--dos-data build\dosdata
  --graphics dos --presented 1`; **V2** the same without `--graphics dos` and with `--switch-every 997`;
  **V3** a generated script of `policy` and `hostsize` commands; **V5** the V0 options on CheckedNoObs;
  **V6** `--game-screen view --switch-every 997` on s3 and orders; **V6w / V6r** `--host-size 1920x1080
  --game-screen view` with and without the DOS options and switching (V6w equal to V6r: the game screen
  is 960x540, so V6r's trace differs from V0's by design); **V7** every scenario with a host-input file of the
  player's chords (Ctrl+Shift+Backspace and Alt+Enter in turn, every 1,009 ms), so the filter stage runs
  at every input-taking sync. Each of V1, V2, V3, V6 and V7 equals V0, and V5 equals its baseline row and
  the Checked V0 outputs. The switching variations assert from `profiles.log` that every request
  became a switch (`switches + pending == requests`, with `withdrawn` added for V7's chords), with
  `replaced 0` and refused 0. `ext_frames.py` (7.4, and the dump check
  of 13.4): runs A `--graphics dos`, A' its repeat, B `--switch-every 997` and C `--graphics windows`, all
  with `--profile-samples 100`, on start, s3 and birth: the sample lines are identical across the four for
  every registered profile, A's `presented.log` equals A''s, and the dump check writes `tick-NNNNN.bmp`
  and no `presented-NNNNN.bmp`. *(D5P, 2026-10-16, F187: a presentation-only exception to draft A 13.2's "every
  profile's frame does not depend on which profile is shown": the DOS view at a return to the title menu depends
  on the profile visible at the core's return (the title picture after a Windows-side return, by the user's
  ruling); no run of `ext_frames.py` or of `ext_timeline.py`'s `start`/`switch` reaches a title return, and P0
  holds.)* `ext_adapt.py` (7.8, I7): two runs of gate3b's S3 in host form (the
  script converted line for line), plain and with six chords and two Alt+Enter, both with
  `--core-input-log 1`: the chord run's `coreinput.log`, frames, snapshots, events and exit equal the
  plain run's. Results: `P0_OK checks 224` (Checked) and `54` (CheckedNoObs), `EXT_FRAMES_OK`,
  `EXT_ADAPT_OK checks 11`, `EXT_G1_OK runs 77` on Checked and CheckedNoObs; V2 made 1,866 switches, V7 928
  chords and 910 Alt+Enter. Probe hits: the Checked matrix hits 179 of the 203 keys; the 24 never hit
  (11 that no base run reaches, 13 that the 27 scenarios do not reach) are listed in the gate record.
  Test counts (Checked / CheckedNoObs / Release): `test_observer` 103 / 1 / 92, `test_present` 521,
  `test_input` 140 / 122 / 140, `test_host_win32` 185, `test_locate` 36.
* **Measured costs** (Open A5; informational). `test_observer`'s loops of 2,000,000 iterations, ns per
  call, Checked / Release: an event with no listener 26.3 / 6.5, with one no-op listener 26.5 / 8.1;
  `fr_enter` + `fr_leave` with no table 49.4 / 32.1, with the 203-key table and a miss 50.9 / 32.9, with a
  hit at both phases and no probe listener 118.5 / 71.7. So a miss costs nothing measurable over the
  frame pair, a hit about 20 ns (Release) or 35 ns (Checked) per hook and an event 6.5 ns (Release).
  Soak S4 (`CAE3540B.SAV`, Space/Enter every 500 ms to the game's end: 63,877,031 steps, 4,085 frames,
  284,300 events, 944,229 entry and 671,969 exit hook calls), median wall time of passes 2..4: 16.36 s
  with the table, 16.42 s with `--probes 0`, 16.18 s in CheckedNoObs; the observer as a whole costs about
  0.2 s of 16.2 s (about 1 %), the table alone less than the run-to-run spread. Release's cost is the
  timing loops' (the harness is built only for the Checked variants). The cost under a real listener
  is not measured.
* **Opens closed.** A1 (the tables are generated: 203 keys, 98 rows), A2 (the emission points above; no
  `OBS_CTL_CHECK` site), A5 (the costs above), A6 (no key has an `FR_NONE` or near frame; the paths are
  tested on synthetic frames) and A7 (existing getters reused; the file-static state needs the
  accessors above). A3 stays open (the adapter keys of area C; D3b2 audited the game's keys).
* **Deviations from the design** (one block per unit; the tables with the reasons are in the records):
  * D3a ([deviations](ext-D3a-record.md#deviations-from-the-design)): 12.10's list widened by the
    read-only accessors and `rgn.c`'s counted overflow; the window queries' empty region for a window
    being destroyed; `w16q_obs_start` refused inside the observer; the emission points of `OBS_DLG_CREATE`,
    `OBS_WND_CREATE`, `OBS_WND_DESTROY` and `OBS_PAL_REALIZE`; no `OBS_CTL_CHECK` site; no `OBS_DRAW` for
    `DT_CALCRECT`; additions to `ObsEvent`, `ObsDraw` and `W16qDiag`; the guard takes the kind and the id
    and keeps its depth in Release; the sentinel compares the queued input count; `obs_callsite_in`;
    `obs_lint.py`'s include exemption and consumer-table rules; the tables as separate generated files;
    the spin test headless; `--probes 0`; `obs_tick` left empty for D3b.
  * D3b1 ([deviations](ext-D3b1-record.md#deviations-from-the-design)): `present_core` split into
    `present_core_frame` inside the guard and `host_present` after it; the ids `W16_OBS_OTHER_PRESENT`,
    `_CURSOR`, `_COMMAND`; E.2.2's include rule admits `present.h` and `profile.h` in `pdos_profile.c`;
    `policy` keeps omitted values; `--switch-every` makes one toggle per step; `profiles.log`'s `replaced Q`
    and the `replaced 0` assertion; `presented.log`'s first line is a `view` line; the Win32 diagnostics go
    to the debug log; V5 runs in `test.cmd checked noobs`; the P0 runs have no frame dumps and use their
    own saves and a second wave; the extra V6w / V6r; `--presented 1` on the DOS variations;
    `--graphics windows` and `--profile-samples` accepted under `CIV_OBSERVER 0`; `--graphics dos` checked
    right after `pdos_assets_open`; the tick clock's reset of the throttle; the test seams
    `present_tick_at`, `present_profile_register`, `hw_ticks_dumped`.
  * D3b2 ([deviations](ext-D3b2-record.md#deviations-from-the-design)): `PI_OUT_MAX` 264; focus loss
    releases keys only and buttons go at capture loss; the chord key's repeats are swallowed after the
    modifier's release (the only S2 behaviour changed, an edge); no chord on a Backspace already down;
    Alt+Enter serves only the held Alt; a lone release comes after the presses held before it; a press
    not delivered leaves the held keys held; the capture-loss release's position and frame; unpaired
    releases still delivered (S2); the player's switch through `present_player_profile` with radio marks
    for the profile to come; `civ64_locate_dos_choose`'s `owner`; Locate grayed without the observer; a
    separate profile hook; `profiles.log`'s player lines and `withdrawn W`; `hostinput.log`;
    `--core-input-log` refused under `CIV_OBSERVER 0` and empty without `--host-input`; `--script` together
    with `--host-input`; sorted commands and host lines; the guard id `W16_OBS_OTHER_INPUT`; the extra V7;
    `ext_adapt.py`'s S3 conversion; the C-11 case with area C's real note left to D4.
* **Not verified.** Asan (deferred); civ64.exe was not started, so `--graphics`, the start profile, the
  aspect default (4:3 in D3; square under UX2; 4:3 again since UX3), the monitor-rate tick, the pixel cursor, the chord, the menu items, the refusal message,
  Locate's dialogs and the remembered `graphics` key are covered by the Win32-headless tests only (the
  user's checks D3-1..D3-10 are pending); the tick clock's monitor change and a real focus change with a
  button down; the DOS profile shows the Windows frame in D3, so 7.4's sample identity holds by
  construction; the filter's adapter, note, release and ring run with stubs until D4 and M; the
  reviewers of the three units were separate read-only agent sessions.

**Additions in M** (phase M, 2026-10-11; the map zoom and layer are described in 12.9; records
[ext-Ma-record.md](ext-Ma-record.md), [ext-Mb1-record.md](ext-Mb1-record.md),
[ext-Mb2-record.md](ext-Mb2-record.md)):

* **Tick.** Step 3a, `pmap_tick()`, runs inside the tick's guard before the sample hook (Mb1: it brings the
  world canvas up to date at level -1). Step 5 (A-1): a profile that shows the core frame returns only when
  `pmap_pending()` is 0; otherwise the throttle and step 7 follow, and for such a profile step 7 shows only
  the layer's damage and the base frame's give-back, as a TICK show. `pmap_pending()` is also 1 while the
  canvas is behind the snapshot at level -1.
* **Layer.** `layer()` runs after every scaling (A-2): the CORE show in `present_core_frame`, the VIEW redraw
  `show_all` and the TICK show. `pmap_on_core_present` is called for every core present, whichever profile
  is visible. The map listener is registered by `present_profiles_init` after the profiles' (three of
  `OBS_LISTENERS_MAX` 4 in a DOS run).
* **Input.** `PiOut` gains `cmd_steps` (the wheel's whole steps, 1 for a chord) and `cmd_at` (1 when
  `cmd_hx`, `cmd_hy` are an anchor). `PI_LAYER_GONE` (-1) is the layer classification when no layer is
  drawn: the arrival stage then maps a held view press's remaining parts by the visible frame. Each latch
  keeps the last point and frame delivered for its button, and a capture-loss release uses its own.
  Frame 4 goes to `pmap_filter_ring` (D3's consuming stub is removed; `present_input_set_dispatch(NULL)`
  restores it).
* **Tests.** `test_map.c`; `ext_p0.py` V4 and the CheckedNoObs zoom refusals; `ext_adapt.py`'s zoomkeys,
  ring and ringref runs (`EXT_ADAPT_OK checks 20`: D3b2's 11 and M's 9); `ext_mirror.py` and `ext_zoom.py`,
  which run in `test.cmd checked` after `ext_adapt.py`.

**Added in D4b4 (area A).** Record [ext-D4b4-record.md](ext-D4b4-record.md) (2026-10-15): the Birth
auto-continue, by the user's direction of 2026-10-15 (its area-C part is in 12.8, "Added in D4b4").
Compiled and headless in Checked, CheckedNoObs and Release (final eight-step cycle ALL_GREEN,
`build/d4b4-status.txt`); Asan not run.

* **The filter stage's default pending release** (`PiDispatch.pend_release`) is the DOS sequencer's Birth
  auto-continue, `pdos_seq_autocontinue_release`, in place of D3's empty stub. `present_input_release` calls
  it only while the DOS profile is registered; `host_sync.c` posts what it returns at the input-taking sync,
  after that sync's host events (I4's path; no w16 change). Since D4c2 `pdos_pend_release` takes that place
  and calls it once per call, after its own step.
* **Presses noted.** `present_input_filter` notes each key press and left-button press it returns for
  posting (the adapters' outputs included; consumed events not) to the DOS sequencer
  (`pdos_seq_note_press`): these are the core's movie-skip input (11E0:0973, 0986).
* **Tests.** `test_input` 142 checks with the observer (+2: the noted presses; the default release off).

### 12.6 DOS assets at run time (D1 area E; phase D2)

Design of extension 5.9, 7.6, D-1 and 2.3 "No DOS bytes". Detailed design:
[verify/ext-d1-draft-E.md](verify/ext-d1-draft-E.md) (Revision 3; its interfaces are unchanged since
Revision 2, and D2 builds against them).

* **Files.** `present/dos/pdos_assets.[ch]` (the store and the only header renderers include),
  `pdos_shapes.[ch]` (the start-up cut O5:1455), `pdos_exe.[ch]` (MZ, EXEPACK, overlays, address
  spaces), `pdos_pic.[ch]` (`*.PIC`, `*.PAL`: LZW, RLE, X0/X1 rows), `pdos_font.[ch]` (`FONTS.CV`,
  the models of G#4 and G#12), `pdos_text.[ch]` (line files and the read-only section lookup),
  `pdos_tables.[ch]` and the generated `pdos_registry.[ch]`; `res/zip` gains `zip_find_base`;
  `host.h` gains `host_dos_read`, `host_dos_where` and `HOST_DOS_*` with both back ends
  (`hw_set_dos_source`, `hw_find_name`; `hh_set_dos_source`, `hh_dos_inject`); `gen/dos/registry.py`,
  `shapes.py`, `nodos.py`; `tests/dos-files.txt`, `tests/dos-decoded.txt`, `docs/dos/OFFSETS.md`,
  `tests/test_dos.c`. Every symbol takes the prefix `pdos_` / `Pdos` / `PDOS_` (`dos_` is the virtual
  DOS's).
* **The host service.** `int host_dos_read(const char *name, uint8_t *buf, uint32_t size)` and
  `const char *host_dos_where(void)` hide whether the source is a folder or an in-memory ZIP; the
  only access is read-only. Results `HOST_DOS_OK`, `_NONE`, `_MISSING`, `_SIZE`, `_ERROR`.
* **Activation.** `pdos_assets_open()` runs once, before `vm_init` (civ64 after `civ64_locate` and
  the data-directory rule; harness in `hh_run`), all or nothing: every pinned file read and checked
  by size and SHA-256, `CIV.EXE` unpacked (or `CIV_UNP.EXE` accepted, reachable headless) and checked
  against derived hashes, every registered table validated, fonts and texts parsed, every picture and
  palette decoded, the shape cut reproduced. Afterwards the store is immutable, allocation-free and
  fault-free; nothing reopens it during a run ("Locate DOS files..." acts at the next start).
  `pdos_assets_status()` and `pdos_assets_message()` give the result and one sentence naming the
  file; an explicit `--graphics dos` exits with code 2, otherwise the Windows profile runs.
* **Accessors.** Pictures and palettes (`pdos_pic`, `pdos_pal_dac`), fonts (`pdos_font`,
  `pdos_font_row`, `pdos_font_advance`, `pdos_font_line_height`), texts (`pdos_text_count`,
  `pdos_text_line(id, i, &len)`, the section lookup), tables (`pdos_table`, `pdos_table_u16` by
  registry id), shapes (`pdos_shape(PDOS_SH_<family>, dos_index)`, 15 families named after their DOS
  receivers, NULL for entries DOS never fills; `PDOS_POINTER_HOT_X/Y` 0; the page-2 art rectangle
  `PDOS_PAGE2_ART_*` read from `pdos_pic(PDOS_F_SP299_PIC)`). File ids are generated, one per pinned
  file with its extension (`PDOS_F_CREDITS_TXT`).
* **The offset registry.** Data tables are read only through `docs/dos/OFFSETS.md` rows, generated
  into C and checked by `registry.py check`; only initialised data (below DGROUP:6B30h, the far data
  segments, the driver's data) can be registered. Code immediates (rectangles, layouts, timer
  values) are C constants citing their DOS address (decision E.18-5, the user's X0 D6).
* **Prompt rules met.** 5.9 (the location rules of phase R, refusal of a DOS folder equal to the data
  directory, pins, the 6-bit palette delivered raw, failure behaviour), 2.3 and D-1 (no DOS byte in
  the repository or the binary: `nodos.py check`), 7.6 (decoders against independent goldens), P0
  (activation before the emulation touches clock, memory or queue; no emulated access), P8 (the DOS
  files are never written: no write handle exists; the section lookup runs without its cache write),
  memory safety (bounds on every decoder, E.11).
* **Choices** (one line each):
  * the `pdos_` prefix for every `present/dos` symbol;
  * every file checked and decoded at activation, before the emulation (E.18-4);
  * only decoded data kept, raw buffers freed after decoding;
  * decoders refuse only inputs whose DOS result would depend on stale memory or overrun a DOS buffer;
  * shapes reproduced from the decoded pictures by the cut table, rectangles as cited constants;
  * the TER257 row rule with DGROUP:1A3E taken as 0 (enough memory; recorded, E.17 E7);
  * an uncut shape entry draws nothing and counts `dos_shape_uncut`;
  * a DOS folder inside the data directory is accepted (E.18-1), equal to it refused.
