* **Live and drawn** (`pmap_layer.c`; D 5.1..5.5, 7.4). The layer's geometry and clip ("live": the view, the level, the
  anchors, the clip in host pixels) come from the layout of a frame of the core's size in the view (`pmap_core_layout`:
  the view's client and options, the core frame's size), not from the frame the visible profile shows, so they are the
  same in every profile. The image is drawn ("active") for the visible profile (`pmap_layer_profile`) only when the view
  is laid out for that frame (`present_layout_equal(present_view_layout(), L)`; otherwise `layout_wait`) and, in the DOS
  profile, while it presents its A frame (`pmap_dos_frame_ok`; otherwise `dos_frame`: an N frame, e.g. a DOS screen in
  play, is another picture) and `M_dos` is filled (`pmap_dos_inview_ready`; otherwise `dos_wait`). When it cannot be
  drawn the client goes back to the base frame (`undraw`) and the geometry stays live; a change of the drawn profile
  renders the whole clip. **Choice** (one geometry for both profiles): the DOS profile shows N frames (setup, DOS
  screens) and the A frame of the core's size, and the view is laid out per frame, so a geometry from the visible frame
  would differ by profile and by moment; the core frame's layout equals D6c's geometry whenever the Windows profile is
  visible (the Windows goldens are unchanged).
* **Sources** (`sample(prof, x, y)`). The Windows profile `M` / black / `G_win` as before; the DOS profile `M_dos` in
  view, DOS 0 beyond the poles, `G_dos` on the ring. The palette of the drawn profile (`palette_of`): the core's for
  Windows, the play palette as XRGB for DOS (`pmap_dos_xrgb`).
* **G_dos, the DOS ring** (`pmap_world.c`, `pmap_dos.c`; D 14.9, 14.5 "In the DOS ring"). 1280 x 800 DOS indices
  (1,024,000 bytes, allocated at the first -1 update with the DOS profile registered), one per DOS art pixel, which
  covers world pixels (2i..2i+1, 2j..2j+1) (14.1); square (x, y) is the 16 x 16 cell at (16x, 16y). As `G_win`: one
  `PmapDosKey` per square (`pmap_dos_key`: the inputs the DOS rules read, the core's decisions through the Windows
  readers of the snapshot), recomputed when the snapshot's gen changes; a square whose key changed is cleared to DOS 0
  and drawn again by `pmap_dos_ring_square` (RedrawMapSquare's two calls: R:1AEA:03BA's layers, then the top unit
  through R:1AEA:0E29 with the stack shadow; black when unseen), then every label whose pixels meet the cell, clipped to
  it (equal to a whole redraw at every update). Labels: `G_win`'s list (the core's label policy, column-major,
  east-west wrap) drawn at 14.5's ring position (R:1AEA:0174..0180 px - 8, R:1AEA:0168..016C py + 10h, in DOS pixels)
  without R:1DC4:007C's clamp and R:1F4D:04F7's truncation (those belong to the DOS screen's edges), colour 0Bh with
  its shadow (R:1AEA:0164..018D, R:0182:0086); a label past the east edge wraps to the west; a changed, added or
  removed label draws the squares under its old and new pixels again. The DOS rules draw `G_dos` through a k = 1 pen
  (`g_pk`, `pen_at`): one DOS pixel per `G_dos` pixel; the layer's `m = 2·s_l` gives each DOS pixel k x k host pixels
  at -1 (4.1/4.2). `pmap_world_update` brings the canvas of the layer's profile up to date at each tick and core present
  (as before for `G_win`); the other canvas follows when `pmap_layer_sample` samples it. **Choice:** the hidden
  profile's ring is brought up to date lazily, at its samples (`G_dos` costs `world_dos_us` per update and only the
  shown canvas is needed for the picture); known limit: after a switch at -1 the newly shown ring is brought up to
  date at the next tick or core present (at most one tick, 1/60 s, old). Each canvas damages the layer only while the
  layer shows it. Diagnostics: `world_dos_updates`, `world_dos_squares`, `world_dos_us`.
* **M_dos, the DOS in-view source** (`pmap_dos.c`, `pmap_inview.c`; D 6.2). The map client's DOS pixels at map pixels:
  `Wn_dos` where the client is visible (the map window's visible region), `P_dos` where it is covered; since MZS
  (F190, `md_fill`) each shadow's pixels where it has them (the overlap at the same client coordinates), M_dos's
  previous pixel where neither has one, DOS 0 only where M_dos never had one (`dos_port_size` counts the presents with
  a size that differs; before MZS DOS 0 where a shadow was missing or of another size); `Wn_dos` is the DOS rule set's
  shadow of the window, not the screen, so it never holds the outline's XOR band. Filled at every core present in every profile
  (`pmap_dos_inview_present`, after D6c's `pmap_dos_present`), only the rows that changed (`dos_fills`); their damage
  reaches the layer only while it is drawn for the DOS profile. Freed at level 0 and taken whole when the level leaves
  0. **Choice:** a copy (client-sized) rather than reads through the shadows at render time: `Wn_dos` and `P_dos`
  change at the core's draws and the layer renders at shows, so a copy at the core present keeps the in-view source
  consistent with the ring's snapshot instant, as `M` is for the Windows profile.
* **The play palette's steps** (14.7). A new play-palette generation while the DOS layer is drawn makes the layer
  pending (`pmap_pending`) and the overlay converts the image again (`dos_palette`, `n_dos_reconverts`): under nearest
  the indices are kept and converted (5.3), under `fit` sharp the XRGB image is rendered again (M's deviation, now also
  for the play palette; below).
* **Evaluation at profile-independent instants** (`pmap_inview.c`, `pmap_zoom.c`; 7.4). At every core present and
  every tick, whichever profile is visible, the layer is evaluated (`pmap_layer_update`) with the show's request
  (`recheck`) left as it was, so that the shows are D6c's; `M`'s changes and the core's palette damage the layer only
  while it is drawn for the Windows profile. The active-unit follow rule (+1) and the wheel's anchor use the live
  geometry, drawn or not; the +1 refusal (below 3 x 3 squares) uses the core layout. So the layer's level, origin and
  clip evolve alike in every profile and with switching.
* **A-6** (`pmap_layer_sample(prof, &w, &h)`, `pmap.h`). The layer of either profile rendered whole over the clip's
  bounding box, now, from that profile's sources and as XRGB in that profile's current palette, whichever profile is
  visible; at -1 that profile's world canvas is brought up to date first. NULL when the level is 0, the layer is not
  live or that profile's in-view source is missing; under `CIV_OBSERVER 0` a stub (NULL). The harness's `profiles.log`
  `sample` lines carry ` map <profile> <sha256>` for each registered profile whose layer sample exists (XRGB bytes,
  4 w x h), replacing D6c's single ` map <sha>` of the drawn image. **Choice:** per profile, fresh and as XRGB: an index
  image would hide palette steps and differ between nearest and sharp, and the drawn image exists only for the visible
  profile.
* **Diagnostics.** `presentation-diag.log`'s `pmap` line gains `live drawn_dos dos_frame dos_wait layout_wait
  dos_port_size dos_fills dos_reconverts world_dos_updates world_dos_squares world_dos_us` (its buffer 1024 -> 2048
  bytes; the longest line seen privately 623 bytes); at level 0 `live 0`, `drawn_dos 0` and every new counter 0
  (`world_dos_us` is host time). `pmap_int.h`: the D6d API; `pmap.h`: the sample entry.
* **OPEN L12** (from the listing; D 14.7, B 5.5). L12 asks whether the map's cycling runs over faded entries. In the
  play return path R:0238:1BEB the DOS program stops the map slots (R:0238:1B48 calls R:1DC4:065F) before the fade-out
  (R:0000:04D4, at R:0238:1B66), stops them again (R:0238:1BFC) before the fade-in (R:0000:04AA, at R:0238:1C86, which
  busy-waits until the fade completes) and restarts them only after it (R:0238:1C8E, R:1DC4:0626), without capturing
  the colours again. So the slots never cycle over faded entries in that path, and the A machine has no fade in play
  in D6d: nothing to model. The restart without a capture is D6cF's R3 (for D6e, below). L11 (entry 45, the
  `BACK*.PAL` entries) is D6e's (the play palette's life cycle). **Read in D6e:** R:0238:1BEB writes no entry 45 (the
  forced colour belongs to R:1DC4:047D's recipe, not the play return), and the differing `BACK*.PAL` entries are
  applied as 128..255 by the recipe.
* **D6cF** (the D6c review's follow-ups; [record](ext-D6d-record.md) "D6cF", [ext-D6c-record.md](ext-D6c-record.md)
  "Review"). The user's other chat verified D6c's palette cycling read-only against the listing (slots, direction,
  step, rate against CyclePaletteColors 1208:22B7, cycles off, switching): as designed and matching the listing, no
  defect. **R2 (fixed):** `pdos_replay.c` `nearest_rgb` searched the whole DAC, so on the A machine's DAC a replayed
  colour could map to a play slot 60h..7Fh and then cycle with the slots; the search now skips 60h..7Fh on the A DAC
  (`dac == &pdos_ma()->dac`), the N machine's DAC (no play slots) is searched whole (12.7 "As implemented (D6b; area
  B)"; FIDELITY F186). **G2 (tests):** `test_pdos_profile`'s `test_play_cycle` and `test_map`'s map element composed
  again on a step. **R1 (recorded as intended):** the step is taken at CyclePaletteColors' entry probe, before the
  core's `g_noPaletteDevice` early return (seg066_1208_p2_r.c:33..36): under the 16-colour switch the DOS slots step
  while Windows' do not; DOS-faithful, since the DOS program cycles its slots regardless of a Windows palette device
  (FIDELITY F133). **G1, G3:** FIDELITY F133's clause; draft D 14.7's direction (each entry takes its lower
  neighbour's colour) proven by the listing (R:0000:0471..04A1, the slot rotation), no longer inferred; checklist
  D6c-5's wording (only 96..111 visibly cycle on the map). **R3 (for D6e):** the DOS return path R:0238:1BEB restarts
  the slots through R:1DC4:0626 / 03FA without capturing again (the position is kept); draft B 5.5 item 2's
  `pdos_play_back` captures again and resets to position 0. D6e keeps the position or records a deviation.
  **Resolved in D6e:** `pdos_play_back` keeps the position (12.7 "As implemented (D6e; area B)").
* **Runners** (`tests/harness/ext_zoom.py`, `ext_p0.py`, `ext_adapt.py`; D 16.3..16.5). **ext_zoom** per combination
  (the scenarios `new`, `load`, `orders` at the five host sizes and policies, the soak S4 at 1920x1440; levels -1 and
  +1): `win a` (no DOS profile: the Windows goldens' run, unchanged) and `win b` (`--dos-data`, the DOS profile
  registered and hidden) with equal zoomed shows; `dos a` (`--dos-data --graphics dos`) at every size of the three
  scenarios (the DOS goldens) and `dos b` at 1920x1440 with shows equal to dos a's (Z-2 for the DOS layer); `sw a`
  (`--dos-data --switch-every 997`) at 1920x1440, the scenarios and the soak, with at least one switch; in every DOS run
  no refused switch, the dos runs starting in the DOS profile; `profiles.log`'s `map windows` and `map dos` hashes equal
  in win b, dos a, dos b and sw (and `map windows` in win a) at every common sample time, sw with a sample of both.
  Options `--profiles`, `--sizes`, `--exe`, `--root`, `--port`; `--update` writes both profiles' goldens (only with
  every size and scenario): a third header line and 240 `dos` lines after the 388 Windows lines, which are unchanged.
  **Choice** (cost): the full product would be about 2.5 times D6c's section time; the soak runs as win a/b and sw,
  and its DOS layer is checked through sw's `map dos` samples against win b's (measured privately: the soak's dos a
  and b at -1 had equal shows and per-profile samples, equal to the switching soak's, 393 switches, at all 78 sample
  times). **ext_p0** V4d: V4 (`--host-size 1920x1440`, the zoom commands every 1,301 ms cycling +1, -1, reset, 0) with
  `--dos-data build\dosdata --switch-every 997`, every V4 run; P0 against V0, the switch assertions (switches >= 1,
  refused 0), no `zoom refused`, and over all V4d runs the layer drawn in at least one show of each profile; in the
  default Checked list (`est=760`). **Choice:** one combined variation, which shows each profile half of the time; the
  DOS-only zoom determinism is ext_zoom's. **ext_adapt**: `zoomkeys-dos` (zoomkeys' host lines plus the profile chord
  at 40,000 ms, turn 1, in play) equals `plain`, every zoom chord and wheel line `events 0` with its command, the chord
  `cmd profile`, one switch, refused 0, the layer drawn in DOS shows; `ring-dos` (ring's lines plus the chord, at
  `--zoom -1`) equals `ringref` (the ring click at 59,400 ms on the DOS layer's ring gives the small-map click),
  ring_press 1, ring_refused 0, one switch, the DOS layer drawn. **Choice:** the chord at 40,000 ms keeps the S3 host
  forms (intro, menus, setup) valid and puts every zoom chord, wheel line and the ring click in the DOS profile.

* **The menu bar** (B 11.2, P24 as built). One A element at scale 1 over the Windows bar rectangle of the shown
  top-level window with a menu (`w16q_menu_bar`: its menu, the bar and the titles' rectangles as w16 draws them), id
  `PDOS_R_BAR_ID` (FF01h). Fill 0 (R:0238:0FEA..0FFD); font 1 at scale 1 (B15); each title at its Windows title
  rectangle's left + 8 (w16's MENU_PAD), vertically centred; clipped by the windows above. File, Orders, Advisors,
  World and Civilopedia show the DOS titles of the registry's MENU_TITLES (DGROUP:1D6F, five strings, the pushes at
  R:0238:1011, 1029, 1041, 1059, 1071) raw in colour 15, the 87h switch giving the rest colour 7; a title is paired by
  its popup's commands (`pdos_r_menu_title`: the hundreds of the first command id). Edit, City and Help: capitals with
  8Fh before the mnemonic letter and 87h after it (B14). The highlighted title (the menu loop's selection on the bar,
  `w16q_menu_state`; not the system menu) is recoloured 0 -> 11 over its title rectangle. Drawn only when the bar's
  window, its menu, a title, its text or the highlight change (`bar_draws`). (Replaced in D6fS-b: the DOS bar at 2x
  with the DOS titles at their DOS x, the DOS zones and no highlight, Choice B15 reversed; "As implemented (D6fS-b)"
  below.)
* **The drop-downs** (B 11.2; OPEN OB5, L19, L25; ruling 2). Each popup of the running w16 menu loop but the system
  menu is a DOS menu box in font 1 at 2x anchored at the Windows popup's top-left (`w16_menu_track_item`, read only),
  ids `PDOS_R_DROP_ID` + level (FF11h..FF17h). **Choice D6f-2:** the box is at least the Windows popup's size with
  w16's 2-pixel shadow, and every row covers its Windows item (reversed in D6fS-a: the box as large as its content,
  the rest of the popup hidden; "As implemented (D6fS-a)" below). One row per `w16q_menu_item` (ruling 2): separators as
  empty disabled rows, grayed or disabled items in colour 3, checked items with the DOS mark '^' (R:1D05:0939..0951 for
  the bits of DGROUP:D7F2; `PdosMenuBox.marks`), the loop's highlighted item as the box's highlight. An item paired by
  role takes the DOS row's text (GAME DGROUP:2BD4; Options DGROUP:2C3E with its header line; ADVISORS DGROUP:2DD6; WORLD
  DGROUP:2E5D; CIVILOPEDIA DGROUP:2ED1; the registry rows MENU_GAME, MENU_OPTIONS, MENU_ADVISORS, MENU_WORLD,
  MENU_PEDIA). **Choice D6f-3:** an unpaired item (ORDERS, built per square at R:1C84:01D8; Edit, City, Help) shows the
  core's display text in DOS font 1 and, after a space, its accelerator in colour 15. Retire's View Replay has no core
  item and is not shown. A drop-down is redrawn when the loop's selection or the popup's items change, removed when its
  popup closes. R1 and R2 (the Windows-only menus, separators and grayed ORDERS rows) have no owner unit (FIDELITY F196,
  F197). (Replaced in D6fS-b: the DOS menus' own boxes with their DOS rows at their DOS places and the item map; the
  rest of Choice D6f-2 and Choice D6f-3 reversed, R1 and R2 settled; "As implemented (D6fS-b)" below.)
* **The A placement of D5b's re-skins** (11.3, Choice B16; decision 1, ruling 1; ext-D4a C1). While the frame
  presented is A (`pdos_compose_kind`), the generic boxes of the MenuZ dialogs 136 and 145 (rows 14 and 22) and every

  * **C.15.4 case 13:** a drag's release decides at the press's frame; a release on another frame (the map layer's

**As implemented (DBL; areas B and D).** Record [ext-DBL-record.md](ext-DBL-record.md) (session E4, 2026-10-17; the
user's revision list of about 07:00 UTC, items 1 and 2, "DOS units blinks at slightly faster rate" and "End of turn
blinks at slightly faster rate", clarified about 07:50 UTC: "I meant 'the real DOS game blinks faster'"; D6e-5 failed
on item 2). Staged on the D7a+D7b tree and rebased onto UX5b's `test.cmd` and D7c's
`pdos_profile.c` (three-way merges, clean but for one adjacent-line conflict in `dos_advance`, both lines kept);
landed 2026-10-17 after D7c (the copy-in 11:50:46 UTC; the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 12:28:57 UTC,
`build/dbl-status.txt`; Release 12:35:29 UTC, user build `build/user-builds/dbl/`); compiled and tested headless in
Checked (CheckedNoObs and Release privately in the stage); P0 holds (`P0_OK checks 476` unchanged). FIDELITY F133
(changed), F220 (the clock's rules), F191 (the "End of Turn" blink noted).

* **The DOS game's blink** (DOS listing, R:0866:0AD6..0CF4; called by the main loop R:0403:0655 before its key read).
  Phase A: the square without the unit (R:1AEA:03BA), the small-map pixel in colour 15, "End of Turn" in colour 7, a
  cursor square outlined in 15; phase B: the unit (R:1AEA:0E29), the small-map pixel in 1 (ocean) or 2, "End of Turn"
  in 0, the cursor in 0; each followed by wait(n), n = 1 when a key waits, else 0Ah (R:0866:0C00..0C12, 0CB9..0CD0),
  the loop leaving after a phase B only (R:0866:0CD8). wait(n) counts VGA frames (TIMING.md 1, 3.3: 70.086 Hz), so on
  the DOS clock model (`pdos_clock.h` M1, M2) a phase is 10 frames, 142.68 ms (142,677.3 us), a cycle 285.35 ms. The
  base build blinked at the core's TickCount() % 60 (a whole blink a second, about 3.5 times slower).
* **The clock** (`present/dos/pdos_ublink.h/.c`, new, portable, pure; 299 portable sources). `PdosUb` on its own M1
  grid: phase A for frames 0..9, B for 10..19 of every 20 from the grid's start (`pdos_ub_phase_at`, `pdos_ub_next`;
  `PDOS_UB_PHASE_FRAMES` 10). Started at BlinkActiveUnitUntilInput's entry with the core's civ and unit (phase A at
  once), stopped at the core's end of the loop. `pdos_ub_update(t, held)`: while held (a host modal loop: a menu
  tracked, `w16q_menu_state`; a window moved or sized, `w16q_sys_track`) phase B; at the hold's end the grid starts
  again with phase A (inferred: DOS calls R:0866:0AD6 again after a menu). No emulation read, no random number.
* **"End of Turn" and the small-map square** (`pdos_aplay.c`). At BlinkActiveUnitUntilInput's entry (unit 0..80h)
  `blink_start` reads the unit's square and shows phase A; `pdos_aplay_advance(now)`, called by the DOS profile's
  advance at every presentation tick whether or not the DOS graphics show (`pdos_profile.c` `dos_advance`), shows each
  change of phase: "End of Turn" in DOS colour 7 (A) or 0 (B), the Status column drawn again; the small-map square in
  15 (A) or 1/2 (B: ocean by the core's terrain of the square, read as GetTerrainType reads it). Meanwhile the core's
  own blink drawing (its PlotSmMapSquare plots and "End of Turn" TEXTOUTs outside the panel functions) is ignored and
  counted (`blink_core`); a panel drawn again during the blink keeps the DOS phase. The end (DrawUnit at the loop's
  end, or the function's exit) shows phase B and stops the clock; the core's later plots apply as before. `def_modal`
  gives the hold.
* **The main map's square** (`pmap_dos.c`, `pmap_model.c`, `pmap_inview.c`, `pmap_int.h`). BlinkActiveUnitUntilInput's
  entry begins the map's blink for a unit below 80h (`pmap_dos_blink_begin`). At the first write the two pictures are
  drawn by the DOS rules (`pmap_model_blink_pics`, with `map_tile_dos_surf` split out): on a scratch surface of
  P_dos's size holding P_dos's square and a 2-pixel margin, the square as the model draws DrawMapTile (the core's hide)
  gives picture A, then the unit as the model draws DrawUnit (the core's show, with the stack shadow) picture B, 32 x 32
  each at the square's position, clipped to the flushes' rectangle. At every core present (before the map element is
  set) and every tick the picture of the clock's phase is written over that square of Wn_dos, compare-first, with its
  damage (`pmap_dos_blink_apply`, before `pmap_dos_present` in `core_present` and before `pmap_world_update` in
  `pmap_tick`); at a tick M_dos (the zoom layer's source) follows. P_dos keeps the core's own drawing (the dos-settled
  check reads it); a map drawn again during the blink has the pictures drawn again at the next write
  (`pmap_dos_blink_stale`). `PmapDosBlinkDiag` counts begins, renders, writes and stops. Nothing when
  g_autoFlushToWindow is 0, at a scaled port or with a dialog port (the model draws neither).
* **Not modelled, recorded** (FIDELITY F220): the 1-frame phases while a key waits (the core leaves at the key, and
  the end shows phase B as DOS's loop does); the first wait's fraction of a frame (the first phase exactly 10 frames
  from the core's entry, at most 14.3 ms longer); the cursor square (the core's blink has none); the small-map row
  limit 0..49 (the Windows World Map shows the full map).
* **Windows graphics and P0.** The Windows graphics keep the core's blink; the DOS shadows and M_dos are DOS-only
  (M_dos damages the layer only while it shows the DOS profile). The clock runs while the Windows graphics show, so a
  switch keeps each profile's pace. Every read is a `w16q_*` query or the model's PmapSrc; written are only the
  module's memory, the DOS shadows and M_dos; no random number, no event, nothing the core reads. DBL uses the existing
  probes (BlinkActiveUnitUntilInput, DrawUnit, PlotSmMapSquare, TEXTOUT, DrawTilesToPort, RedrawMapRect): no probe,
  call site, registry or adapter row is added.
* **Tests.** `test_pdos_play` 78 (32 DBL checks: `test_ub_clock`, the 10- and 20-frame boundaries, the hold and the new
  grid; `test_blink_eot`, "End of Turn" in 7 and 0, the core's TEXTOUTs ignored, the panel drawn again, the exit;
  `test_blink_unit`, the small-map square in 15, land 2 and ocean 1, the core's plots ignored, the end at DrawUnit),
  `ext_dbl.py` (test.cmd's new Checked section `ext_dbl`, three runs from CAE3540B.SAV: the DOS unit and its World Map

* **The cause** (DOS oracle: none; the port's own frames, a private research build with a tick dump per presented
  frame). Ctrl+T, then Return or Escape, in S3: one frame showed the DOS box with its header only (MenuZ destroys its
  item controls 500.. before it hides its dialog, and D6f redrew the box from the controls left), the next two the
  Windows dialog 136 whole (D6f dropped the A box at the dialog's OBS_WND_HIDE, while the core frame still held the
  dialog's pixels until the windows beneath had repainted; under Choice D6f-6 only the box, as large as the dialog, had
  hidden them). The same for the luxuries rate, the revolution question and the message boxes; a drop-down at least
  its popup's size hid the popup while it was open.
* **The DOS sizes** (Choices D6f-2 and D6f-6 reversed; FIDELITY F226). DOS's menu box R:1D05:0000 sizes itself from
  its content: its right edge x + the widest line's width + 8 (R:1D05:059D..05A7), its bottom y + lines x the line
  height + 6 (R:1D05:05AA..05B7), the outline around that rectangle (R:1D05:09F0..09FD and its callee R:1D05:0A05), as
  D5b's `pdos_menubox_layout` (unchanged) computes; the tax box (R:0403:35CE..368B: "Select new Tax rate" and one line
  per allowed rate, R:0238:001E at (100, 80)) is no larger than its lines. `pdos_reskin.c`'s `min_size` and
  `min_size_win` are removed with `PdosRSpec`'s `min_w` / `min_h` and the redraw when the Windows window's size
  changed; `PdosRPlace.win` stays (the diagnostics and the hidden window's rectangle).
* **The covers** (the hidden set; FIDELITY F227; Choice D6fS-a-1). Members: a Windows popup whose DOS drop-down is
  shown (the menu loop's levels 1.. that `drops_update` replaces; the system menu, which has no drop-down, is not one)
  and a Windows window whose DOS box replaces it on A (a `#MsgBox`, and the dialog of a MenuZ box of dialogs 136 and
  145 bound to a re-skin row, plain and help templates). A window joins at its OBS_WND_SHOW (`wnd_cover`; w16 sends it
  before the window's first drawing, `user_wnd.c` show_sequence), a popup when its drop-down is first drawn
  (`popup_cover`). The underlay: for each member the screen pixels that lay under it when it appeared (a popup's the
  menu loop's saved pixels with its shadow, `w16q_menu_saved`; a window's the virtual screen under its rectangle at its
  show, `w16q_screen`), kept as w16 indices in one screen-sized surface (a pixel already under an earlier member keeps
  that member's value). The underlay is one A element, `PDOS_R_COVER_ID` (0xFF20), composed with the core's palette (a
  copy of `w16q_system_rgb`, updated at each core present) directly over the A frame's base and below every other A
  element (`pdos_compose_elem_set_under`, new), clipped to the members' rectangles (at most `PDOS_R_COVERS` 12: seven
  popup levels, four boxes' windows and a spare; an open beyond refused and counted, `cover_full`).
* **The A consumers draw under it.** The DOS art on A where a hidden window is (the DOS map element, the zoom layer in
  the DOS profile, D6e's World Map, Status and unit panel elements and the bar) is clipped by the visible regions of
  their Windows windows computed without the hidden windows and every popup (`w16q_wnd_vis_skip`,
  `w16q_wnd_client_vis_skip`; `pmap.h` `PmapMap.a_set`, `navis`, `avis`, filled in `pmap_inview.c`; `pmap_a_vis`,
  `pmap_screen_exclusions_a`; `pdos_aplay.c` `def_wnd`; `pmap_layer.c` `layer_vis`, `make_clip`'s profile argument),
  so their DOS art is drawn over the underlay as the screen beneath would show it. A popup that is not hidden (the
  system menu) is excluded from each element by its exact three rectangles, the frame and the two 2-pixel shadow
  strips, as `user_menutrack.c` takes them out of every window's visible region (`pdos_reskin_popups_shown`; at most
  four such popups per element, `PDOS_EXCL_MAX` 12). The DOS shadows already hold the picture beneath a popup (w16
  clips window output away from popups); under a window, what the windows beneath drew before it covered them. The
  mirror check (`pmap_check.c`) keeps the real regions.
* **Closing and the hold** (FIDELITY F228; Choice D6fS-a-2). A member hidden (OBS_WND_HIDE) or destroyed
  (OBS_WND_DESTROY), or a popup closed: its DOS box goes at once (the box's drop before the cover is marked) and its
  cover becomes closing; a closing window's handle is no longer skipped (it may be reused). A closing cover stays
  until a core present at which no shown window meeting its rectangle has an update region pending
  (`w16q_upd_pending`). While a cover is closing the A frame holds its rectangle (`pdos_compose_hold`,
  `PDOS_HOLD_MAX` 12): a held pixel keeps what the A composition showed when the hold was set (setting a new set first
  composes everything pending, so the held picture is the DOS picture of the close with the box removed); what changes
  beneath is composed at the release; a box opening inside a held area shows at the release. The covers and the hold
  live whether or not the DOS graphics show: setting a hold composes the private A frame then, with the last composed
  core frame or, before any, the one `pdos_compose_set_core(present_core_xrgb())` names at each core present
  (`pdos_profile.c`'s core-present hook; the include rule lets only `pdos_profile.c` include `present.h`), so a profile
  sample or a switch during a hold shows the same picture; a private A frame made anew composes its held rectangles
  too. M_dos (the zoom layer's source, `pmap_dos.c` `md_fill`) takes P_dos's pixels instead of Wn_dos's under a
  closing cover (Wn_dos there is the map window's repaint in progress), filled again whenever `pdos_r_cover_gen`
  changes (`cgen`); with DBL's blink, a square under a closing cover shows the core's last picture for that moment,
  then the DOS phase again. A new screen size drops every cover (F105); `pdos_reskin_reset` drops them all.
* **The MenuZ box's close** (FIDELITY F230; Choice D6fS-a-4). The DOS box of a MenuZ dialog of 136 or 145 on A goes
  at the first OBS_WND_DESTROY of one of its item controls (ids 500..520; `item_gone`), so no frame shows it without
  its rows; DOS closes its box at once on the choice, its caller redrawing the screen beneath (R:0238:1B44;
  R:0866:25D7 for the menus).
* **A press in a hidden popup outside its drop-down** (FIDELITY F229; Choice D6fS-a-3). Such a press or move is passed
  to the menu loop as a press at a screen corner off the bar and every popup (`pdos_reskin_menu_point` returns 3;
  (0, h - 1) first): the Windows menu loop ends at a press outside its popups and eats it (`user_menutrack.c`), and a
  move there selects nothing. DOS's row is -1 outside the box's x range (R:1D05:01A1..01B2); the press-outside
  behaviour of the whole DOS box loop is D6fS-b's (ruling 2, M2).
* **Read-only w16 queries** (12.5, 12.10): `w16q_wnd_vis_skip`, `w16q_wnd_client_vis_skip` (`user_wnd.c`
  `w16_wnd_vis_skip`: the visible region without the overlays and the skipped top-level windows with their
  descendants; -1 for an invalid handle), `w16q_upd_pending` (`w16_wnd_upd_pending`) and `w16q_menu_saved`
  (`user_menutrack.c` `w16_menu_track_saved`); obs_lint's `REVIEWED_CALLS` names the three getters (read only, no
  allocation, no side effect). Core, rt and w16 behaviour are unchanged (P0).
* **Test infrastructure.** `present/profile.h` `PRESENT_TICK_EVERY`, a tick clock mode with no throttle (every damaged
  tick presented), handled in `present.c`, whose `present_set_tick_clock` now also restarts the throttle; the
  headless harness's `--tick-period-us N` (0..1000000, set after `present_reset`; 0 is `PRESENT_TICK_EVERY`, N the
  virtual clock's throttle period), so with `--presented 2 --dump-presented` every presented A frame of a close is
  dumped. Without it the 16,667 us virtual throttle as before; the Win32 host never uses `PRESENT_TICK_EVERY`.
* **The diag line.** presentation-diag.log's `preskin` line gains, after D6f's fields, `drop2_ax`, `drop2_ay`,
  `drop2_w`, `drop2_hh`, `popup_l/t/r/b`, `popup2_l/t/r/b` (the last level-1 and level-2 hidden popups with their
  shadow), `cover_opens`, `cover_closes`, `cover_releases`, `cover_kept` (core presents at which a closing cover
  stayed) and `cover_full`.
* **Limits** (the record's "Deviations and limits"): a window's underlay is the virtual screen at its show, so a
  Windows-only area beneath a box (a part of the desktop) shows its Windows pixels as before the box opened; a core
  present inside the repainting window's WM_PAINT after BeginPaint would release a cover early (inferred; not seen in
  the runs); a deeper system menu leaves its fifth level's exclusion out; the neutral point is a screen corner.
* **Tests.** `test_pdos_reskin` 121 -> 147 (CheckedNoObs 136, the observer-only cover checks compiled out;
  `test_drop`: the drop-down 47 x 33, its content's size, the row-2 point, the neutral point; `test_awin`: the A box
  35 x 33 and its A rectangles; `test_covers`: the underlay's values, an earlier cover's pixel kept, an element above
  the underlay, the palette change, close, kept and release with the held core pixel, the screen clip, no source, a
  new screen size, `PDOS_R_COVERS` in use, the reset), `test_pdos_compose` 68 -> 90 (`test_a_under`, `test_a_hold`),
  `test_observer` 115 -> 126 (Release 115; `w16q_menu_saved` at OBS_MENU_OPEN, the popup's centre out of
  `w16q_wnd_vis` and in `w16q_wnd_vis_skip`, a skipped window, the client form, an invalid handle,
  `w16q_upd_pending`), `test_present` 951 -> 956 (`PRESENT_TICK_EVERY`), `test_map` 338 -> 342 (M_dos under a closing
  cover); `ext_adapt.py`'s `d6fs_runs` (after `d6f_runs`): seven cases in play in the DOS graphics, S3's lines in host
  form with the profile chord at 40,000 ms and `--tick-period-us 0 --presented 2 --dump-presented`, each box opened at
  55,500 ms and closed before S3's Space at 58,000 ms: f6s-tax-ret (Ctrl+T, Return), f6s-tax-esc, f6s-lux-esc,
  f6s-rev-esc (dialog 145), f6s-msgbox (the Game Help `#MsgBox`, Return), f6s-game (Alt+G, Escape twice) and
  f6s-options (levels 1 and 2, Escape three times); per case four checks (the run, the covers opened and released;
  every frame of the transitions, 250 ms after each step and 700 ms after the last, shows over the hidden window or
  popups only values a settled state shows there or, within 4 A pixels of a place the input-free settled frames show
  animating, one of its animated colours; while open, the hidden part beside the DOS element as the screen before and
  after; the box or drop-down as large as its content), and the P0 line (f6s-tax-esc without `--tick-period-us` and
  `--dump-presented` has the same core input 39, frames 667 and events 104). f6-zcover-m1 and -p1 are rewritten (the
  hidden dialog's part beside the smaller box shows each pixel as one of the six views before the box; at +1 the
  layer where the A frame differs from them), f6-zcover-menu states the content size. On the base (the tree with only
  the test infrastructure) every f6s case fails: the tax box's close shows 69,965 other pixels (58,197 in one frame,
  the whole dialog 136), the luxuries box 50,982, the revolution question 33,378, the Game Help box 12,280; a
  colour-only animation rule was too lax and let the base's message box pass. User checks D6fSa-1..D6fSa-3 and the
  rewritten D5-7 and D6f-8.
* **At the landing** (`build/d6fsa-prep/compare.py` against D7j's outputs, 58 of 65 files equal, host times left
  out): as predicted, `test_map` 338 -> 342, `test_observer` 115 -> 126, `test_pdos_compose` 68 -> 90,
  `test_pdos_reskin` 121 -> 147, `test_present` 951 -> 956, every other count equal; `EXT_ADAPT_OK checks` 208 -> 237
  (the 29 f6s lines; f6-zcover-m1 and -p1 with D6fS-a's texts: the tax box at A 203,101..485,311, its content's
  size, 59,220 of its pixels the A frame's, the hidden dialog's part beside it as the views before the box;
  f6-zcover-menu unchanged); OBS_LINT_OK files 132 probes 203 callsites 101, ADAPT_OK rows 130 steps 25,
  OBS_TABLES_OK, `pdos_scene_check` 1,084, REGISTRY_OK, EXT_FRAMES_OK, ZOOM_OK (no golden change), EXT_DBL_OK,
  EXT_TIMELINE_OK, `P0_OK checks 476`, `REPLAY_OK checks 70`, `MIRROR_OK runs 29` and every other section unchanged;
  `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` unchanged; 300 portable sources; no build.cmd or test.cmd change. Informational, as
  the predictions allowed: coverage's "other functions entered" 671 -> 664; NODOS_OK 1,779 -> 1,780 files (the
  record), 0 hits; unit.log's OBS_COST line (host timing).

**As implemented (D6fS-b).** Record [ext-D6fS-b-record.md](ext-D6fS-b-record.md) (session E4, 2026-10-17; the
orchestrator's rulings of about 08:20 UTC, H2, M2, 3 and 4): the second half of the rework of D6f's DOS menus in play,
from the user's answer to D6-1 of 2026-10-17 ("Changed my mind draw top menu at 2x, they all have to be consistent.")
and the revision list of about 07:00 UTC, items 4 ("In game DOS Menus highlights selection, in real DOS game there is
no highlighting present.") and 5 ("DOS Menus still uses windows menu contents, DOS graphics shall exactly reflect DOS
game menu and its contents"): the DOS bar at 2x, the DOS game's own menus with their DOS rows at their DOS places
(ORDERS built per unit and square), each row doing its job through the Windows command that does it, and the DOS keys,
mouse and highlight. Staged on the tree after D6fS-a's landing and rebased onto D7d's, D7l's and D7m's landings and the
docs pass e4d (`git merge-file -p` three-way merges, the conflicts adjacent additions resolved with both sides kept; the
registry's outputs and DOS-PROFILE.md's adapter section regenerated, not merged); landed alone after D7m and e4d (the
bases rechecked, `build/d6fsb-prep/land.py` DRY_RUN_OK 22; the 18 changed and 3 new files with the record copied at
17:44:37 UTC, `build/d6fsb-prep/landed.sha256`; the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 18:16:46 UTC, `build/d6fsb-status.txt`;
Release 18:20:37 UTC, user build `build/user-builds/d6fsb/`); compiled and tested headless in Checked (CheckedNoObs and
Release privately in the stage); P0 holds (`P0_OK checks 476` unchanged). FIDELITY F251..F257 (F123, F196, F197 and
F198 changed: Choice B15, the rest of Choice D6f-2 and Choices D6f-3 and D6f-5 reversed, report items R1 and R2
settled; F172, F200..F202 and F229 noted). With D6fS-a the rework D6fS is complete. Edit, City and Help have no DOS
form: they are in the Windows graphics only (the user's DOS rule of 2026-10-16); no file of UX5b and no build.cmd or
test.cmd line is changed (the new `tests/test_pdos_menu.c` is built and run by their `tests\test_*.c` loops).

* **The bar** (FIDELITY F251; DOS R:0238:0FEA..1071). `pdos_menu_bar_draw` (new `present/dos/pdos_menu.c/.h`, pure
  portable C including only `pdos_*.h`) draws the DOS bar on a surface of ceil(w / 2) x ceil(h / 2) for a Windows bar
  of w x h A pixels, put on the A canvas at 2x over the Windows bar of the shown top-level window with a menu (D6f's
  element, `bar_update`): every row filled in colour 0, the five titles of the registry's MENU_TITLES at their DOS x 8,
  64, 128, 192, 240, y 1, font 1 from colour 15 with the 87h switch; no Edit, City or Help title and no title
  highlight (H2). D6f's 1x drawing (`pdos_r_bar_draw`, `pdos_r_bar_title`, `pdos_r_menu_title`) and ruling 2's paired
  texts (`pdos_r_drop_dos_text`, `pdos_r_drop_dos_header`) are removed; `PdosRBar` keeps the Windows titles' rectangles
  with each title's DOS menu (`menu`, `pdos_menu_of`) for the item map. **The zones** (`pdos_menu_zone`,
  `pdos_reskin_bar_zone`): the logical bar x = (A x - the bar's left) / 2; the menu x / 60 clamped to 0..4
  (R:1C84:0019..002E), except 184..239 WORLD (**Choice D6fS-b-1**, ruling 3; DOS 180, the ADVISORS title being drawn
  over 128..183); -2 for ORDERS without a box, -1 off the bar.
* **The menus** (FIDELITY F252; DOS R:1C84:00AD..07C5). `pdos_menu_spec(menu, facts, spec)` builds a DOS menu box's
  lines from the DOS program's data read at run time (the registry's MENU_GAME, MENU_OPTIONS, MENU_ADVISORS,
  MENU_WORLD, MENU_PEDIA and the new MENU_ORDERS, MENU_REPLAY, TERRAIN_BY_COLOUR, TERRAIN_NAMES) in the listing's
  order, its place (`pdos_menu_box_xy`: GAME (16, 8), ORDERS (72, 8), ADVISORS (112, 8), WORLD (144, 8), CIVILOPEDIA
  (182, 8), GAME's Options box (24, 16)), its disabled rows (Save Game at turn 0, R:1C84:00B6..00BE; Build Irrigation
  without water next to the square; Build Fortress before advance 2Dh; SpaceShips while ([4CD8] & FE00h) is 0) and the
  Options marks, and each row's Windows command in the new `PdosRSpec.cmd[]` (`PDOS_M_GAP` for a row with no command,
  `PDOS_M_POPUP | c` for a popup item); -1 without DOS data (the caller keeps the core's items, D6f's form), 0 rows for
  ORDERS without a unit. GAME: Tax Rate, Luxuries Rate, FindCity, Options, Save Game, REVOLUTION!, a blank row, Retire,
  QUIT to DOS, and View Replay when [4CD8] & 100h. ORDERS in the order of R:1C84:023D..05B6: No Orders; for settlers
  Add to City or Found New City, Build Road or Build RailRoad (railroad when the road is there and advance 31h known),
  Build Irrigation or "Change to" a terrain unless irrigated, Build Mines or "Change to" unless mined, Clean up
  Pollution, Build Fortress; Fortify for other land units; Wait, Sentry, GoTo; Pillage on an improved square for a unit
  type below 1Ah other than 0Eh; Home City in a city; Unload for a transport with units aboard; Disband. The terrain
  names are DGROUP:0282 + 13h x [2BA6 + 2 colour] (R:1C84:034A..035D, 03C9..03DC).
* **The facts** (`PdosMenuFacts`, `pdos_menu_facts_read`), read from the core's emulated memory through the observer's
  reader (no core call, P0): the turn (g_gameTurn 12D8:42FE), the spaceship flags (12D0:00B6: View Replay & 100h,
  SpaceShips & FE00h) and, for the active unit (the core's blink probe of a unit below 80h with its Orders menu not
  grayed, g_ordersMenuGrayed 1420:2E9A: UpdateOrdersMenu grays it for unit -1 or 80h, DOS's no-box case), the unit
  (g_units 1420:62FC), its type (g_unitTypes 1420:1A38: domain, role), the square's improvements (GetTileImprovements's
  map-page pixels), its terrain's irrigation and mining effects (g_terrainByMapColor, g_terrainImprovementRules) and the
  advances 31h and 2Dh (g_civKnownAdvances). `pdos_reskin.c` adds the two facts the core keeps in its menu: the Options
  marks (items 105..112 checked) and the water (Orders item 304 enabled). The facts are read at each drop-down build:
  an ORDERS box open while the unit's square changes keeps its rows until the loop ends (DOS's box is modal too).
* **The item map** (`map_rows`): GAME 102, 103, 104, the Options popup (`PDOS_M_POPUP | 105`), 101, 113, none (the
  blank row), 114, 115, none (View Replay); Options 105..112; ORDERS by the row's key (' ' 301, b 302, r 303, i 304,
  m 305, p 306, f 307, w 308, s 309, g 310, P 311, h 312, u 313, D 318); ADVISORS 401..406, WORLD 501..506, CIVILOPEDIA
  601..606 (resources.md MENU 128). Each row gets its Windows item's rectangle in the open popup; a row whose item the
  core grays is marked `PDOS_R_CMD_GRAYED` and does nothing when chosen (**Choice D6fS-b-2**, DOS's disabled-row case
  R:1D05:0377; the DOS disabled rows are drawn in colour 3 as DOS draws them); a row whose command the popup lacks has
  no rectangle. **Choice D6fS-b-3:** View Replay is shown and chosen like the blank row: the menu ends (the core has no
  replay command).
* **The drop-downs** (`drop_build`): a popup of a DOS menu (by its first command: File GAME, its Options popup
  OPTIONS, Orders ORDERS, Advisors ADVISORS, World WORLD, Civilopedia CIVILOPEDIA) is drawn as that DOS menu box at its
  DOS place, the bar's top-left + 2 x the DOS position; ORDERS without a unit has no box (the Windows popup stays
  hidden by D6fS-a's cover). A DOS box can lie away from the hidden popup it replaces (GAME at (16, 8) and the File
  popup coincide; Options at (24, 16) lies over GAME as in DOS). **Choice D6fS-b-4:** a drop-down of Edit, City or
  Help, which the DOS bar never opens, keeps D6f's form if a profile switch finds one open.
* **H2, the DOS highlight** (FIDELITY F254; DOS R:1D05:006F..007B, 0238..0332). Each drop-down level keeps its own DOS
  row (`spec.sel`; the H2 state `g_h2` per level: the popup, the reopening state, the presented highlights' log); the
  w16 loop's own selection is never shown. A new popup opens at row 0 (R:1D05:006F), except level 1 opened by the
  mouse: while the button is down the DOS box's mouse code runs before its first highlight and the press on the bar is
  outside the box, so no row (-1); `W16qMenu.mouse` (new: the loop's mouse mode, `user_menutrack.c`, read only; 12.10)
  tells which. The Options box (level 2) opens at row 0, or at the toggled row when it opens again (ruling 4). The
  highlight then moves only by the DOS keys (the adapter's view: Up, Down, a hotkey) and by a press or a held move over
  the deepest drop-down's rows (`pdos_reskin_menu_mouse`); a hover without a button moves nothing; a title is never
  highlighted. The re-skinned MenuZ boxes (the tax and luxuries rates, the revolution question) are unchanged: their
  highlight is the core's MenuZ selection from its first key poll, the current rate at the opening as
  R:0403:3673..367C sets [2F9A].
* **M2, the mouse** (FIDELITY F255; `pdos_reskin_menu_mouse`, replacing D6fS-a's `pdos_reskin_menu_point`; DOS
  R:1D05:016A..01D9). Only the deepest drop-down is the DOS box in its loop. On it a press and a held move put the
  highlight on the row under the point (a disabled row keeps the previous one, the header and the frame none:
  `pdos_menubox_row`, the DOS idiv) and go to the w16 loop at the Windows popup's top-left (on no item, so the loop
  selects nothing); the release on an enabled row with a command goes to the centre of its Windows item (the loop
  chooses it; an Options row also asks for the reopening), on a row without a command to a corner off the menus (the
  loop ends: DOS's blank row does nothing), on a disabled row or a grayed item to the popup's top-left (nothing). A
  press or a release on a lower drop-down, or on a hidden popup outside every drop-down (D6fS-a), goes to a corner off
  the menus (the loop ends; DOS: outside the box, -1); a move there does nothing. On the bar (a press only, out of the
  menu loop; in it a press, a release and a held move): the centre of the Windows title of the DOS zone, so the w16
  loop opens that menu; a move without a button goes to a corner off the menus (no title opens under a hover); the
  ORDERS zone without an active unit and a zone without a Windows title go to a corner off the menus (I-play/dead in
  play). The w16 loop's click model is kept: a click on a title opens its menu and leaves it open (DOS: a quick click
  ends it, the release lying outside the box); the bar's y 5..7 over a headerless box stay the bar's (DOS's idiv makes
  them row 0); a held move across the bar switches the menu as Windows does (DOS: -1 outside the box).
* **The keys** (FIDELITY F253; `pdos_adapt_tab.c` I-menubar on the AMENU state, I-play; DOS R:1D05:0031..0456,
  R:0403:3ABD..3C40). In a menu box (the deepest drop-down is the adapter scene's box, `pdos_reskin_drop_top`): Up and
  Down move the DOS highlight (consumed, the view), the edges do nothing (no wrap), a row's hotkey moves it to the next
  row with that letter below it (no wrap, nothing chosen), Return and Space choose the highlighted row
  (`PDOS_A_DCHOOSE`: a left press and release at its Windows item, an Options row's reopening path after it; a row
  without a command or none highlighted: Alt down and up, the loop ends; a disabled row or a grayed item: nothing),
  Escape ends the loop (`PDOS_A_DEND`: Alt down and up; in the Options box GAME ends too, R:1C84:0168), Alt+V keeps
  D6f's sound row; Alt alone and every other Alt key (Alt+H's help flag included, FIDELITY F257) and every other key
  are consumed (the w16 loop's own keys, its bar moves, Home, End and mnemonics are Windows-only); a right press is
  consumed with its release (the rows' help has no Windows menu form). In play Alt+G, Alt+O, Alt+A, Alt+W and Alt+C
  open the GAME, ORDERS, ADVISORS, WORLD and CIVILOPEDIA menus through their Windows titles' mnemonics (I-play/game;
  D6f had Alt+G only, Alt+C and Alt+H kept the Windows City and Help menus); Alt+O without an active unit, Alt alone
  (DOS has no menu key) and a press on the dead ORDERS zone are consumed with their release (I-play/dead,
  `PDOS_AA_DEAD` 0x80000000, the orchestrator's allocation); every other Alt key is consumed (I-play/altkey; Alt+F4 is
  the host's); Alt+Q and Alt+V keep D6f's rows; a press on the bar goes to the zone's Windows title (I-menubar/title).
  The user's Alt is never passed in the DOS graphics (the w16 loop ends at a VK_MENU key down, and DefWindowProc arms
  the bar at an Alt press and opens it at the release); the produced paths (`put_alt`, `put_reopen`) post their own
  Alt down and up while the loop runs or to open a title. The rows I-menubar dead, edge, updown, hotkey, choose,
  escape, key, press and rpress (replacing D6f's key and press) and I-play dead and altkey, with game, title and key
  rewritten; `gen/dos/adapters.py` gains the two acts' texts and step counts (ADAPT_OK rows 147 steps 27; 12.8).
* **Options open after a toggle** (FIDELITY F256; ruling 4; **Choice D6fS-b-5**; DOS R:1C84:013B..0199). A choice of
  an Options row (Return, Space or a release) clicks its Windows item (the core toggles the option and the w16 loop
  ends) and then posts the path that opens the Options popup again: Alt down, the File title's letter, Alt up, the
  Options item's letter (`put_reopen`). The w16 loop posts the item's WM_COMMAND when it ends, behind the reopening
  keys already queued; the reopened loop dispatches it (a menu loop dispatches the messages that are not its keys), so
  the core toggles the option while the Options popup is opening again, and the player sees DOS's order: the toggle,
  then the box again. While the path runs, `pdos_reskin_reopen_hold` keeps both drop-downs and their highlights on the
  A canvas (the GAME box with the Options row, the Options box with the toggled row; D6fS-a's covers close normally)
  until level 2 is open again, a window shows, or 1 s of virtual time passes; the reopened Options box keeps the
  toggled row highlighted ([2F9A]). Escape then ends the whole menu (DOS: -1 ends GAME). Ruling 4's fallback (closing
  the box, a deviation) was not needed.

**As implemented (D6fSc).** Record [ext-D6fSc-record.md](ext-D6fSc-record.md) (session E4, 2026-10-17;
[verify/BRIEF-d6fsc.md](verify/BRIEF-d6fsc.md), the orchestrator's ruling of about 18:30 UTC): a rework of D5b, D6f and
D6fS-a from the user's report of 2026-10-17 about 18:28 UTC on build "d6fsb" ("QUIT to DOS popup dialog is also wide
then it should be."): the A boxes' text lines in the DOS graphics follow DOS's line breaks, wording and capitals; where
the listing pairs a box with its DOS string, the DOS game's own string is read at run time, hash-pinned; otherwise the
core's text is wrapped by a rule fitted to DOS's boxes; the core's dynamic parts fill DOS's slots; the box width
follows D6fS-a's rule. Staged on the tree after HM's landing, rebased onto D7l2's landing (`docs/dos/OFFSETS.md` by
`git merge-file -p`, its two conflicts of appended rows and notes resolved with both kept, D7l2's first; the registry's
outputs regenerated) and rechecked after the docs pass e4f; landed alone after D7l2 and e4f (the bases rechecked,
`build/d6fsc-prep/land.py` DRY_RUN_OK 9; the 8 changed files and the record copied at 21:46:57 UTC,
`build/d6fsc-prep/landed.sha256`; the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 22:25:59 UTC, `build/d6fsc-status.txt`; Release
22:30:10 UTC, user build `build/user-builds/d6fsc/`); compiled and tested headless in Checked (CheckedNoObs and Release
privately in the stage); P0 holds (`P0_OK checks 476` unchanged). FIDELITY F266..F268 (F172, F200, F201, F202 and F226
noted; Choice D5b-3's 272 pixels replaced for the MenuZ and message boxes); OPEN L29 and L30, both taken up by the
follow-up unit D6fSd ([verify/BRIEF-d6fsd.md](verify/BRIEF-d6fsd.md)). Not in the unit: the boxes' place (Choice B16
stays; OPEN L30), the header variant (the title, the portrait and "(HELP AVAILABLE)", D7g's) and a general pairing of
the KING.TXT texts (OPEN L29) (the place and the pairing since D6fSd: "As implemented (D6fSd)" below). No change to
build.cmd, test.cmd, the core, rt, w16, the Windows graphics, `pdos_adapt`
(no new PDOS bit or owner) or the observer tables.

* **DOS facts** (DOS listing, `build/dos/disasm/root.lst`; the DOS files read only, statistics only). The menu box
  R:1D05:0475 wraps nothing: it makes one line of each line feed (the line count DGROUP:658E grows at the line feed,
  R:1D05:0551..0577), takes a line starting with ' ' or '_' for an item (R:1D05:04EF..0519) and measures every byte of
  a line, the item's leading byte included (R:1D05:051C..0535; the widest line [D4C8] is taken at the line feed,
  0556..0561); a text's last bytes after its last line feed (the rate menus' final " ") count as an item but are no
  line; R:0238:001E(text, x, y) calls it with DGROUP:BA06, so a DOS box's lines are exactly its text's breaks. The quit
  question R:0403:38B6: its text DGROUP:2037, copied to BA06 (R:2045:1E62), two header lines and the rows " Keep
  Playing" and " Yes, Quit", at (100, 80) (R:0403:38C6..38D2), 1 quits; reached from GAME's QUIT (R:1C84:01B1, then
  01A9: [D4CA] = 1000h), Retire (R:1C84:01A3: [DC48] = 2 and the same 1000h) and Alt+Q (R:0403:3B22..3B27): DOS's
  Retire asks the quit question. The revolution question R:0403:3542..355F: KING.TXT's section named by DGROUP:1F6A,
  read by R:1F4D:044F (R:1F4D:01AD, then R:1F4D:0000(50h): the '^' breaks), at (64, 80); one header line and two '_'
  items, with the same words as the core's KING text. The rate menus R:0403:35CE..368B and R:0403:36B9..377F
  (SCENES-PLAY 7.20): the header DGROUP:1F9F (1FCC), then one row per rate, R:2045:1F6A's number, DGROUP:1FB6 (1FE8),
  the second number and DGROUP:1FBF (1FF6), at (100, 80); the six strings consecutive (DGROUP:1F9F..2002). The
  section texts' hand breaks (KING.TXT 60 texts, ERROR.TXT 12): R:1F4D:0000(80) breaks at '^' only in practice; the
  DGROUP box strings are broken by hand too (their 22 two-line texts are mostly fragments around a name's slot, best
  fit 139 pixels, so they do not fit a whole core text); HELP.TXT is drawn by R:1F4D:0088 at 32 characters (the
  full-screen help and the Civilization Note, O4:0000 and O4:02D3), not in a menu box.
* **The line rule** (FIDELITY F267; `present/dos/pdos_reskin.h`: `int pdos_r_spec_dos_lines(PdosRSpec *s, const
  uint8_t *text, size_t n, int fold)` adds text as header lines of s in s's font, the raw breaker `int
  pdos_r_dos_wrap(const uint8_t *text, size_t n, int font, uint8_t (*lines)[PDOS_R_LINE], int max)`,
  `PDOS_R_DOS_WRAP` 25; the signatures given to the orchestrator for D7g, which shares the rule). A hand-broken text (a
  line feed or a '^') is DOS's own and its lines are kept as they are: '^' is R:1F4D:0000's forced break, the byte
  after it becoming the line feed, so a joining space there goes; a line wider than `PDOS_R_WRAP` x 8 = 272 pixels
  (D5b's limit; no DOS box line is that wide) is still wrapped. Any other text is the core's: a '\r' ("\r\n" too) ends
  a line (the core's forced break, as in CompleteResearch's "\r   - " and ChooseCityProduction's "Military
  Advisor:\r\r"), and each line wraps by R:1F4D:0088's rule (before the word that would pass the width, at its space; a
  longer word cut where it passes) at `PDOS_R_DOS_WRAP` x 8 = 200 pixels in the box's font. On every line leading
  spaces and '_' go (R:1D05:04EF would take the line for an item) and trailing spaces go; an empty line between two
  breaks stays a line; a final break adds no line. With `fold` the core's Windows-1252 bytes are folded
  (`pdos_fold_byte`) and '\r' and '\n' are kept as breaks (D5b's '?' for the core's '\r' fixed: `pdos_fold` maps
  control bytes to '?'). For D7g's research choice: the DOS string (hand-broken, kept) or, unpaired, the core's text
  (wrapped at 200). D5b's `pdos_r_spec_header` (272 pixels, line feeds only) stays for `pdos_menu.c`'s builders, whose
  DOS menu rows are DOS's own; its wrapping is now `wrap_piece` with the width as a parameter (`put_line`; `wrap_line`
  keeps D5b's behaviour).
* **The fitted width** (Choice D6fSc-1; the record's fit, `build/d6fsc-stage/obj/fit_wrap.py`, `fit_both.py`,
  `fit_dgroup.py`, `fit_union.py`, statistics only, no DOS text written). For each width W from 96 to 319 pixels the
  count of DOS's hand breaks that a greedy wrap at W in DOS font 1 reproduces (a break between two lines of a paragraph
  is reproduced when the line fits in W and the line plus the next word does not), over all header paragraphs without
  a token read from the user's DOS files: KING.TXT 33 paragraphs (27 with a $ token left out), 70 lines, 37 breaks,
  line widths median 181, p90 220, max 254 pixels; ERROR.TXT 12 paragraphs, 31 lines, 19 breaks (the texts of the DOS
  game's questions, advisors' notes and messages, the class of the core's MenuZ and message boxes). W = 200 is the only
  width with the largest sum, 31 of 56 (KING 20, ERROR 11; 196: 28, 202: 30, 240: 16, D5b's 272: 4), within one break
  of each file's own best (KING 21 at 186, ERROR 12 at 202..212); it is 25 columns in R:1F4D:0088's units, so the rule
  is "R:1F4D:0088's rule at 25 columns". Per box class: the MenuZ boxes of dialogs 136 and 145 and the message boxes
  200 pixels; the named boxes DOS's own breaks; D5b's builders 272, unchanged.
* **The pairing** (FIDELITY F266; Choice D6fSc-2). The listing pairs five ShowTextDialog call sites with DOS strings;
  they are D6f's O1 call sites (`g_esc_site`, resolved at the reset through `obs_callsite`), and a MenuZ call made
  inside one of them takes the call site's pairing (`g_esc_site[].pair`, `MzCall.pair`, set at the MenuZ entry with
  `esc`): 1048:0278 (command 115, Ctrl+Q / File > Quit) and 11F0:401A (key 0x171, Retire) QUIT, the quit question
  R:0403:38B6's DGROUP:2037 (`BOX_QUIT`; DOS's Retire asks it); 11F0:3B85 REV, the revolution question's KING.TXT
  section (`BOX_REV_SECTION`, R:0403:3547); 11F0:3CFC TAX (`BOX_RATES` strings 0..2, R:0403:35CE..366D); 11F0:3E4A LUX
  (`BOX_RATES` strings 3..5, R:0403:36B9..3758). The DOS text (`pdos_r_pair_text`, pure, through the store;
  `split_box_text`, `digits`, `pair_rates`, `dos_breaks` for R:1F4D:0000(80)'s rule): QUIT DOS's header lines and
  rows, row k for the core's row k; REV the section's joined text through `dos_breaks`, then its header lines and '_'
  rows; TAX and LUX DOS's header (up to its line feed) and, per core row, DOS's row with the core row's first two runs of
  digits in DOS's two number slots (the numbers the core's, P0: nothing recomputed). Any refusal keeps the core's text
  by the line rule: a DOS text missing, DOS's row count not the core's, a core row without two numbers, a text too
  long. The rows are the core's items, each with DOS's text; the rate texts' trailing " " is no row (R:1D05:0475: no
  line). The pairing does not change the input map: the rows keep their item controls, so clicks, keys and Escape (O1)
  work as before. `menu_build` pairs or uses the line rule. The visible differences from D6fS-b are the quit question
  (DOS's breaks, "Quit?", "Keep Playing") and the Retire question (DOS's breaks); the revolution question and the rate
  menus already had DOS's words and one-line headers, so they look the same, their pairing pinning them and keeping the
  revolution question's 216-pixel DOS line whole where the 200-pixel fallback would break it.
* **Message boxes** (FIDELITY F268; Choice D6fSc-3). w16's message box lines are Windows's: the text's own breaks,
  placed for Windows's width, and w16's 400-pixel wrap (`user_msgbox.c` layout_text). `pdos_r_spec_msgbox` re-flows
  them (consecutive lines join with a space; an empty line, a line after an empty one, or a line starting with a space
  keeps its break, as '\r'), and the paragraphs then take the line rule (200 pixels). The Game Help box's text
  (`user_misc.c` WinHelp) is three lines of up to 33 characters instead of two Windows lines re-wrapped at 272.
* **The place** (not changed; OPEN L30). ShowTextDialog 1220:00D2 ignores its x and y and calls MenuZ with (0, 0), so
  MzCall's x and y are 0, but the core passes DOS's own place at four of the five call sites: the revolution question
  (64, 80) = R:0403:3553..355B, tax and luxuries (100, 80) = R:0403:367F..3687, 3773..377B, Retire (100, 80) = DOS's
  quit place R:0403:38C6..38CD; command 115's quit (1048:0278) passes (0, 0), and its pairing (`BOX_QUIT`) would supply
  R:0403:38B6's (100, 80). The presentation can read the ShowTextDialog probe's argument words 2 and 3 at its entry
  (`OBS_P_ShowTextDialog`, already handled in `pdos_reskin_on_probe`) and place each box at 2 x (x, y) from the DOS
  play screen's origin on the A canvas (the DOS map element's layout, D6d) in place of Choice B16's Windows dialog
  top-left; not done here (the brief); the follow-up unit D6fSd takes it.
* **Deviations and limits.** No general KING.TXT pairing: the core's KING texts (LoadKingText 11D0:01F7, TEXT
  `KNG_name`) have no observer probe, so a box cannot tell which section it shows without a new probe, a change to the
  observer tables this unit does not make (OPEN L29; D6fSd: about 60 boxes, R:1F4D:0471's tokens filled from the core's
  names); until then those boxes keep the core's words with the fitted line rule. '^' followed by a byte other than a
  space keeps that byte, where R:1F4D:0000 would replace it with the line feed (no DOS text has one). An item line's
  leading marker is measured as a space (D5b's rows are ' ' + text; DOS measures KING's '_', 4 pixels wider in font 1;
  not the widest line in the paired boxes). The unpaired boxes keep the core's Windows wording and capitals; only their
  breaks are DOS-like. The message box re-flow joins Windows's hand breaks (for example the 16-colour conversion text)
  into paragraphs; DOS has no such boxes to compare.

**As implemented (D6fSd).** Record [ext-D6fSd-record.md](ext-D6fSd-record.md) (session E4, 2026-10-17/18;
[verify/BRIEF-d6fsd.md](verify/BRIEF-d6fsd.md), the orchestrator's brief of about 22:35 UTC, and its later rulings of
2026-10-18 after D7k's landing): the follow-up of D6fSc, which closes OPEN L29 and L30. In the DOS graphics a MenuZ box
whose text is a KING text the core loaded by name shows the DOS game's KING.TXT section of the same name (DOS's breaks,
words and capitals, R:1F4D:0471's tokens filled from the core's values, DOS's '_' items as the box's rows, the core's
suffix on its own line), the leader messages of D7k's audience among them in the leader font; in play an A box whose
ShowTextDialog call carries x, y other than (0, 0) stands at DOS's place, the quit question at its pairing's (100,
80), D7f's buy question by the same rule. Staged on the tree after D6fSc's landing, rebased onto D7e's (one base
replaced, no merge), D7k's and D7f's landings (three-way merges, `obj/rebase_on.py`: with D7k three conflicts, both
sides kept, D7k's first, `menu_build`'s end, the header's extension blocks and ext_adapt's docstring; with D7f one,
ext_adapt's docstring, D7f's first; D7f's wrapper `pdos_reskin_a_place` rewritten for `a_place_of`'s D6fSd arguments,
`g_rdlg_dos` added); landed alone after D7f (the copy-in at 04:28:49 UTC; the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN
05:06:44 UTC, `build/d6fsd-status.txt`; Release 05:11:37 UTC, user build `build/user-builds/d6fsd/`);
compiled and tested headless in Checked (CheckedNoObs and Release privately in the stage); P0 holds (`P0_OK checks
476` unchanged). FIDELITY F288..F291 (F202 and F266 noted; Choice B16 reversed for the boxes with DOS places). No
change to build.cmd, test.cmd, the core, rt, w16, the Windows graphics, `pdos_adapt` (no new PDOS bit, owner or row)
or the observer tables. Not in the unit: the research choice's and the other header boxes' texts (D7g), the
Civilopedia (D7h).

* **The probes** (no observer table change). The brief asked for a read-only probe at LoadKingText; the keys exist
  since D3 (`present/obs_tables.c`; WINDOWS-INVENTORY 10.2; `ext-D3-probe-verification.md`): LoadTextResource
  11D0:00E4 (entry), LoadKingText 11D0:01F7 (entry) and SubstituteTextTokens 11D0:022D (exit), so no `obs_tables`
  regeneration, call-site row or new key is needed (D6fSc's record had assumed the probe missing). The presentation
  consumes two: LoadTextResource's entry (words 0..1 the prefix, 2..3 the name; LoadKingText calls it with "KNG", and
  ShowTextNoteDialog goes through it with HLP_ and ERR_): the prefix and the section name (`pdos_r_src_load`);
  SubstituteTextTokens's exit (no arguments; it works on g_messageText 12D8:24FA): the core's text after the
  substitution (at most 1023 bytes read) and the five values g_textSubstParams 13C8:0000 (char[5][32])
  (`pdos_r_src_tokens`); a second substitution without a load (ChooseCityProduction's, after AppendTextResource) drops
  the noted text. At MenuZ's entry (D6f's probe) a box whose text starts with the noted text takes that section, the
  rest of its text the core's suffix. Reads only: `w16q_strn`, `w16q_read`, `w16q_rd16`, `w16q_wnd`.
* **The sections** (the record's table; the core's TEXT resources KNG_ 61, ERR_ 12, HLP_ 18, MAK_ 51 against KING.TXT's
  60 names, ERROR.TXT's 12, HELP.TXT's 18 and PRODUCE.TXT's 51, by name; statistics only). Paired: the 16 KING sections

  ESCHISM, UMAX, REV; FAME and REV two items, the others none; D4 with the core's GRANARY sentence on its own line, the

### 12.10 w16 changes (decision K2)

Every change the extension's design makes outside `present/`, with its reason, phase and guard. The
list is A 17's (K2, with RV1-4 and RV2-14); it is the complete set D1 admits. Each is guarded so that
every core frame hash, checkpoint and `events.log` stays equal (G-1).

| Change | Reason | Phase | Guard |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vm.h`/`vm.c`: `CIV_OBSERVER` (default 1), the probe table, hooks in `fr_enter`/`fr_leave` | O2 probes | D3 | G-1; V5 (`CIV_OBSERVER=0`); `test_observer.c` |
| `host_sync.c`: `take_input` counts each input-taking sync (`g_input_syncs`), calls `w16_host_input_sync_fn`, passes each polled event through `present_input_filter` and posts `present_input_release`'s events inside the guard (`W16_OBS_OTHER_INPUT`; an event is posted unchanged when the guard cannot be entered); the post hook `w16_host_post_fn` in `posted()` for `coreinput.log`; `W16_TIMING_SCRIPT` (3, `w16_int.h`), which takes input this way and returns before the clock part. Under `CIV_OBSERVER 0` the ring is posted directly | I2, I4, 7.8 | D3 (D3b2) | G-1 (unreached under timing none); V5; V7; `test_input.c`; `ext_adapt.py` |
| `w16_obs.[ch]`: the `w16q_*` queries, emission macros, Checked guard | O1, O3, O4 | D3 | obs_lint; probe verification record |
| emission sites in `gdi_*`, `user_*` (A 3.3); `W16_TICK()` in queue.c, clock.c, dos.c, user_cursor.c | O1, O7 | D3 | G-1; V5 |
| Checked forbid checks across w16 | O4 | D3 | G-1 (they never fire in correct runs) |
| `user_cursor.c` `w16_SetCursor`: one `present_cursor` call inside the guard (`W16_OBS_IN_OTHER`, `W16_OBS_OTHER_CURSOR`) replaces the two direct `host_set_cursor` calls; `g_current`, the result and the `OBS_CURSOR` event are unchanged | D-3 pointer, K2, 5.7, 8.5 | D3 (D3b1) | G-1; 7.3; `test_present.c`; 7.2 |
| `gdi_pal.c` `w16_present`: `present_core_frame` (on a copy of the rectangle) inside the guard (`W16_OBS_IN_OTHER`, `W16_OBS_OTHER_PRESENT`), then `host_present` with the unchanged arguments, unconditionally | O1, P0 | D3 (D3b1) | G-1; 7.3 |
| `w16_obs.h`: the guard ids `W16_OBS_OTHER_PRESENT`, `_CURSOR`, `_COMMAND` (D3b1) and `_INPUT` (D3b2) of the kind `W16_OBS_IN_OTHER` | O4 | D3 | the Checked sentinel's fault names the kind and the id |
| `user_wnd.c`, `user_ctl.c`, `user_listbox.c`, `user_menutrack.c`, `user_cursor.c`: read-only accessors for the `w16q_*` window, control, list-box, menu-state and cursor queries (`#if CIV_OBSERVER`) | O3, A7 | D3 (D3a) | read-only, reviewed (probe verification 4, 5); G-1; `test_observer.c` |
| `rgn.c`: a region overflow inside the observer is counted (`W16qDiag.rgn_overflows`), not logged | O3 (5.3) | D3 (D3a) | G-1 (the emulation's path unchanged) |
| `host.h`: `HostEvent.frame`, `host_set_cursor_pixels` (K5) | I5, D-3 | D3 | build; 7.2 |
| `host.h`: `host_dos_read`, `host_dos_where`, `HOST_DOS_*`; back ends in `host_win32.c` (`hw_set_dos_source`, `hw_find_name` moved from `civ64_locate.c`) and `host_headless.c` (`hh_set_dos_source`, `hh_dos_inject`); `zip_find_base` in `res/zip` (moved from `civ64_locate.c`) | 5.9 read-only DOS file access | D2 | E.13.3 (Win32-headless); G-1 (no core path calls them) |
| `host.h`: `host_data_read(name, buf, cap, &size)`, `HOST_DATA_OK/MISSING/SIZE/ERROR`: the data directory's top-level files, read only and whole; back ends in `host_win32.c` (one block after `host_dos_where`) and `host_headless.c` (`hh_set_datadir`'s directory, `hh_data_inject`) | the Windows Birth's store (`present/bs`), read before the emulation (D2) | BS (BS1) | G-1 (never called on a core path); `test_pbs_assets`; `ext_bs.py assets` |
| `user_syscmd.c`: `g_outline`/`g_outline_on` toggled at each `outline()` call, set to "no band" at a loop's start and end and by `w16_syscmd_reset` (called from `w16_user_reset`, after the review); `w16_sys_track_info` for `w16q_sys_track`. The loop's behaviour is unchanged (A-3a) | D 5.2 layer clip | M (Ma) | G-1 and the base tests |
| `gdi_draw.c`/`gdi_font.c`: the DC-free rasterisers split from the DC-bound APIs: `w16_paint_brush`, `w16_rast_fill`, `w16_rast_frame`, `w16_rast_line`, `w16_text_draw`, `w16_text_box`; FillRect, FrameRect, Polygon, TextOut and DrawText resolve the DC's state and call them (LineTo and Polyline keep the static `line()`) (A-3b) | D 7.3 out-of-view rules | M (Ma) | G-1 (every core frame hash unchanged: 77 G-1 runs and the 27 P0 runs), base tests, `test_observer.c` pixel comparison |
