### 12.11 Scope of P0 (decision K1)

Invariant P0 (extension 4): for a fixed core input and virtual time, the checkpoints, the Windows
virtual screen and the base event trace do not depend on presentation. Phase G made the game screen
follow the host client during play (F105, F110), so the host size and the scaling policy are core
inputs under `--game-screen view`, by the user's direction. Decision K1 scopes P0 accordingly
(FIDELITY F113):

* **Held in every mode:** the graphics profile, the switch history (any time, any number of switches),
  the map zoom level and its changes, full screen, and the scaling filter.
* **Held with a fixed game screen** (the harness default 640x480, or `--game-screen WxH`): the host
  size and the scaling policy. The 7.3 variation V3 changes them with a fixed game screen, never
  `view`.
* **Requirement:** `present_game_screen(_follow)` reads only `CORE_W x CORE_H`, the client size and the
  policy; never the visible profile, the shown frame or the zoom. `test_present.c` checks equal answers
  under both profiles and every zoom level; V6 runs switches under `--game-screen view` (D3b1: V6w
  against V6r at `--host-size 1920x1080`, whose game screen is 960x540, so V6r's trace differs from V0's by
  design and is V6w's reference).
* **Proof** (A 1): presentation reads only through `w16q_*`; only the filter's output and the game
  screen size reach the core; the filter is off the P0 input path (P0 runs post scripted core input
  directly, `W16_TIMING_NONE` returns before the input block; the exceptions are V7 and `ext_adapt.py`,
  which run in `W16_TIMING_SCRIPT` through the arrival and filter stages and show that the stages, the
  sync hooks and the player's switches change nothing the core receives); the tick never reaches
  `w16_present`'s state; probes do not count as steps; Checked builds trap any breach; harness
  commands are not input.
* **Tests:** `ext_p0.py` (V0..V3, V5, V6 with V6w / V6r and V7 as built in D3; V4 from M),
  `ext_frames.py`, `ext_adapt.py`, `ext_g1.py` on the observer and `CIV_OBSERVER=0` builds, the Checked
  sentinels and S1's relation check; from M also `ext_mirror.py` (the core-trace equality below) and
  `ext_zoom.py` (determinism across the two runs of each combination).
* **Evidence from M** (V4 is defined in 12.9, "P0 evidence"). **V4** runs the 27 P0 runs with the zoom
  cycling at 1920x1440 and each equals V0 (`P0_OK checks 279` in Checked); on CheckedNoObs V5 and the zoom
  refusals pass (`P0_OK checks 56`). Ma's review found no P0 violation: every `w16q_*` call in `present/map`
  sits inside the guard (`pmap_on_core_present`, the probe and event listeners, `pmap_filter_ring` from
  `host_sync.c`); the entries reachable from the window procedure (`pmap_request_zoom`,
  `pmap_zoom_command`, `pmap_hit`, `pmap_layer_point`, `pmap_in_refused`, `pmap_overlay`, `pmap_restore`,
  `pmap_pending`) read only the module's and `present.c`'s state; no w16 API call, no handle, no write to
  emulated memory, no read of 1420:4EE0..4EE3; the zoom is absent from `present_game_screen(_follow)`, the
  pwin frames and the S1 check. The snapshot copies and the canvas update are `w16q_*` reads and
  `w16q_draw_on` drawing on presentation memory inside the guard (Mb1); the state is the snapshot's, copied
  at probes and never at an arbitrary poll. The mirror checks (Mb2) run in the map listener, read through
  `w16q_*` and draw only on their own memory; `ext_mirror.py` compares each of its 29 runs' frame,
  snapshot and result lines and `events.log` with ext_p0's V0 (27 runs) or uninit.py's runs (S4, S5) and
  all 29 are equal (`MIRROR_OK runs 29`). `EXT_G1_OK runs 77` on Checked and CheckedNoObs in each unit's
  final cycle. The settled check's mode 1 (the dissolve) reads and draws no random number: it takes
  its order from the call's own entries.
* **One exception by the user's direction (2026-10-15, D4b4).** P0 holds for any fixed stream of core
  input. The Birth auto-continue (12.8, "Added in D4b4") adds one input event, an Escape press and
  release, to the core's stream when the visible DOS Birth ends first, so for it P0 is checked against the
  recorded input: `ext_continue.py`'s `replay` (Windows only, given `dos`'s skip as host lines at its sync)
  equals `dos` in its 1,670 frame, snapshot and result lines, its 42 events and its posts. T4's hold after
  DOS Birth applies only while the Windows profile is visible. With the harness default (feature off)
  every existing expectation is unchanged (P0_OK 279 and 56, EXT_G1_OK on both observer variants).
* **The Birth synchronisation (2026-10-15, BS; 12.8, "Added in BS").** (a) **The frame exception (D9):** the
  5.1 relation (the Windows presented frame is `g_win`'s conversion) holds except inside the BS interval
  (`bs show` .. `bs native`), where the Windows presented frame is the view's; `g_win` itself is unchanged and
  every core output with BS on equals V0 (`ext_p0.py` V8 and V8s on birth, new, earth, custom and settlers;
  P0_OK 304 in Checked). (b) **D4b4's exception extended (D7):** the produced skip is also given with the
  Windows profile visible while the Birth synchronisation is active, so P0 is checked against the recorded
  input there too: `ext_continue.py`'s `bs_win_replay` (the Windows profile alone, given `bs_win`'s skip as
  host lines at its sync) equals `bs_win` (1,670 lines, 42 events, 2 posts). With the harness default (BS off)
  every existing expectation is unchanged (P0_OK 304 and 56, EXT_G1_OK runs 77 on both observer variants).
* **The D6 phase gate (2026-10-17, D6g; [ext-D6-acceptance.md](ext-D6-acceptance.md)).** Play and zoom in the DOS
  profile keep P0 in the gate's eight-step cycle: `ext_p0.py` V1, V2 and V4d over every scenario (V4d: 27 runs at
  1920x1440 with the zoom cycling and the DOS profile shown and switched every 997 ms, each equal to V0; the DOS layer
  drawn in 22 runs, the Windows layer in 22) with decision 8's counters (12.9, "As implemented (D6g; the D6 phase
  gate)"): `P0_OK checks 476` in Checked, 62 in CheckedNoObs (V5 with the zoom refusals); `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended
  1)` on both observer variants; `MIRROR_OK runs 29` (`present_mismatched 0`, `dos_mismatched 0`).
* **D7a and D7b (2026-10-17; 12.7 "As implemented (D7a; areas B and C)" and "(D7b; areas B and C)").** The reports in
  the DOS profile keep P0: in the landing's three-step cycle `ext_p0.py` V1 and V2 over every scenario equal V0
  (`P0_OK checks 476`; V4d's `menus` run shows the zoom layer 847 times, 869 before, since its six reports now show
  the N frame), `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` is unchanged, and the d7a-report and d7b-info DOS-form runs equal
  their Windows-form pairs (coreinput, frames, state, events, exit). The renderer reads the core only through
  `w16q_read` and the shadow engine, draws on presentation memory and the A machine's DAC, and adds no input: I-report
  and I-report/info pass or consume the user's own, so Intelligence's second wait stays (FIDELITY F210).
* **UX5b (2026-10-17; 12.7 "As implemented (UX3; changed by UX5 and UX5b)").** Birth's square start keeps P0: one
  presentation-state word set by the DOS timeline's own step (O7:1A59) and one predicate; no RNG use, no host or
  harness-program change; `ext_aspect.py`'s P0 groups (fame with fame_sw, earth_fade with earth_fade_sq) equal, and the
  landing's cycle left every core output unchanged (`EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)`).
* **D7c (2026-10-17; 12.7 "As implemented (D7c; areas B and C)").** The World reports and the historian keep P0: in the
  landing's three-step cycle `ext_p0.py` V1 and V2 equal V0 (`P0_OK checks 476`; V4d's menus, retire, conquest and
  arrival runs show the zoom layer fewer times, since the World reports and the Score now show the N frame),
  `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` is unchanged, and the d7c-world DOS-form run equals its Windows-form pair. The World
  Map reads the world state through `w16q_read` and draws on presentation memory only; no input is added (I-report
  passes or consumes the user's own).
* **DBL (2026-10-17; 12.9 "As implemented (DBL; areas B and D)").** The DOS blink clock keeps P0: the core decides
  what blinks and when (BlinkActiveUnitUntilInput's entry and end), the clock only which picture shows; it reads
  through `w16q_*` and the model's PmapSrc and writes only presentation memory; `ext_dbl.py`'s DOS and Windows unit
  runs have equal core frames and final snapshots, equal to the base's; in the landing's three-step cycle `P0_OK checks 476` and `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` are unchanged.
* **D7j (2026-10-17; 12.7 "As implemented (D7j; areas B and C)").** The DOS palace keeps P0: in the landing's
  three-step cycle `ext_p0.py` V1 and V2 equal V0 (`P0_OK checks 476`; V4d's `conquest` and `city` runs show the zoom
  layer 103 and 163 times, 112 and 153 before: the improvement's N frame, the view's return fading over 30 presented A
  frames), `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` is unchanged, and the d7j DOS-form run equals its Windows-form pair
  (coreinput 22 lines, frames 578). The renderer reads the palace state through `w16q_read` at use and the shadow
  engine, draws on presentation memory and the N machine only, and adds no input: I-palace passes or consumes the
  user's own (a left press at class W, WaitForKeyOrClick's own point, from which the core computes its choice).
* **D6fS-a (2026-10-17; 12.9 "As implemented (D6fS-a)").** The covers and the hold keep P0: they read through
  `w16q_*` (the four new queries read only, with no allocation and no side effect) and write only presentation memory;
  the neutral point beside a smaller drop-down is an ordinary press off the menus the user could make in the Windows
  graphics (the Windows menu loop's own rule ends it); `ext_adapt.py`'s f6s-tax-esc with `--tick-period-us 0` equals
  its run with the 16,667 us throttle (core input, frames and events); in the landing's three-step cycle `P0_OK checks
  476`, `MIRROR_OK runs 29` and `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` are unchanged.
* **D7d (2026-10-17; 12.7 "As implemented (D7d; areas B and C)").** The DOS city screen keeps P0: in the landing's
  three-step cycle `ext_p0.py` V1 and V2 equal V0 (`P0_OK checks 476`; V4d's `city` run shows the zoom layer 158
  times, 163 before, since its city screens show the N frame), `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` is unchanged, and the
  d7d DOS-form run equals its Windows-form pair (coreinput 36 lines, frames 662). The renderer reads the core only
  through `w16q_read`, the map module's hooks and the shadow engine, makes no core call and no core random draw, draws
  on presentation memory and the N machine only, and checks its totals against the core's (every mismatch 0 in every
  run). I-city turns the user's own keys and presses into the core's input (a key's character message, a click at the
  core's logical point, Escape's pair; Escape, Return, e and q passed with their releases) and adds none of its own.
* **D7l (2026-10-17; 12.7 "As implemented (D7l; areas B and C)").** The DOS spaceship screens keep P0: in the
  landing's three-step cycle `ext_p0.py` V1 and V2 equal V0 (`P0_OK checks 476`; V4d's `spaceship` run shows the zoom
  layer 61 times, 74 before), `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` is unchanged, and the d7l DOS-form run equals its
  Windows-form pair (coreinput 15 lines, frames 556). The stars are the core's: the renderer maps the core's 20 stars
  and never moves or makes one (no presentation random number; F238; since D7l2, where the core's stars stand, DOS's
  motion moves the drawn stars with `pdos_rand`, F259); the five cycling slots run on the screen's own
  timeline from the presentation clock; I-space turns the user's keys and buttons into a click in the spaceship window
  or on the core's Launch button and adds none of its own.
* **D7m (2026-10-17; 12.7 "As implemented (D7m; areas B and C)").** The end screens keep P0: in the landing's
  three-step cycle `ext_p0.py` V1 and V2 equal V0 (`P0_OK checks 476`; the retire, conquest and arrival runs pass
  through the end sequence), `MIRROR_OK runs 29` and `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` are unchanged, and the d7m
  DOS-form run equals its Windows-form pair (coreinput 43 lines, frames 690, the same `REPLAY.REP`). The rating's
  border is a `pdos_rand` draw from a local state seeded by `pdos_fx_rand` with the effect "border" per screen
  instance: it never reads, writes or advances the core's RNG stream (g_randSeed) and reads no clock (F248). The
  module reads the core only through `w16q_read`; the wait after a replay, Complete Replay's pace and the question's
  loop are the core's (F244..F246).
* **D6fS-b (2026-10-17; 12.9 "As implemented (D6fS-b)").** The DOS menus keep P0: they read through `w16q_*` (the new
  `W16qMenu.mouse` read only) and the observer's reader of the core's memory (`pdos_menu_facts_read`, no core call), and
  write only presentation memory; every produced path is ordinary host input a Windows player could make (a press and
  release at a Windows item or title, a menu's mnemonics with Alt, Alt down and up), and each f6b case's DOS form
  equals its replay in Windows form (coreinput, frames, events, exit); no core RNG draw. In the landing's three-step
  cycle `P0_OK checks 476`, `MIRROR_OK runs 29` and `EXT_G1_OK runs 77 (amended 1)` are unchanged.
* **HM (2026-10-17; 12.2 "System menu", since HM).** Host only: the window menu, "Switch graphics" (the chord's own
  call) and the refusal's hint change no core, w16, resource, harness or DOS-presentation code; the refusal message is

### 12.12 Windowless play (WL, WL2, WL3; session E5)

Records [ext-WL-record.md](ext-WL-record.md) (design note `verify/BRIEF-wl.md` with the orchestrator's answers 1..5 and
the user's direction of 2026-10-18 about 06:20 UTC; landed 2026-10-18, ALL_GREEN 21:53:07 UTC, `build/wl-status.txt`)
and [ext-WL2-record.md](ext-WL2-record.md) (`verify/BRIEF-e5rev.md` "Unit WL2"; landed 2026-10-19, ALL_GREEN 03:34:16
UTC, `build/wl2-status.txt`) and [ext-WL3-record.md](ext-WL3-record.md) (the user's direction of 2026-10-19 about 06:40 UTC; landed 2026-10-19, `build/wl3-status.txt`); FIDELITY F322..F325, F337..F340; D7g3's black rule F336, F324. Display's first item
"Windowless play" (12.2) switches the mode at any time in both graphics.

* **The scene** (`present/wl.c`, `wl.h`, portable C, prefix `pwl_`): read inside the observer's guard at every core
  present and tick (`pwl_read`): the three windows by their ports (g_mapWindowPort 12B0:0004, class "CIV", created by
  WdwMapOpen 1208:0AF1 at 1208:0B4E with style 0x02FF0000, which includes WS_HSCROLL and WS_VSCROLL;
  g_worldMapWindowPort 12B0:001C, WdwSmMapOpen 1210:025B; g_statusWindowPort 12B0:0034, WdwStatusOpen 1218:0386),
  their clients' visible regions (in the DOS graphics without the windows the DOS art hides), the map window's menu
  bar (`w16q_menu_bar`) and the floats: the other shown top-level windows, the menu loop's popups with their shadows
  (`w16q_menu_saved`), the move or size outline (`w16q_sys_track`) and, in the DOS graphics, the A canvas's DOS boxes
  and drop-downs (`pdos_reskin_a_rects`, `pdos_reskin_a_rects_drop`).
* **The arrangement** (`pwl_arrange`, pure; since WL2): the bar piece B (`PWL_B` 3, `PWL_PART_BAR`) is the map window's
  menu bar at canvas (0, 0); the canvas is the column's width plus the map client's (at least the bar's width), and
  beyond the bar's own width its rows repeat its last column. The map piece M is the client alone, below the bar (its
  translation as in WL, so the map layer is unchanged); the World Map W and the Status S form one column below the
  bar, on the side of their windows' mean centre, ordered by their tops. Only the clients' and the bar's visible pixels
  are copied, so the scroll bars are never shown (F323); covered parts are black (F324). WL2's start-up layout: canvas
  613x439, B at (0, 0), W at (0, 18), S at (0, 120) grown by 12 rows, M at (160, 18). (WL before WL2: M was the bar's
  rectangle down to the client's bottom, the column from canvas y 0; canvas 629x439.)
* **S grown** (WL2, `s_cut`, `s_ext`): when S is the column's last piece and the canvas is taller than the column,
  `s_ext` rows are inserted at frame row `s_cut` (`PWL_FOOT` 12 rows above the client's bottom), filled with the band
  of `PWL_BAND` 32 rows above the cut repeated; the rows below the cut and the floats anchored at S below it move down;
  a float over the band moves the cut up in steps of 2 (at most to S's middle). F338.
* **The whole client** (WL3, F339): `pwl_screen_of` (pure) / `pwl_screen_for_view` give the game screen for a view
  of vw x vh logical pixels: each side grown by the frame's size minus the canvas's, on a side only while the
  canvas follows the screen one to one there (the map window's right, or bottom, edge on the screen's edge, so it
  follows by its size, F110; at the bottom M the tallest piece); `present_game_screen_follow` (`follow_size`)
  applies it while the option is on, so the map window grows by the chrome, the canvas is the view's size and is
  laid out without a border (a remainder below one scale step only). The game learns the new screen at its
  PeekMessage calls as for the player's own window enlarging; `present_game_screen` (the first screen, F105) and
  the headless host (fixed `--game-screen`) are unchanged. Default layout at a 1280x960 view: screen 667x521,
  canvas 640x480 at 2x, origin (0, 0).
* **Floats**: anchored by their centre (W, S, else M; the Windows popups and the DOS drop-downs at B since WL2,
  `PwlScene.flbar`), moved by the anchor's translation and clamped inside the canvas; a float larger than the canvas
  pauses windowless play. A DOS re-skin box is not a window, so no piece's visible region excludes it: since D7g3 a
  piece leaves a DOS float's rectangle black where the float moves otherwise than the piece (`PwlScene.flbox`,
  `PwlFloat.box`); the float alone shows the box.
* **The canvas and points**: `pwl_frame` / `pwl_peek` make the canvas from the visible frame, translating the frame's
  damage piece by piece (`piece_cv`, `cv_to_frame`, `dmg_piece`, `put_part` through the bar fill and S's inserted
  rows); `pwl_point` is the inverse map (topmost float, else piece; a black part or a gap returns 0 and a press there
  is not delivered, F100; a latched press keeps its part for drags); `pwl_check` checks the canvas against its
  definition (every canvas pixel the arranged frame pixel or black).
* **The seams**: `present.c` passes the visible frame through `pwl_frame`/`pwl_peek` while windowless play shows,
  re-lays out when its showing changes, maps `present_view_point` through `pwl_point`, takes the pointer factors from
  the canvas's layout (`present_set_windowless`, `present_windowless`, `present_windowless_showing`; `wl_due()` at the
  tick for a scene change or base-frame damage between core presents, such as MZW's World Map rectangle); `arrive.c`'s
  press latch keeps the part; the map layer (`pmap_layer.c`, `pmap_input.c`, `pmap_zoom.c`, `pmap_dos.c`) follows M (the
  canvas's layout, M's translation, the floats as cuts, the wheel anchor through the view point); `host_win32.c` the
  item, its mark, `hw_set_windowless`, `hw_set_windowless_hook`; `win64` `--windowless 0|1` and the key `windowless`.
* **Harness**: `--windowless 0|1` and the script command `windowless on|off`; presented.log's ` wl CWxCH` and
  ` wl MISMATCH` marks, the `wl shows S pauses P arranges A mismatches M` line, and the fact lines `wl arrange` (since
  WL2 with `barpiece on l t r b dx dy sext cut ext`), `wl bar`, `wl popup`, `wl windows`, `wl view` that `ext_wl.py`
  builds its input pairs from. Tests `test_wl` (78 checks since WL2), `ext_wl.py` (section `ext_wl`; `WL_PAIRS_OK
  checks 15`), `ext_p0.py` V9, V9d, V9t.

### 12.13 4:3 for the DOS gameplay (A43; session E5)

Record [ext-A43-record.md](ext-A43-record.md) (the user's request in `verify/BRIEF-extras.md`; landed 2026-10-18,
ALL_GREEN 22:34:29 UTC, `build/a43-status.txt`); FIDELITY F326..F328.

* **The aspect is a bit set** (`present/scale.h`): `PRESENT_ASPECT_4_3` (1, the intro item, UX3's meaning: a 320x200
  frame gets the pixel aspect 6/5 outside play) and `PRESENT_ASPECT_PLAY` (2, the gameplay item: a frame of any size
  gets 6/5). The DOS gameplay's A frame is DOS art at 2x on the Windows layout, so its rows stretched by 6/5 give every
  DOS pixel the DOS screen's 4:3 shape (inferred: the DOS game draws 320x200 for a 4:3 display).
* **Layout** (`present_layout_compute`): the PLAY bit sets the pixel aspect 6/5 for any frame; under "Integer scaling"
  the whole pairs are searched as for UX3, and a frame with no whole 6/5 pair is laid out by fit with the current filter
  and recorded as fit (F326). Example: the 640x480 A frame in 1920x1080 is 1200x1080 at (360, 0).
* **Which frames** (`present.c` `present_layout_aspect`, `PresentProfile.in_play`): while the visible profile is in
  play (the DOS profile: `pdos_seq`'s core scene `PDOS_CS_PLAY`, from the setup's end or a loaded game's start to the
  run's end) the gameplay item decides for every frame, the in-play 320x200 scenes included (F327); otherwise UX3's
  rule with the intro item. The Windows profile has no `in_play` (NULL). A change re-lays out at the next tick.
* **What follows the shape**: the scaler, the client-to-frame mapping of the pointer and clicks, the DOS pointer's
  factors, the map zoom layer (`pmap_core_layout` lays the core's frame out with `present_layout_aspect`) and the
  windowless canvas. **What does not**: the game screen's size (`present_game_screen`, `_follow`) and the Windows
  frame's layout for the pointer factors use the intro bit only (P0; F328).
* **civ64**: `civ64_cfg_aspect_play` / `civ64_cfg_set_aspect_play` (key `aspect_play_4_3`), `civ64_start_aspect`;
  `--aspect gameplay` and `--aspect both` (the harness takes the same values). Tests: `test_present`
  `profiles_in_play` (974 checks), `w32_test_host_win32`.

### 12.14 CRT scanlines (CRT, CRT2; session E5)

Records [ext-CRT-record.md](ext-CRT-record.md) (the user's request in `verify/BRIEF-extras.md`; landed 2026-10-18,
ALL_GREEN 23:29:44 UTC, `build/crt-status.txt`) and [ext-CRT2-record.md](ext-CRT2-record.md) (the user's report of
2026-10-18 about 23:50 UTC; landed 2026-10-19, ALL_GREEN 01:05:46 UTC, `build/crt2-status.txt`); FIDELITY F329..F331,
F335.

* **The source**: DOSBox Pure Unleashed's `fragment_shader_crt_src` (main.cpp, (C) Bernhard Schelling,
  GPL-2.0-or-later, read read-only) with the user's settings, which give ShadowMask 1 (no mask, tone mask 1),
  ScanlineThinness 0.65 and HorizontalBlur -7. civ64 copies no code: `present/crt.c` reimplements the arithmetic in
  portable C, with the attribution and formulas in `present/crt.h`.
* **The place** (`present.c`): a post-process of the scaled client image. With the option on, the scaler and the map
  layer draw into a clean client buffer; `show()` filters the written rectangle into the back end's buffer and shows
  that; core presents filter only their damage rectangles, the map zoom layer is filtered with the picture, the black
  border is copied.
* **The grid and aspect**: the visible frame's pixels; for the DOS A frame its DOS pixels (one scanline per DOS row),
  for the Windows graphics one per Windows pixel row (F331); output pixels map to source rows through the laid-out
  image, so both 4:3 items give 4:3 scanlines; no overscan border.
* **The arithmetic since CRT2** (F335): no resampling. Each image pixel keeps its clean pixel; per image row one scan
  weight w = own + neighbour of the shader (own = scan(a T), neighbour = scan((1 - a) T), a the row's distance from
  its own source row's centre), which is what the shader gives a uniform area; then the shader's tonal curve. A table
  `fac[level][brightest channel]` (256 x 256, 16.16, made once) gives w f(p) / p; a pixel is 3 decode reads, 1 factor
  read, 3 multiplies and 3 encode reads, and a run of equal pixels reuses the result. `present_crt_expand` returns the
  rectangle itself (no reach). A whole 3840x2160 client takes about 8 to 9 ms (CRT's resampling, two rows x four
  columns per pixel: about 400 ms, the user's slowdown; its horizontal taps and row blend were the smoothing).
* **Menu and settings**: Display's "&CRT scanlines" (12.2); `hw_set_crt` for the start state; civ64 `--crt 0|1`, else
  the key `crt_scanlines` ("1" on), else off. Tests: `test_crt` (21 checks since CRT2: the scan levels, white and
  black, sharpness, no reach, a golden SHA-256, a timing bound of 120 ms for a whole 3840x2160 client, the
  presentation's option checks), `w32_test_host_win32`.

### 12.15 Alternative music and sound effects (AMS; session E5)

Record [ext-AMS-record.md](ext-AMS-record.md) (the user's request in `verify/BRIEF-extras.md`; landed 2026-10-19,
ALL_GREEN 00:12:36 UTC, `build/ams-status.txt`); FIDELITY F332..F334.

* **Where the sounds are played**: the game plays every sound through MMSYSTEM sndPlaySound
  (`platform/w16/user_misc.c` `w16_sndPlaySound`; asynchronously at 1150:00BE, stopped with a NULL name at
  1150:012D); the name resolves through the virtual DOS like a file and `host_play_sound(relpath, async)` plays the
  data directory's file with PlaySoundW. The replacement sits there (`platform/win32/host_win32.c`): the core's call,
  its return value and every state it sees stay as they are (P0).
* **Lookup** (`win64/civ64_locate.c`, after the DOS steps): 1 `--music-sfx <dir|zip|none>`; 2 `MusicSfx.zip` next to
  civ64.exe; 3 a `MusicSfx` subfolder there (names compared ignoring case); 4 civ64.cfg's `music_data` (a folder or a
  ZIP); 5 the question, unless `music_ask=never`: Yes opens the file dialog for a WAV file (its folder is the source)
  or a ZIP; No asks whether to ask again (No writes `music_ask=never`), as the DOS question does. A chosen source is
  remembered (`music_data`) and the item checked again (`alt_sounds` removed).
* **Valid**: at least one of the 24 names is a valid WAV file (RIFF WAVE, a "fmt " chunk of at least 16 bytes with a
  format tag, channels and a rate, a non-empty "data" chunk: `civ64_wav_ok`); in a folder the top level (any case), in
  a ZIP any one folder (`zip_find_base`). An invalid file of a name is ignored (the game's own plays). A source without
  a valid file: `--music-sfx` stops civ64 with a message naming the 24 files, steps 2..4 skip it, a choice is refused
  and asked again.
* **In memory**: the valid files are read once at the start (a ZIP in memory, never extracted; nothing written to
  `%LOCALAPPDATA%`) and kept for the run (F333).
* **Playback** (`host_play_sound`): the last path component of the game's name matched to the 24 names ignoring ASCII
  case (F334); with a replacement and the item checked, PlaySoundW plays its bytes (SND_MEMORY, the game's async/sync
  flag, SND_NODEFAULT); otherwise the game's file. The stop (NULL name) stops either (F332).
* **Menu**: the top-level "A&lternative music/soundfx" (12.2); grayed without a valid source; checked at the start when
  a source is valid unless `alt_sounds` is "0". Tests: `w32_test_locate` `test_ams` (47 checks), `w32_test_host_win32`
  (495 checks, 15 top-level items), on synthetic 48-byte WAV files; the question flow is not exercised headless (user
  check AMS-2).
